- **Consequence:** **The merged F7-2 preflight is wrong and must be fixed before US-071/072.** `run_preflight()` in `scripts/09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh` reads `vm()` / `absolutePrestate()` on the implementation, so post-wipe it will see zeros and refuse to start against a perfectly healthy deployment; the documented bypass `CHALLENGER_SKIP_PREFLIGHT=1` would become the routine path, which is worse than having no preflight. That defect traces to the F7-2 brief, which was written from D-0052 — not to the workers, who built what was specified. D-0054 is unaffected: `permissioned` remains the working start path, `cannon` / `cannon-kona` still need a pre-image server binary. **D-0052 Finding 1 still stands** — `op-program` is gone from the pinned monorepo — but it is no longer on the critical path, since the required prestate is Cannon/MIPS and its commitment is known. What remains genuinely open is obtaining the *artifact* whose commitment is `0x038512e0…` (a local file for `--cannon-prestate`, or a base URL for `--cannon-prestates-url`); that is a lookup, not a Rust build, and `cargo` / `rustc` are no longer blockers for it. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`.

### D-0056 — The on-chain absolute prestate is a **cannon32** artifact; the deployed VM is **MIPS64 `stateVersion() = 8`**. A redeploy repeats the mismatch unless the intent overrides it
- **Context:** D-0055 established that the absolute prestate commitment is knowable before the wipe — `0x038512e02c4c3f7bdaec27d00edf55b7155e0905301e1a88083e4e0a6764d54c` — and closed with "obtaining the artifact is a lookup, not a build." The lookup was done 2026-08-19 during review of F7-3 (#93). It did not find an artifact; it found a contradiction in the deployment that is about to be replaced.
- **Finding 1 — the commitment is a cannon32 prestate.** The hash appears in the pinned tree's own copy of the superchain registry, `superchain-registry/validation/standard/standard-prestates.toml:296-302`, as `type = "cannon32"` under prestate releases `1.3.1` and `1.3.1-rc.2`. `cannon32` entries run from `0.0.1` to `1.6.0-rc.2` and stop; `cannon64` entries start at `1.4.0` and run to `1.9.0` (`latest_stable`). The commitment therefore predates 64-bit Cannon entirely. Note that the leading `0x03` is the VM status byte (`VMStatusUnfinished`, `cannon/mipsevm/state.go:16`) and carries no version information — every registry hash starts `0x03`.
- **Finding 2 — the deployed VM cannot execute a cannon32 state.** `gameArgs(1)` names the VM as `0xacc005dcd857b401e4732e6f7837135a22825cfa`. Read live over public Sepolia RPC: `version()` = `"1.10.1"`, `stateVersion()` = **8**. The only MIPS contract in the pinned contracts tree is `MIPS64.sol`, whose constructor **reverts unless `_stateVersion == 8`** (`packages/contracts-bedrock/src/cannon/MIPS64.sol:37`). `stateVersion` 8 is `VersionMultiThreaded64_v5` (`cannon/mipsevm/versions/version.go:29`). The superchain registry lists this exact address as the standard Sepolia `mips` for `op-contracts/v7.0.0-rc.2` through `-rc.4`.
- **Finding 3 — the pinned `cannon` matches the VM, not the prestate.** `cannon --version` reports `multicannon version untagged-da197e45-1782514747`, and `cannon witness --help` lists exactly one embedded VM: `filename: cannon-8   version: multithreaded64-5 (8)`. It reads the state version from the input file's first byte — a bogus input fails `unknown version: 110`, and a `.json` input looks for `embeds/cannon-0` and reports `file does not exist`. So the local binary can execute and hash **only** stateVersion-8 states. It matches the deployed MIPS64 exactly and cannot load a cannon32 (`VersionSingleThreaded`, version 0) artifact at all.
- **Finding 4 — op-deployer 0.7.1 will repeat it, and there is an override.** `standard.DisputeAbsolutePrestate` (`op-deployer/pkg/deployer/standard/standard.go:47`) is hardcoded to the same `0x038512e0…` cannon32 value and is used as the default in `pipeline/opchain.go:119`. It is merged through `jsonutil.MergeJSON` under `intent.GlobalDeployOverrides`, and the per-chain intent field is `faultGameAbsolutePrestate` (`state/chain_intent.go:38`). ForteL2's `deployments/sepolia/.deployer/intent.toml` sets **no** prestate field, so the 2026-07-22 deploy took the stale default — and a redeploy with the same pinned op-deployer would take it again. **And `intent.toml` is not where the fix lives.** `scripts/02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh:98-100` rewrites the intent wholesale from the environment on every run — its own comment says "stale intent.toml must not win over .env.sepolia" — and the `[globalDeployOverrides]` block it emits carries the six clock knobs and no prestate field. A hand-edited `intent.toml` is overwritten before `op-deployer apply` ever reads it, so the override has to be written by the script.
- **Decision:** Record this as a contradiction in the deployment, **not** as a proven blocker — the D-0052 lesson is that an inference from a partial read is not a measurement. What is measured: the commitment's registry provenance, the VM's `stateVersion()`, the local binary's single embedded VM, and the absence of an intent override. What is **not** measured: whether a game on the current deployment actually fails when stepped, because no game has ever been played on it. **This must be resolved before US-071/072**, since the wipe is the only cheap moment to change what the new factory commits to. The candidate fix is to set `faultGameAbsolutePrestate` in the intent's `GlobalDeployOverrides` to a stateVersion-8 prestate we actually hold — the registry's current `cannon64` entry at `1.9.0` is `0x033c000916b4a88cfffeceddd6cf0f4be3897a89195941e5a7c3f8209b4dbb6e`, **unverified**: nobody has confirmed that `cannon64` at 1.9.0 means stateVersion 8, and no artifact for it is in hand.
- **Consequence:** D-0055's "what remains genuinely open is obtaining the artifact" is narrowed and re-pointed. Obtaining an artifact for `0x038512e0…` is now the **wrong** goal — it would be a cannon32 file the pinned `cannon` cannot load. The right goals, in order: (1) confirm which prestate release/type corresponds to stateVersion 8; (2) obtain that artifact and verify its `cannon witness --input` hash; (3) set `faultGameAbsolutePrestate` in the intent before the redeploy. Until (1) and (2) are answered, **US-071/072 must not run** — a wipe that repeats the stale default spends a full redeploy to arrive back here. Nothing in this entry authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`; it argues the opposite. D-0054 (`permissioned` is the working start path) and D-0055 (preflight reads `gameArgs`) are both unaffected. Found by the Codex bot on #94 and confirmed by the planner: the binding operator sequence in `tasks/prd-phase-7-fault-proofs.md` did not encode any of this — step 3 ran `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY=1` on the precondition "file knobs already set", meaning the six clock values only, and the PRD's out-of-scope list still read "Cannon / Kona prestates pinning for production". Following the documented roadmap would therefore have spent the wipe and landed back here, which is exactly the PRD's own "a forgotten parameter is a second network-wide wipe" rule. The PRD and README are amended in the same change: a **Prestate** constraint row, a blocking sequence step (**0b** in the PRD, **1b** in the README), a US-071 acceptance criterion, and the out-of-scope line narrowed to prestate *build reproducibility*. The gate sits **before the announcement**, not merely before the redeploy: it is artifact research plus a reviewed code change, and D-0049's rule is that notice goes out only once all Phase 7 coding/config work is complete. Gating at the redeploy instead would have opened the ≥24h notice window and stopped the writers before this work started, holding chain 852 down for its full unbounded duration — caught by the Codex bot on #94. The script change itself is a separate worker task (**F7-6**), not an operator edit.

### D-0057 — Prestate-commitment verification in the preflight is **three-state**: mismatch refuses, "cannot compute" warns
- **Context:** E-F7-3-1 (answered on #93, verified by the planner) asked whether anything in the tree can compute a local prestate file's commitment. It can: `cannon witness --input <path>` emits `witnessHash`, from `EncodeWitness` / `stateHashFromWitness`. `op-challenger` has no equivalent CLI — it compares only at game time via `PrestateProvider.AbsolutePreStateCommitment` — and `--cannon-prestates-url`'s `<commitment>.bin.gz` naming is a convention, not a content hash. So a local check is buildable, and it is the only thing that can tell a correct artifact from a plausible one.
- **The constraint that shapes it.** `op-challenger` accepts `.bin.gz`, `.json.gz` and `.json` prestates (`op-challenger/game/fault/trace/prestates/multi.go:23`), but `cannon witness` parses only a binary state whose version byte matches an embedded VM — today only stateVersion 8 (D-0056 Finding 3). A legitimate, working `.json` artifact is therefore unhashable by the pinned binary. "Cannot compute" is an ordinary configuration, not an edge case.
- **Decision (operator, 2026-08-19):** when the preflight can compare, it compares, and the three outcomes are distinct. **Match** → proceed. **Mismatch** → `exit 1`; the challenger provably cannot step that game and gas must not be spent on it. **Cannot compute** — no `cannon` binary, unparseable state version, a `.json` artifact, or `-r` URL mode where there is no local file — → a loud `WARN` that names the specific reason, then proceed. A check that refuses for a reason unrelated to the property it is testing is precisely the D-0055 defect in a new place, and must not be reintroduced.
- **Consequence:** This is **not** F7-4. It becomes a later task (F7-5) that cannot be written until D-0056 (1) and (2) are answered — the brief would otherwise have to guess whether "cannot compute" is the common path or the rare one, and that changes what the task is. F7-4 (`tasks/worker-prompts/F7-4-preflight-assertion-hardening.md`) covers only the assertion and citation cleanups found reviewing #93. The check is scoped to the `-f` (local file) form of `CHALLENGER_PRESTATE`; `CHALLENGER_PRESTATES_URL` has no local file to hash and takes the WARN path by construction.

### D-0058 — F7-4 merged; the preflight assertion set is mutation-proven. **E-F7-4-1 resolved: do not infer "old factory" from a failed `gameArgs` call**, however tempting the signal
- **Context:** F7-4 (#95, `2ff28ac`, merged `3978614`) hardened the F7-3 assertions and named the failed-`gameArgs` state. Reviewed independently in a scratch clone; the worker raised no escalation but explained the judgement, and reviewing it surfaced one argument worth recording before somebody re-opens it as an improvement.
- **What the review actually established.** `test-helpers.sh` 108 → **110 PASS**, exactly one deletion (the weak `grep -q 'CHALLENGER_SKIP_PREFLIGHT'`, replaced by a structural `awk`). Seven mutations were run against the merged script and **all seven went red**: delete the bypass block, relax `< 104` to `< 0`, revert the WARN citation, revert the failed-call guard, **invert the bypass branch**, **drop `>&2` from the WARN**, and **make the failed call fall back to the implementation getters instead of exiting**. The first two had been measured **green** during the #93 review, before F7-4 existed — so the two blind spots F7-4 was created to close are demonstrably closed, against a prior measurement rather than the worker's report. `run_preflight` was also driven through a call-logging `cast` stub: a valid blob never calls the impl getters, an empty `0x` does, a *failed* call does neither and exits 1, and a deliberate `false` after the guarded call still aborts — so `if ! args="$(…)"` captures the status without disarming `set -e`. All five unmapped trace types (`alphabet`, `fast`, `zk`, `cannon-kona`, `super-cannon-kona`) return 0 with **zero** `cast` calls.
- **The near-miss.** `run_preflight` calls `gameImpls(uint32)` immediately before `gameArgs(uint32)`. If the first succeeds and the second fails, that is *evidence* the RPC was alive and the function is simply absent — which looks like a clean way to tell "this factory predates `gameArgs`" from "the RPC blipped," and therefore to fall back to the implementation getters in the first case instead of refusing both.
- **Decision:** **Do not build it.** The signal is a heuristic, not a discriminator: a transport failure between two consecutive calls is entirely possible, and the inference runs from an indirect observation to a claim about the deployment's contract layout. That is the exact shape of reasoning that produced D-0052's withdrawn conclusion — a real measurement, a plausible inference, a wrong answer, and a gate that would have refused a healthy chain. The cost asymmetry settles it: guessing "old factory" wrongly means reading zeros off an implementation and either refusing a good deployment or, worse, being talked into trusting the fallback. Refusing both states costs one operator message. `run_preflight` therefore exits 1 on any failed `gameArgs` call, naming both candidate causes and declining to choose between them. **E-F7-4-1 is closed, not deferred.**
- **Consequence:** The `gameImpls`-succeeded-first argument is recorded so it is not re-proposed as an optimisation. Two known brittlenesses in the new assertions, both **fail-safe** (they produce false alarms, never false passes) and neither worth fixing today: the bypass check keys on top-level `^else$` / `^fi$`, so cosmetically indenting that block turns the harness red, and the bound check matches the literal `< 104`, so the semantically identical `<= 103` also turns it red. Anyone reformatting `run_preflight` should expect a confusing failure and read this entry. Nothing here touches US-071/072, which remain blocked on **D-0056 (1)** — identifying which prestate release/type corresponds to stateVersion 8. `permissioned` remains the working start path (D-0054); the preflight reads `gameArgs` (D-0055); the prestate-commitment check is still F7-5 and still unwritten (D-0057).

### D-0059 — The prestate must be **stateVersion 8** for either candidate VM; **D-0055 was wrong about Kona and about Rust**. The artifact will be built in CI, not downloaded
- **Context:** D-0056 left three things open: (1) which prestate release/type is stateVersion 8, (2) obtain that artifact, (3) pin it in the intent. Worked (1) and (2) on 2026-08-20. (1) is answered; (2) turned into a build problem with a decided route.
- **Finding 1 — stateVersion 8, and it survives the redeploy.** Read live over public Sepolia RPC: the VM named by `gameArgs(1)`, `0xacc005dcd857b401e4732e6f7837135a22825cfa`, is `MIPS64` `version()` = `1.10.1`, `stateVersion()` = **8**. That contract is the superchain registry's standard `mips` for `op-contracts/v7.0.0-rc.2` … `-rc.4`. But op-deployer 0.7.1's `CurrentTag` is `op-contracts/v6.0.0-rc.2` (`standard/standard.go:43-44`), whose standard `mips` is a **different contract**, `0x6463dee3828677f6270d83d45408044fc5edb908` at `version()` = `1.9.0`. Read that one too: `stateVersion()` = **8** as well. So a redeploy will wire a different MIPS implementation than the one now live, and **the prestate requirement is unchanged either way**. `stateVersion` 8 is `VersionMultiThreaded64_v5`, the newest in `cannon/mipsevm/versions/version.go`, and the only VM the pinned multicannon embeds.
- **Finding 2 — D-0055's closing sentence is wrong in two ways, and this one is mine.** D-0055 concluded "the artifact needed is a **Cannon/MIPS** prestate, not a Kona one" and that "the missing `cargo`/`rustc` on the mini was never on the critical path." MIPS64 is the **VM**; the fault-proof **program** it executes may be Go `op-program` *or* Rust **Kona** compiled to MIPS64 — the registry carries `cannon32`, `cannon64`, `interop`, `cannon64-kona` and `cannon64-kona-interop` as distinct types, and `cannon64-kona` is a Kona program on the Cannon VM. Since **`op-program` is absent from the pinned monorepo** (D-0052 Finding 1), Kona is the *only* program we can build: in the pinned tree `just reproducible-prestate` resolves to `build-kona-reproducible-prestate`. **Rust is therefore squarely on the critical path**, which D-0055 explicitly denied.
- **Finding 3 — the artifact is built, not downloaded, and neither build path works on the mini.** Optimism's own documentation directs operators to run the reproducible build and upload the result to their own host; it publishes no canonical bucket, and the guessed `oplabs-network-data` paths 404. In the pinned tree there are two routes. `just reproducible-prestate` → `build-kona-reproducible-prestate-variant`, which is a **`docker build --platform linux/amd64`** against `rust/kona/docker/fpvm-prestates/cannon-repro.dockerfile`. The native route `just build-kona-prestate` needs Rust **nightly** plus a MIPS64 cross-compiler (`mips64-linux-gnuabi64-gcc`, `CARGO_BUILD_TARGET` = `rust/kona/docker/cannon/mips64-unknown-none.json`) and then runs `cannon load-elf`. Measured on the mini: `cargo`, `rustc` and `docker` are all **absent**; `just` 1.56.0 is present. The PRD Host constraint forbids Docker on this workstation, and a Linux MIPS64 cross-toolchain on macOS ARM is worse than the constraint it would dodge.
- **Decision (operator, 2026-08-20):** **Build the prestate in GitHub Actions and download the artifact.** A manually-triggered workflow in this repo checks out `ethereum-optimism/optimism` at the pinned `da197e45ed44b9fca258b3b0d0709e8dfca1c7cd`, runs the reproducible Docker build on `ubuntu-latest` (native amd64, no emulation), self-checks the output with the pinned `cannon witness`, and uploads `prestate.bin.gz`, its hash-named copy and `prestate-proof.json`. The operator downloads it and re-verifies locally before anything trusts it. This keeps the mini free of Docker rather than amending the Host constraint, and leaves an auditable build log. Worker prompt: `tasks/worker-prompts/F7-7-prestate-build-workflow.md`. **The workflow must not run on push** — manual `workflow_dispatch` only.
- **Consequence:** D-0056 (1) is **answered**: the target is a **stateVersion-8** Cannon-VM prestate, and `cannon witness --input` is a perfect discriminator because the pinned binary parses nothing else. D-0056 (2) is now **F7-7**, and (3) remains **F7-6**. Note the ordering that follows: because the redeploy's `faultGameAbsolutePrestate` is ours to choose, we do **not** need an artifact matching a predetermined hash — we build one, compute its commitment locally, and pin *that*. The hash will not necessarily equal the registry's `cannon64-kona` entry for any release; the workflow should **report** whether it matches and must **not** gate on it. What that does not remove: the program still has to be the right one for chain 852's fork configuration — and **pinning the monorepo commit does not achieve that**, which was an error in the first draft of this entry (found by Cursor Bugbot on #97). Chain 852 is not in `kona_registry::ROLLUP_CONFIGS`, so `kona-client` falls back to loading the rollup config from the preimage oracle (`rust/kona/crates/proof/proof/src/boot.rs:228`), which the source itself warns is "insecure in production without additional validation". It still *runs*, so this is a soundness gap rather than a functional failure — but the prestate does not commit to 852's config. The mechanism that would fix it exists — `KONA_CUSTOM_CONFIGS_DIR`, threaded through to the Dockerfile as a named build context by `build-kona-reproducible-prestate-variant` (`rust/justfile:526-535`) — **but using it runs into a circularity**, recorded as **E-D0059-1**: `RollupConfig` includes `genesis.l1`, `genesis.l2` and `genesis.l2_time` (`deployments/sepolia/.deployer/rollup.json`), all of which change on a redeploy. A prestate baked with today's config would be wrong for the post-wipe chain, and the post-wipe config does not exist until after an apply that must already commit to a prestate hash. Two escapes exist and the choice is not made here: build **chain-agnostic** and accept the documented oracle fallback, or **split the deploy** — redeploy first, then register the type-8 game in a second `op-deployer apply` once the real `rollup.json` exists. The second is viable because `dangerousAdditionalDisputeGames` is guarded by `shouldDeployAdditionalDisputeGames` comparing intent against state (`op-deployer/pkg/deployer/pipeline/dispute_games.go:34,260`), so a later apply adds the game without redeploying what is already there — and it can happen *after* the network is healthy, outside the outage window. Note the distinction this exposed: `cannon witness` proves state version and commitment integrity and proves **nothing** about which chain the program can prove. Two different properties; the first must not stand in for the second. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`; the step 0b gate (D-0056) is unchanged and now has a route. Separately: **`L1_BEACON_URL` is resolved** — the existing QuickNode Sepolia endpoint already serves the beacon REST API (`eth/v1/config/spec` → `CONFIG_NAME: "sepolia"`, `node/syncing` → `is_syncing: false`), no add-on and no second endpoint. It takes the *same* value as `L1_RPC_URL`; the trailing slash is safe because `op-service/client/http.go` joins with `url.URL.JoinPath`, which cleans duplicate separators.

### D-0060 — **Supersedes D-0054's "`permissioned` is the working start path".** op-challenger binds program to game type at compile time, and the pinned tree can only build Kona — Phase 7 moves to **game type 8 / `cannon-kona`**
- **Context:** Codex flagged on #97 that F7-7 would build a Kona prestate while the documented path is game type 1. Verifying that turned up something larger. 2026-08-20.
- **Finding — the binding is compile-time, not configuration.** `op-challenger/game/fault/register.go` wires an executor per game type with no knob between them: type **0** `cannon` → `vm.NewOpProgramServerExecutor` (line 61); type **1** `permissioned` → `vm.NewOpProgramServerExecutor` (line 82); type **8** `cannon-kona` → `vm.NewKonaExecutor` (line 68); type **9** `super-cannon-kona` → `vm.NewKonaSuperExecutor`. Numbers from `op-challenger/game/types/game_type.go:14-23`. **`op-program` is absent from the pinned monorepo** (D-0052 Finding 1), so types 0 and 1 cannot generate a trace *whatever prestate they are given*. Our factory registers type 1.
- **What this supersedes.** D-0054 recorded `permissioned` as "the working Cannon-family start path", with the explicit caveat that passing `CheckRequired` means the daemon reaches monitoring, **not** that it can generate a trace. That caveat is now closed, and closed against us: `permissioned` starts and can never play. D-0056 and D-0059 were both built on the unclosed caveat. D-0054's other findings — the fail-open behaviour, the `--cannon-server` requirement, the `super-cannon-kona` refusal — are unaffected.
- **Decision (operator, 2026-08-20):** **pivot Phase 7 to game type 8 end-to-end.** The redeploy registers an **additional dispute game at type 8** carrying a **Kona** `faultGameAbsolutePrestate`, with `MakeRespected` so it becomes the respected game type; the challenger runs `CHALLENGER_TRACE_TYPE=cannon-kona` with `--cannon-kona-prestate` and `--cannon-kona-server`. Feasibility was checked before deciding, not after: op-deployer's `VMType` describes the MIPS **state version** (`VMTypeCannon` → `versions.GetCurrentVersion()`, i.e. 8) and not the program, and `AdditionalDisputeGame` embeds `ChainProofParams` plus `MakeRespected` (`op-deployer/pkg/deployer/state/chain_intent.go:17-52`) — so a second game type with its own prestate is a supported intent shape. `kona-host` is an ordinary host binary needing no MIPS cross-compiler, so it builds natively on macOS ARM once Rust is installed; only the **prestate** needs the cross-compile, which is what CI is for (D-0059).
- **Consequence:** **US-071 step 0b gains a second requirement** — the redeploy must register game type 8, not only pin a prestate — and the gate text is updated in all four places named in `AGENTS.md`. **F7-7 is rewritten** to build a **Kona** prestate rather than assuming the permissioned/op-program path — but it cannot be dispatched until **E-D0059-1** is settled, because chain-agnostic and config-baked builds are different workflows. **Two new tasks follow and are not yet written:** obtaining `kona-host` on the mini (Rust install, native build) and teaching `scripts/02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` to emit the additional-game intent stanza (this is now F7-6's real scope, wider than "write one field"). `CHALLENGER_TRACE_TYPE` stays operator-set with no default (D-0052) and `cannon-kona` remains refused until `CHALLENGER_KONA_SERVER` names an executable (D-0054, unchanged and now load-bearing rather than theoretical). **Still unverified, and must be before the wipe:** that op-deployer 0.7.1 actually registers a type-8 game correctly against these contracts, and that `kona-host` builds and serves pre-images on macOS ARM. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`.

### D-0061 — **E-D0059-1 resolved: split the deploy.** The type-8 game is registered in a *second* apply after the network is healthy, so the prestate can commit to the real post-wipe rollup config
- **Context:** D-0059/E-D0059-1 recorded a circularity — a Kona prestate that commits to chain 852's config needs the post-wipe `rollup.json` (`genesis.l1`, `genesis.l2`, `genesis.l2_time` all change on redeploy), but the redeploy must already commit to a prestate hash. Two escapes were on the table: build chain-agnostic and accept Kona's documented oracle fallback, or split the deploy. 2026-08-20.
- **Decision (operator):** **split the deploy.** The wipe registers only the standard game. Once the network is back and cross-checked healthy, the new `rollup.json` is committed to `deployments/sepolia/`, CI builds a Kona prestate with that config baked in via `KONA_CUSTOM_CONFIGS_DIR`, the operator verifies it locally with `cannon witness --input`, and a **second `op-deployer apply`** registers the type-8 game carrying that prestate with `MakeRespected`. No oracle fallback and no soundness gap: the program image commits to 852's actual configuration.
- **Why this is safe to do as a second apply.** `dangerousAdditionalDisputeGames` is gated by `shouldDeployAdditionalDisputeGames`, which compares the intent against recorded state (`op-deployer/pkg/deployer/pipeline/dispute_games.go:34,260`), so a later apply adds the new game without redeploying anything already live. Crucially the second apply happens **outside the outage window** — the chain is up, writers are running, and nothing is under time pressure. That is strictly better than performing a CI round-trip between "writers stopped" and "network restored".
- **How CI gets the config.** `deployments/sepolia/.deployer/` is entirely gitignored (`.gitignore:9`), but `deployments/sepolia/deployments.json` is committed, so the pattern for publishing post-deploy artifacts already exists. The post-wipe `rollup.json` (1.4 KB) is committed to `deployments/sepolia/` and the workflow reads it from the repo — auditable and reviewable, rather than pasted into a form field. This publishes nothing new: `pack-replica-artifacts.sh` already ships genesis and `rollup.json` to the public replica repo, so the file is public by design. **Assumption flagged rather than asserted:** no secret has ever been present in `rollup.json`; if that changes, this route changes.
- **Consequence:** **US-071 step 0b shrinks and step 8b is new.** Step 0b is now a *code* gate — deploy script able to emit the additional-game stanza (F7-6, wider than originally scoped), the CI workflow (F7-7), and `kona-host` on the mini — and explicitly **not** the prestate, which cannot exist yet. The prestate build and type-8 registration become **step 8b**, after the health cross-check. This also relaxes the pressure D-0049 created: less must be finished before the announcement, not more. **US-073 now depends on step 8b, not on step 3.** F7-7 is unblocked and takes the post-wipe `rollup.json` from the repo as its input. Still unverified before the wipe: that op-deployer 0.7.1 registers a type-8 game correctly against these contracts, and that `kona-host` builds and serves pre-images on macOS ARM (D-0060). Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`.

### D-0062 — **F7-8 done: `kona-host` 1.0.2 builds natively on macOS ARM.** Plus a CI toolchain skew the step-0b smoke test caught in 0 seconds
- **Context:** D-0060 made game type 8 / `cannon-kona` the only playable path, which needs a Kona pre-image server on the mini. D-0059 recorded that Rust was absent. Executed 2026-08-20; this entry records what was measured, not what was expected.
- **Finding 1 — it builds, and the pinned toolchain is honoured automatically.** `rustup` was installed fresh (none existed: no `rustup`, `cargo`, `rustc`, no Homebrew rust). Default toolchain resolves to `stable` = **1.98.0**, but inside `~/src/fortel2/optimism/rust` `rustup show active-toolchain` reports **`1.94-aarch64-apple-darwin (overridden by rust-toolchain.toml)`** and `rustc --version` there is **1.94.1**. So `cargo build --release -p kona-host` used the tree-pinned compiler without anyone having to ask for it. Result: **`kona-host` 1.0.2**, 22 MB, symlinked to `~/src/fortel2/bin/kona-host` per the README pattern. No MIPS cross-compiler was needed — that is only for the prestate, which is CI's job (D-0059). This closes the half of D-0059 Finding 3 that said Rust was a blocker for the *host*; it was only ever a blocker for the *prestate*.
- **Finding 2 — the binary matches what op-challenger actually invokes.** `kona-host single --help` exposes **all eleven** flags `KonaExecutor.OracleCommand` passes: `--l1-node-address`, `--l1-beacon-address`, `--l2-node-address`, `--l1-head`, `--agreed-l2-head-hash`, `--agreed-l2-output-root`, `--claimed-l2-output-root`, `--claimed-l2-block-number`, `--server`, `--data-dir`, `--rollup-config-path`. `CHALLENGER_KONA_SERVER` is now set to the absolute path in local `.env.sepolia`; verified non-empty and executable, so the D-0054 refusal will no longer fire for `cannon-kona`.
- **Finding 3 — correction to a mid-investigation misread, recorded so nobody repeats it.** `NewKonaExecutor()` sets `nativeMode: false`, and a grep-shaped read of `kona_server_executor.go` makes it look as though no rollup config is passed in server mode — which would mean kona-host falling back to a registry that does not contain 852. Reading the whole function, the `--rollup-config-path` block sits **outside** the `nativeMode` branch and fires whenever `cfg.RollupConfigPaths` is non-empty, which our start script guarantees (D-0053). kona-host reads 852's config from a file. There is no host-side registry problem.
- **Finding 4 — the CI `just` skew, and why the smoke test paid for itself immediately.** The first step-0b dispatch failed in **0 seconds**: `error: The [script] attribute is currently unstable`. Cause: `build-prestate.yml` installed `just` **1.40.0** while `README.md`'s toolchain table pins **1.56.0**. The pinned monorepo uses `[script('bash')]` in **eight** recipes across `justfile` and `rust/justfile` and never sets `set unstable`; 1.40.0 rejects that at **parse** time, so it dies before reaching any recipe. Verified locally that 1.56.0 parses the exact recipe (`just -n build-kona-reproducible-prestate-variant kona-client prestate-artifacts-cannon`) with **and** without `JUST_UNSTABLE=1`. Fixed by matching the README table rather than setting the unstable flag: the defect was CI running a different `just` than the documented stack, and the flag would have papered over that. **Root cause was the F7-7 brief, which said "install `just`" without naming a version** — the same unstated-requirement failure mode AGENTS.md now warns about.
- **Finding 5 — the step-0b smoke test ran green, and the runtime estimate was wrong by an order of magnitude.** Run `32416709442` on `66f51a1`: **4.5 minutes wall clock**, of which the Docker build was **3.7**. E-F7-7-2 had estimated 45-90 minutes; both the worker and the planner assumed a from-scratch Rust toolchain build. It is not: `cannon-repro.dockerfile`'s build env is `FROM us-docker.pkg.dev/oplabs-tools-artifacts/images/cannon-builder:v2.0.0@sha256:2a1db1d7…`, a **digest-pinned prebuilt op-labs image**, so only `kona-client` itself compiles. Reproducibility is unaffected — the image is pinned by digest, not by tag. Results: commitment `0x0344d9edad14c46fa5e8d95883911697405b1c8196f9a632ad4d04067414ced2`; **check 1 PASS** (`witnessHash` == `.pre`, and it parsed, so stateVersion 8); **check 2 PASS** (found `9fe210f5…` in the image); sentinel absent, so the scanner can fail; stock-source negative control valid. Artifact `kona-prestate-cannon`, 3.67 MB. **That commitment is a pre-wipe smoke-test value and must not be pinned anywhere** (D-0061). Three things the F7-7 handoff flagged as reasoned-not-measured are now measured: the Route A generator runs in CI, the generated JSON **deserializes** (a malformed config would have failed `build.rs` inside that build), and check 2's needle is findable in a real image.
- **Decision:** F7-8 is complete and the prestate pipeline is **proven end to end**. `cannon-kona` now has an executable pre-image server on the mini, and every CI toolchain version in `build-prestate.yml` must match `README.md`'s toolchain table — that table is the reference for this stack and CI is not exempt from it.
- **Consequence:** Step 0b's three prerequisites are now: F7-7 (workflow — **landed**, and proven once the smoke test completes), F7-8 (`kona-host` — **done**), F7-6 (emit the additional-game stanza — **not started**, and the only one left). Operational note for the planner role: the GitHub token used for docs PRs lacked the **`workflow`** scope, so writes to `.github/workflows/` returned **404, not 403** — a confusing failure worth recognising on sight. The scope has since been granted. The measured runtime also settles a design question left open in F7-7: check 2 uses image inspection rather than a negative-control second build because a second build "roughly doubles runtime" — at 3.7 minutes that objection is void. **F7-9 should replace image inspection with the negative control** (build once without custom configs, assert the commitments differ), which is decisive rather than probabilistic and removes the page-straddle false-negative risk the planner review raised. The build is also re-runnable on a whim rather than once-per-wipe, which is a materially better operational position. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`; **step 0b now waits only on F7-6**.

### D-0063 — **F7-6 merged (#104); step 0b is complete.** Registering the type-8 game at step 8b **flips the chain's respected game type** — a consequence no document had recorded
- **Context:** F7-6 was the last step-0b prerequisite (D-0062). Reviewed and merged as **#104** (`075e0bb`). This entry records what the planner review verified *independently* rather than accepting from the handoff, the two escalations the worker was required to answer, and one property of `MakeRespected` that neither the brief, the PRD, nor the README had noticed.
- **Finding 1 — the review re-derived the evidence rather than reading it.** Byte-identity was regenerated by slicing the intent-generating region out of both `origin/main` and `d0cb2a6` and running each under an identical dummy environment with `FAULT_GAME_ABSOLUTE_PRESTATE` unset: **sha256 `75daeb7a97c14bcdf1e0cddfc8e9365b4ff38ade65485233935f3163c1621957`, 1665 bytes, both sides.** The probe was then proven falsifiable by forcing the append unconditional. Gate counts reproduced exactly on both refs (110 → 115). **Nine** mutations all went RED against `scripts/test-helpers.sh`, including one not in the handoff: **moving the refusal call to after `rm -rf "$DEPLOY_DIR"`**. The function's comment claims it must refuse before any write, and the `awk` line-ordering assertion genuinely enforces that — the claim most expensive to be wrong about is the one that is actually tested. Adversarial prestate inputs (no `0x` prefix, 63 hex, 65 hex, `0X` prefix, trailing space, whitespace-only) all fail closed; whitespace-only is **not** treated as unset.
- **Finding 2 — the generated intent decodes against the real Go types; the handoff's largest declared gap is closed.** The worker could not parse the TOML with BurntSushi and said so. The planner review built a throwaway Go module with a `replace` onto the pinned monorepo and decoded the generated file with the real `state.Intent` and **BurntSushi v1.5.0**: `undecoded keys: []`, `DisputeGameType=8`, `AbsolutePrestate` round-tripped, `MaxGameDepth=73 SplitDepth=30`, `ClockExtension=600 MaxClockDuration=7200`, `VMType="CANNON"`, `MakeRespected=true`, chain scalars and `[chains.roles]` intact. `undecoded keys: []` is the load-bearing line: nothing was silently ignored, and the F7-6 fact-table claim that untagged Go fields match on field name is now **measured, not inferred**. `VMTypeCannon = "CANNON"` and `MipsVersion()` → `versions.GetCurrentVersion()`, so this stanza deploys a **stateVersion 8** MIPS as intended.
- **Finding 3 — NEW: `MakeRespected = true` calls `anchorStateRegistry.setRespectedGameType(8)` on the live chain.** `SetDisputeGameImpl.s.sol` passes the AnchorStateRegistry only when `MakeRespected` is set, and then calls `setRespectedGameType`. Four consequences, all verified against the pinned contracts:
  - **(a) It is Guardian-gated, not L1PAO** (`AnchorStateRegistry.sol:153-155`, `_assertOnlyGuardian`). The second apply therefore needs **both** roles from one signing key. Our intent sets `SuperchainGuardian` and `l1ProxyAdminOwner` to `${ADMIN_ADDRESS}`, so this is satisfied — but it doubles the consequence of Finding 4.
  - **(b) Existing games are NOT invalidated.** `isGameRespected` reads `wasRespectedGameTypeWhenCreated`, which `FaultDisputeGame.initialize` stamps per game at creation (`FaultDisputeGame.sol:318-319`). Games created while type 1 was respected keep the flag permanently; nothing in flight breaks, and no proven withdrawal is retroactively voided. **The planner expected retroactive invalidation and was wrong** — recorded so the fear is not re-derived and 8b is not over-planned around it.
  - **(c) New *type-1* games created after the flip get `false`**, which fails `isGameClaimValid`. `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE=1` today (`.env.sepolia.example`, README § Phase 5). A proposer left on type 1 after step 8b keeps posting games that cannot validate a withdrawal and cannot advance the anchor — a silent money-path regression on a healthy network. **Step 8b must switch the proposer to game type 8 and restart it.** This is added to the operator sequence by this commit.
  - **(d) `SetDisputeGameImpl` never calls `setInitBond`, so `initBonds[8]` stays 0.** Type-8 games are free to create. Accepted deliberately for a learning chain — recorded as a choice rather than left to be discovered.
- **Finding 4 — E-F7-6-1 (L1PAO) resolved: the precondition holds by convention, not by enforcement, and it now gates two authorizations.** The chain is `l1ProxyAdminOwner = ${ADMIN_ADDRESS}` in the intent; `op-deployer apply --private-key "$ADMIN_PRIVATE_KEY"`; `env.Deployer = crypto.PubkeyToAddress(...)` (`apply.go:223-225`); compared at `dispute_games.go:39-41`. Nothing pairs the key to the address — `require_eth_address` is format-only and `refuse_foundry_defaults_unless_local_l2` only rejects the Anvil keys. Two facts the handoff did not have: the pairing also gates **Guardian** for Finding 3(a); and `deployments/sepolia/deploy-spend.txt` records `spent_eth≈0` for the 2026-07-23 apply, so **it cannot serve as evidence the pair ever matched**. `ADMIN_ADDRESS` has **nonce 28** on Sepolia — strong circumstantial evidence the pair is correct today, not proof. The risk window is imminent: US-070 step 0 has the operator hand-editing `.env.sepolia`, and a mismatch is silent through the wipe, surfacing only at 8b when L1PAO is already baked into deployed contracts. **Reserved as F7-10** — a near-copy of the existing check at `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh:134-147`, argv caveat and all.
- **Finding 5 — E-F7-6-2 (once-only) resolved, with a second guard the handoff missed.** `shouldDeployAdditionalDisputeGames` returns false once state records any additional game (`dispute_games.go:260-270`), regardless of hash. **On-chain there is a second, independent guard:** `SetDisputeGameImpl.s.sol` requires `factory.gameImpls(8) == address(0)` ("SDGI-10"). So the obvious workaround — edit `state.json` and re-run apply — also reverts. Correcting a wrong prestate needs a direct L1PAO `setImplementation` outside op-deployer, or another network-wide wipe. **Therefore verifying the CI commitment with `cannon witness --input` before the second apply is a gate, not advice**, and is written into the operator sequence as one by this commit.
- **Finding 6 — E-F7-6-3 was correctly not raised; E-F7-6-4 was real and is fixed here.** The worker agreed refusal (a) is right: at wipe time the post-wipe rollup config does not exist, so a one-shot apply cannot commit to the new chain. Codex's P2 on #104 — the six-knob block still told operators to pin the prestate before `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY=1`, which the new guard would refuse *after writers were already stopped* — was real and fixed in `.env.sepolia.example` and README steps 4/8b (`d0cb2a6`), and the helper now greps that sentence absent so it cannot return. **The same contradiction survived in this PRD's Constraints "Prestate" row** ("the redeploy must register game type 8"); that is the "canonical operator instructions" Codex asked for and it is corrected by this commit.
- **Finding 7 — two defects in the first draft of *this entry's own* documentation, both found by the Codex bot on #105, both verified against the source before being fixed.** **(i) "Restart the proposer" was not executable as written.** `start_bg` returns 0 when the pidfile's process is still alive (`scripts/lib.sh:122-125`), and the **stock** branch of `06-start-proposer-sepolia.sh` — the default on this deploy — does not stop a live proposer first, unlike the `USE_CUSTOM_PROPOSER` branch, whose comment at `:54` names this exact hazard. An operator editing `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE` and re-running the start script would get `op-proposer already running` and a zero exit, leaving the type-1 proposer posting precisely the non-respected games step 8b exists to prevent. Step 8b now prescribes the README § Phase 5 kill-switch idiom (`kill "$(cat "$DATA_DIR/pids/op-proposer.pid")"`) followed by the start script, plus a `game-type=8` confirmation. The same hazard applies to any service reconfigured in place, `op-challenger` included. **(ii) "The next action is the announcement" skipped step 0.** US-070 still records the local `.env.sepolia` immutables edit as pending, and README step 2 gates the notice on all coding **and config** work being complete — so the order is step 0, then step 1. Corrected. The D-0062 pattern holds and is worth restating: the bot's findings keep landing on planner-authored *specifications*, not on worker code.
- **Decision:** F7-6 is merged and **step 0b is complete**. ~~There is no remaining Phase 7 coding work before the announcement~~ — **superseded 2026-08-21 by D-0064**: that clause was already false when written, because F7-10 is identified in this entry's own Finding 4. F7-10 landed as #107; no further Phase 7 code is required as of D-0064. So D-0049's rule now points at the notice rather than at more code. **Step 8b is upgraded from "a second apply" to a four-part sequence:** verify the commitment with `cannon witness --input` (a gate, per Finding 5) → second `op-deployer apply` → switch `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE` to 8 → **stop and then start** the proposer (Finding 3(c); a plain re-run of the start script is a silent no-op, Finding 7). **Step 0b being closed does not mean announce now** — step 0's `.env.sepolia` immutables edit is still outstanding, and README step 2 gates the notice on config work too.
- **Consequence:** **F7-10 reserved** — assert that `ADMIN_PRIVATE_KEY` derives `ADMIN_ADDRESS` in `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` before any spend, mirroring the challenger script. It could not be dispatched alongside F7-6 because it edits the same file. **F7-9** (negative-control prestate build, D-0062) and **F7-5** (preflight prestate-commitment check, D-0057) remain unwritten and are **not** step-0b blockers. Next free decision id is **D-0064**. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`, and nothing here authorizes the second apply.

### D-0064 — **F7-10 merged (#107): the wipe now refuses a mismatched or empty `ADMIN_PRIVATE_KEY`.** Supersedes D-0063's "no remaining Phase 7 coding work", which was already false when written
- **Context:** F7-10 was reserved in **D-0063 Finding 4** and dispatched after the operator chose to land it *before* the announcement rather than after. Merged as **#107** (`dbe454f`). This entry records what the planner review re-derived independently, the three escalation answers, one mechanism the handoff got wrong in an instructive way, and two corrections to D-0063 that this task exposed.
- **Finding 1 — the guard is verified, not accepted.** Gate reproduced **115 → 122** on both refs *and* under both env modes (bare and the brief's temporary-env recipe), so the suite is env-mode independent. Byte-identity of the generated intent regenerated from `wave22-base` and the branch under an identical dummy environment: **1665 bytes, sha256 `75daeb7a97c14bcdf1e0cddfc8e9365b4ff38ade65485233935f3163c1621957`** — the same hash measured during the F7-6 review with the same dummy env, which is a useful cross-review consistency check. **Seven mutations, all RED**, including the one that matters most (mismatch `exit 1` → `return 0`). On the positional-`cast` mutation, three assertions fire rather than the one the handoff reported — better covered than claimed.
- **Finding 2 — the dangerous direction is the *false failure*, and it is now closed by measurement.** A false pass bakes a wrong L1PAO/Guardian into new contracts; a false *mismatch* aborts a scheduled wipe after writers are already stopped, turning a routine step into an unplanned outage. Nine adversarial drives against the extracted guard: checksummed/lowercase/UPPERCASE all match; wrong address, empty key and unset key all refuse; a key **without** the `0x` prefix still matches (correctly tolerant of a realistic operator variant); malformed and truncated keys fail closed via `set -e` on the `cast` failure. The only input that could produce a false mismatch is a trailing-space `ADMIN_ADDRESS`, and `require_eth_address` at `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh:15` rejects it first — `is_eth_address` is `^0x[0-9a-fA-F]{40}$`, verified directly. **No input that reaches this guard can produce a false mismatch.**
- **Finding 3 — a green assertion that proved nothing, and the planner's own brief caused it.** The one existing check the worker modified (`env -u FORTEL2_ENV` on the `gen-viewer-config` bad-address test) is a real **strengthening**, but not for the stated reason. The handoff said a leaked `FORTEL2_ENV` "loads a valid `BATCHER_ADDRESS` and the rejection never fires", which would turn the suite **red**; it does not — `main` under the recipe passes 115 with that PASS line present. Rebuilding the fixture and driving `gen-viewer-config.sh` directly on `main` shows what actually happens:

  | | good address | bad address |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | no `FORTEL2_ENV` | `rc=0` | `rc=1` — assertion discriminates |
  | `FORTEL2_ENV` leaked | `rc=1` | `rc=1` — **assertion is vacuous** |

  stderr on the leaked run: `ERROR: missing …/deployments/.deployer/rollup.json — run deploy scripts first`. The mechanism is that `lib.sh` does `set -a; source "$FORTEL2_ENV_FILE"` **after** the caller's `FORTEL2_ROOT=` command prefix, so a leaked absolute `FORTEL2_ENV` re-sets `FORTEL2_ROOT` and the fixture is bypassed entirely — the test rejects for a missing artifact instead of for the bad address. A silently vacuous assertion is worse than a red suite. Precedent for the fix already existed 50 lines above at `:249-251` for a different trigger. **Root cause was the planner's temporary-env verification recipe in the F7-10 brief**: anyone prescribing an env override against this suite must pair it with `env -u FORTEL2_ENV` on fixture-scoped subprocess runs.
- **Finding 4 — E-F7-10-1 (role-key sweep) answered.** `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh` and now `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` are the **only** scripts that pair a signing key to its separately-configured address. Of the rest, a mismatch is loud-and-cheap: the sequencer fails at op-node startup; the batcher and proposer start and then fail L1 txs from an unfunded signer; `create-bad-proposal-sepolia.sh` reverts at `createGame`; `withdraw-*.sh` revert or move funds to the configured recipient. The exception is **`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh`**, which spends from the key (L2 credit follows `msg.sender`) and then polls `ADMIN_ADDRESS` on L2 for up to 600 s — silent-then-loud, ETH recoverable on the other L2 account. Same shape as the defect this task closed, at much smaller scale. **Recorded as a candidate follow-up, not dispatched and not id-allocated**; nothing on the wipe path depends on it.
- **Finding 5 — E-F7-10-2 (shared `lib.sh` helper) declined by the worker, and the planner agrees.** ~15 lines of duplication with different error text, against refactoring `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh` — a currently-correct script sitting on the fault-proof path. A shared `require_key_matches_address` is a separate task if it ever earns one.
- **Finding 6 — E-F7-10-3: two planner defects, both correctly reported rather than silently rewritten.** **(i)** The brief demanded an assertion that `test-helpers.sh` contain no `0x`-prefixed 64-hex literal beyond the F7-6 prestate constants. That was **impossible**: `test-helpers.sh:79` already carries `DEMO_KEY` for the Foundry tripwire tests, and satisfying the brief literally would have meant deleting an existing fixture. Reframing it as a frozen allowlist is the right handling, and the planner probed it — injecting a new literal still goes RED. Because a committed 64-hex literal deserves checking rather than assuming, it was derived: `0x9965507D1a55bcC2695C58ba16FB37d819B0A4dc`, a well-known public Anvil account, Sepolia nonce 30072, balance 0. Shared public test key, **no exposure, nothing to do**. **(ii)** D-0063's Decision claimed there was no remaining Phase 7 coding work before the announcement — see the Decision below.
- **Finding 7 — a reviewer-instrument failure worth recording, because it is the same class the F7-10 brief was written to prevent.** The planner's first byte-identity probe compared two **empty** files: the heredoc-boundary `grep` failed silently, the extracted blocks were empty, and both sides hashed to `e3b0c442…` (the SHA-256 of zero bytes) producing a confident false "BYTE-IDENTICAL". Caught and redone with non-empty guards, giving the real result in Finding 1. `AGENTS.md:100` requires that a prescribed verification be confirmed runnable *and* falsifiable; this is that rule failing inside the reviewer's own instrument rather than in a brief. **Any probe asserting two artifacts are identical must first assert both are non-empty.**
- **Finding 8 — operator key hygiene, verified rather than assumed.** `.env.sepolia` is now `-rw-------` (600); it had been mode 644 while holding seven live private keys, alongside the `.bak` copies moved out of the repo earlier in the same session. The repo-root `.env` remains 644, but its seven private-key lines are **byte-identical** to the tracked, public `.env.example` (Anvil defaults) — confirmed by digest comparison without printing any value — so nothing secret sits at 644.
- **Decision:** F7-10 is merged and the guard is proven in both failure directions. **This entry supersedes D-0063's sentence "There is no remaining Phase 7 coding work before the announcement", which was false at the moment it was written** — F7-10 had already been identified in D-0063's own Finding 4. The correct statement: step 0b's three code prerequisites (F7-6 / F7-7 / F7-8) closed at D-0063, F7-10 closed here, and **no further Phase 7 code is required now**. Step 0b's "order from here is F7-10 → step 0 → step 1" is consequently stale and is corrected by this commit to **step 0 → step 1**. The general lesson, and the second time this session: a completion claim written in the same entry that identifies remaining work is a contradiction the writer is uniquely placed to catch and uniquely likely to miss.
- **Consequence:** The next action is the operator's `.env.sepolia` immutables edit (**US-070 step 0**) — the exact edit F7-10 now guards — and then the **announcement**, which is the first irreversible step in the whole sequence. **F7-9** (negative-control prestate build, D-0062) and **F7-5** (preflight prestate-commitment check, D-0057) remain unwritten and are **not** blockers. New unassigned candidate: the `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing gap (Finding 4). Next free decision id is **D-0065**. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`, and nothing here authorizes the second apply.

### D-0065 — **US-070 step 0 executed and verified.** Duplicate assignments in `.env.sepolia` silently shadowed four of the six immutables, and **three of the six have no refusal guarding them at all**
- **Context:** The operator wrote the six Phase 7 immutables into local `.env.sepolia` on 2026-08-21, per D-0049. Verification of that edit surfaced a hazard that had nothing to do with the values chosen and everything to do with how the file is loaded. This entry records what was measured, the near-miss, and the coverage gap behind it.
- **Finding 1 — the values were right and the file was still wrong.** The operator added a `#Fault Proofs` block at lines 9-14 carrying all six correct values. The **original Phase 2b block lower in the file was left in place**, re-assigning four of them. `lib.sh` loads config with `set -a; source "$FORTEL2_ENV_FILE"`, so **the last assignment wins**. Verified by sourcing rather than reading — the effective values were `FAULT_GAME_CLOCK_EXTENSION=600`, `FAULT_GAME_MAX_CLOCK_DURATION=`**`10`**, `PREIMAGE_ORACLE_CHALLENGE_PERIOD=3600`, `PROOF_MATURITY_DELAY_SECONDS=`**`12`**, `DISPUTE_GAME_FINALITY_DELAY_SECONDS=`**`6`**, `FAULT_GAME_WITHDRAWAL_DELAY=`**`1`**. Four of six were the old learning-short values while the file *read* as though all six were new. Reading the file would not have caught this; only sourcing it did.
- **Finding 2 — it failed closed, and that was luck rather than design.** `FAULT_GAME_MAX_CLOCK_DURATION=10` trips the `initialize` inequality (`10 < max(2*600, 600+3600) = 4200`), so `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` would have refused. But **that check covers only two of the six knobs.** `PROOF_MATURITY_DELAY_SECONDS`, `DISPUTE_GAME_FINALITY_DELAY_SECONDS` and `FAULT_GAME_WITHDRAWAL_DELAY` are constrained by **nothing** — no inequality, no refusal, no assertion. Had the operator responded to a clock complaint by removing only the `FAULT_GAME_MAX_CLOCK_DURATION` duplicate — the obvious and locally correct fix — the wipe would have proceeded and permanently baked in **12 s proof maturity, 6 s finality delay, 1 s withdrawal delay** on a chain specified for 30 min / 30 min / 1 h. That is the PRD's "a forgotten parameter is a second network-wide wipe", reached by a route nobody had modelled: not a forgotten parameter, a *remembered* one that was overridden further down the same file.
- **Finding 3 — the repair, and the evidence that it was safe.** Four duplicate assignments removed (first occurrence kept), the orphaned Phase 2b comment repointed. Executed by the operator, not by an agent, per D-0049's rule that the file holds signing keys. Verified afterwards: all six knobs correct; **zero** duplicated assignments remain; the seven private-key lines are **byte-identical by digest** before and after, so the rewrite touched no secret; every line other than the four removed and the one comment is identical to the backup; mode is `-rw-------`; the backup lives at `~/src/fortel2/env-backups/` (700 dir, 600 file), **outside the git tree**. The deploy script's own clock gate (lines 84-102, extracted verbatim rather than restated) passes with the current values, and the same harness goes red when fed the old ones — so it is falsifiable, not vacuous. `tasks/spike-phase-7-immutables.md` records the same six values; all six match the file.
- **Finding 4 — a third reviewer-instrument failure this session, same class as D-0064 Finding 7.** The first attempt to run the deploy script's clock gate sliced from a line partway through a *preceding* refusal, so the harness executed a dangling `echo … exit 1` and reported a confident, entirely bogus `ERROR: FAULT_GAME_MAX_CLOCK_DURATION (7200) must be >= FAULT_GAME_CLOCK_EXTENSION (600)`. Caught, re-extracted from the true start of the block, and only then trusted. The pattern across D-0064 Finding 7 and this one is consistent enough to state as a rule: **an extracted-code harness must be falsified before its green *or* its red is believed** — a bogus failure wastes the same time as a bogus pass and is more likely to be acted on.
- **Decision:** **US-070 step 0's `.env.sepolia` half is complete and verified.** The remaining half of step 0 is the notice date, which D-0049 fixed as a rule rather than a date. `tasks/spike-phase-7-immutables.md`'s status line and pending-checklist are corrected by this commit — they still described the edit as outstanding and listed two knobs as absent, which has not been true since 2026-08-21.
- **Consequence:** **F7-11 is reserved** — teach `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` to refuse *duplicate* assignments of the six immutables in the resolved env file, and to refuse when any of the six is absent rather than silently taking a script default. The operator chose (2026-08-21) to land it **before the announcement**, on the same reasoning as F7-10: a fail-closed guard in front of an irreversible step is worth one worker cycle, and D-0049 bars coding inside the notice window. Note for whoever writes it: the script's existing defaults at lines 84-87 (`5`, `10`, `1`, `86400`) are what make absence silent, and the comment above them argues those defaults are deliberately honest about the live chain — so "refuse when absent" must be gated on the wipe path rather than applied unconditionally. **Reserving F7-11 is itself a four-place change, and the first draft of this entry missed it** — caught by the Codex bot on #110. Step 0b, README 1b, the roadmap Phase 7 row and `.env.sepolia.example` all still enumerated the code gate as complete through F7-10, so an operator following any runbook could have announced before F7-11 landed. All four are corrected in this commit, and `AGENTS.md`'s four-place rule now states explicitly that *reserving* a gate counts, not only implementing one. This is the **fourth** instance in one session of new work being recorded in one place while the gates that should block on it went stale — D-0063's Decision, D-0064's step 0b order, this entry, and the `.env.sepolia.example` list. `.env.sepolia.example` also gains an explicit assign-each-exactly-once warning, since it is the file operators copy from and its own six-knob block has the same shape that produced Finding 1. Next free decision id is **D-0066**. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`.

### D-0066 — **F7-11 merged (#112). No Phase 7 code remains; the next action is the announcement, which is the first irreversible step in the sequence**
- **Context:** F7-11 was reserved in D-0065 and dispatched after the operator chose to land it before the notice. Merged as **#112** (`d27514e`). `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` now refuses a *duplicate* assignment of any of the six Phase 7 immutables on every run, and refuses an *absent or empty* one when `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY=1` or `FAULT_GAME_ABSOLUTE_PRESTATE` is set. This entry records what the planner review re-derived independently, both escalation answers, and one gap in the brief that two bots found before the planner did.
- **Finding 1 — the mechanical claims hold.** Gate reproduced **122 → 189** on both refs (67 new assertions). `scripts/test-helpers.sh` verified as a **pure insertion** — one 475-line append hunk, **zero removed lines, zero change/delete hunks** — which confirms the "F7-6 and F7-10 blocks untouched" claim by construction rather than by inspection. Byte-identity of the generated intent regenerated with both captures asserted non-empty first: **1665 bytes, sha256 `75daeb7a97c14bcdf1e0cddfc8e9365b4ff38ade65485233935f3163c1621957`** — the same value measured in the F7-6 and F7-10 reviews under the same dummy env, now three independent agreeing measurements.
- **Finding 2 — the review tested the invariant rather than the examples, and that is what closed the residual gap.** Re-running a worker's own cases grades their homework against their answer key. The real invariant is narrower: **the scanner's notion of an empty assignment must be exactly bash's notion under `${VAR:-default}`** — a disagreement one way bakes in a default silently, the other way falsely refuses and aborts a scheduled wipe. A differential over **19 value forms**, each sourced by bash for ground truth and compared against the scanner, produced **zero disagreements** — covering `VAR=`, `VAR=""`, `VAR=''`, `VAR= # comment`, `VAR="" # comment`, `VAR=#keep`, `VAR="#keep"`, `VAR=3600 # note`, `VAR=3600#note`, `VAR="  "`, unquoted trailing whitespace, leading whitespace, and both `export` forms. The probe was then falsified: replacing the `[[:space:]]#*` strip with the obvious `%%#*` produces exactly one disagreement — `VAR=#keep`, guard EMPTY vs bash VALUE — a **false refusal**, the direction that aborts an outage window. The implementation avoids this deliberately and says so in a source comment. This also closes the handoff's own residual gap: it reported the `VAR=#keep` risk as "measured; tested via `VAR="#keep"`", which is the *quoted* form and a different input. The unquoted form is now measured directly.
- **Finding 3 — the guard was tested against the file the wipe will actually use.** A guard that refuses the operator's real `.env.sepolia` aborts the redeploy mid-outage, so a fixture proves less than the real thing. The guard reads the env file itself and its error text names only variables and line numbers (verified in the implementation), so it was pointed at the real file with no secret entering any agent's context: **both refusals passed, rc=0, on the wipe path *and* the step-8b path**. Pointed at the **pre-repair backup** — the file as it stood before D-0065 — it produced `ERROR: PROOF_MATURITY_DELAY_SECONDS is assigned more than once in the env file (lines 12, 87).` **Lines 12 and 87 are exactly the line numbers measured during the D-0065 investigation.** The guard catches the real historical defect that motivated it, with correct line numbers, and passes the repaired file. That is the strongest validation available short of running the wipe. Call ordering confirmed directly as well as structurally: refusals at 171-172, `require_min_balance_eth` at 183, `rm -rf "$DEPLOY_DIR"` at 278.
- **Finding 4 — the one real defect was a gap in the planner's brief, found independently by two bots.** Against the first commit (`8c50e79`), Codex (P1) and Cursor Bugbot both identified that `PROOF_MATURITY_DELAY_SECONDS= # choose later` sources as empty while the scanner reported it non-empty — the exact silent-default hazard F7-11 exists to close, on one of the three delay knobs no other check constrains. **The brief enumerated `VAR=` and `VAR=""` and missed the inline-comment form.** Fixed in `4c11e9d` rather than deferred, correctly: an inline comment after an empty value is *emptiness*, not the value validation the brief put out of scope. Independently confirmed — the differential in Finding 2 shows both comment forms now reporting EMPTY in agreement with bash.
- **Finding 5 — E-F7-11-1 (loader placement) answered, and the answer is a follow-up rather than a rejection.** The duplicate hazard is loader-level: `lib.sh` does `set -a; source "$FORTEL2_ENV_FILE"` and the last assignment wins, which affects every consumer script, not the six variables of one. The worker's judgement, which the planner accepts: **duplicates eventually belong in the loader; absence does not** — absence is deploy-specific and correctly gated on the wipe / step-8b paths, so it stays in `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` even if duplicate detection later moves. Not done here because `lib.sh` is CODEOWNERS-privileged, a loader-level refusal changes behaviour for every script at once, and this task was the last announcement blocker. **Recorded as an unassigned candidate follow-up; no id allocated.**
- **Finding 6 — E-F7-11-2 (scope beyond the six) answered, with a reason the planner did not have.** Keep it scoped to the six. Neither `.env.example` nor `.env.sepolia.example` has a duplicated assignment, so broadening *duplicates* to all variables would not break CI today — but broadening **absence** to all variables would refuse `.env.sepolia.example`'s intentionally empty `ADMIN_ADDRESS=` template placeholders. That is a concrete blocker the brief's author had not considered, and it settles the question rather than deferring it. Legitimate re-assignment in operator-local `.env` / `.env.sepolia` remains unmeasured by design — D-0049 bars agents from reading the latter.
- **Decision:** F7-11 is merged and proven in both failure directions. **There is no remaining Phase 7 code.** Step 0b's five prerequisites are closed — F7-6 (#104), F7-7, F7-8, F7-10 (#107), F7-11 (#112) — and step 0's `.env.sepolia` edit is done and verified (D-0065). All four gates now read **order from here is step 1**. Under D-0049, the notice is therefore released: it goes out once all Phase 7 coding **and config** work is complete and reviewed, and that condition is now met. **One correction before that release, and it is the fifth instance of the same class in this session — this time on the entry that releases an irreversible step.** Caught by the Codex bot on #113: US-071's binding acceptance criterion at `tasks/prd-phase-7-fault-proofs.md:133` was still **unchecked**, still enumerated the code gate as three items rather than five (no F7-10, no F7-11), and — worse than staleness — still **required the prestate artifact in hand before the notice**, which D-0061 established is impossible: the prestate commits to a `rollup.json` that does not exist until after the wipe. The criterion also named the wrong mechanism, `[globalDeployOverrides]`, where F7-6 actually emits `[[chains.dangerousAdditionalDisputeGames]]` (verified: `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh:349-351`). An operator following the authoritative checklist would have been told to wait for something that cannot exist yet — a deadlock instruction sitting in the acceptance criteria of the story that gates the announcement. Corrected in this commit: the criterion is checked and rewritten to the five real items, and the step-8b requirements it had wrongly absorbed (build the prestate, verify the commitment with `cannon witness --input` as a gate, the second apply with a dual-role signer, and the proposer switch to type 8) are moved to **US-073's** acceptance criteria, where step 8b lives — they existed in no acceptance criteria at all before this.
- **Consequence:** **The next action is the announcement to SOS, Render, and every Phase 3b friend (D-0028 / D-0029), and it is the first irreversible step in the entire sequence.** Everything to this point has been reversible; a notice cannot be recalled. D-0049 requires **≥24 h** between the notice and step 2 (stop Mac writers), and D-0029 records that this wipe must not be coupled to publishing the Access write hostname. Still unwritten and **not** blockers: **F7-9** (negative-control prestate build, D-0062) and **F7-5** (preflight prestate-commitment check, D-0057). Unassigned candidates: the `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` key/address pairing gap (D-0064 Finding 4) and loader-level duplicate detection (Finding 5). Next free decision id is **D-0067**. Nothing here authorizes `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY`, and nothing here sends the notice.

### D-0067 — **The notice went out 2026-08-21 13:00 PDT. Step 2 is gated on 2026-08-22 20:00Z, and every "party" turns out to be the same operator**
- **Context:** US-071 step 1 executed. D-0049 had deliberately left the notice as a *rule* rather than a date — "once all Phase 7 coding and config work is complete and reviewed". D-0066 established that condition was met; this entry converts the rule into the timestamp everything downstream is measured from. **This is the first irreversible action in Phase 7.** Everything before it — every PR, every guard, the `.env.sepolia` repair — was revertible. A notice is not.
- **Finding 1 — the timestamp, and the gate it creates.** Sent **2026-08-21 13:00 PDT = 2026-08-21T20:00Z**. D-0049 requires **≥24 h** between the notice and step 2 (stop Mac writers), so the earliest legal start is **2026-08-22T20:00Z = 2026-08-22 13:00 PDT**. That number, not "tomorrow", is the gate: US-071's criterion "notified ≥1 day ahead (D-0028)" is satisfied only from that instant.
- **Finding 2 — recipients, and a deviation from D-0028 worth recording explicitly.** Sent to **SettlementOS, SOS Explorer, L2Replica, and ChainBank**. D-0028's list reads "SOS + Render + every Phase 3b friend"; **no Phase 3b friends were notified because none are waiting on this** — recorded as an operator determination, not as something an agent verified. `replica/FRIENDS.md` is an onboarding artifact and states that recruiting is operator-owned, so its existence does not imply active friend operators. If a friend node is ever stood up, this row of D-0028 becomes live again and the next reset must notify them.
- **Finding 3 — D-0029 honoured.** The notice did **not** couple this wipe to publishing the Access write hostname. That request was withdrawn by SOS and the two must stay independent; confirmed by the operator at the time of sending.
- **Finding 4 — the coordination model is not what the documents assume, and that changes where the risk sits.** The PRD, the README reset procedure and D-0028 are all written as *multi-party coordination* — announce, wait, signal, confirm. In practice **the operator runs all five systems personally**: ForteL2, L2Replica, SettlementOS, SOS Explorer and ChainBank. There is no counterparty to mis-signal. The real failure mode is therefore the opposite of what the docs guard against: **a phase left half-finished while switching hats** — alerting suppressed in one system and never re-armed, an address book not rebuilt, a replica not confirmed to hold the new artifacts before its disk is cleared. A coordination document is organised by party; a solo playbook has to be organised by time, because that is the order one person executes in. An execution-ordered run sheet was produced for the reset itself, with a coverage grid for exactly this reason. **The most forgettable item is re-arming the two suppressed alerting paths (SOS Explorer, ChainBank) hours after suppressing them** — silent monitoring is worse than none, because it is believed.
- **Finding 5 — one fact that removes work, verified rather than assumed.** **ChainBank needs no address changes.** The batcher and proposer are Sepolia L1 externally-owned accounts held in operator config, not contracts produced by the deploy, so a re-genesis does not move them. Its reconciler keeps working against the same addresses throughout. Its only real exposure is that batcher L1 spend goes flat while the writers are stopped, which any "the funder has stopped working" heuristic will misread.
- **Finding 6 — a pre-flight was built and falsified before it is relied on.** Six offline checks against live config: env file mode 600; zero duplicated assignments; the six immutables equal to D-0049's values; the deploy script's own clock gate; the **F7-11** duplicate and absence guards driven against the real env file on the wipe path; and the **F7-10** admin key/address pairing. Two of these execute the deploy script's actual guard code rather than a restatement of it. All six pass. It was then falsified in two directions: a wrong immutable value reports failure, and — the important one — **a broken code-extraction anchor now reports `NOT CLEAR TO PROCEED` rather than a pass.** The first draft did *not* do that: it printed "ALL CHECKS PASSED" while a check had silently failed to run, which on a go/no-go gate is the same defect class this project has spent D-0064 and D-0065 recording. It also hard-coded a line range that F7-11 had shifted by ~136 lines — the fourth instance of the extracted-harness trap D-0065 Finding 4 names. **The durable fix is to anchor extraction on content, never on line numbers, and to treat "could not run" as failure.**
- **Decision:** **US-071 step 1 is complete.** Its acceptance criterion "SOS + Render + every Phase 3b friend notified ≥1 day ahead (D-0028)" is checked by this commit, with the friend deviation recorded in Finding 2. `tasks/spike-phase-7-immutables.md` §4 and its pending checklist are updated — they still described the notice date as an unmade decision. **Step 2 must not run before 2026-08-22T20:00Z.** Nothing else in the sequence is authorized by this entry.
- **Consequence:** The next action is **step 2**, gated on the timestamp above and preceded by the pre-flight. Between now and then, **nothing should modify `.env.sepolia`** — it is verified correct, and while F7-11 now catches duplicates and empties, no guard catches a wrong-but-well-formed value. If it is edited, re-run the pre-flight rather than assuming. **Finding 7 — instance six, in the pull request that recorded this entry.** The Codex bot caught it on #114. Recording the notice as sent left four live statements saying otherwise: PRD step 0b still read *order from here is step 1*, the US-070 roadmap row still said the notice date was a rule rather than a date, the US-071 row still read *Not started*, and README's operator-facing step 2 still described the announcement as a future action with no date. All four are corrected in this commit. **The timing matters more than the count.** The operator had asked, one turn earlier, how a sixth instance would be prevented; the answer given was a discipline — *write the Consequence last, after re-reading and quoting each gate* — and that discipline was not applied in the very next commit. **That is decisive evidence that a discipline-based fix does not work here and a mechanical one is required.** It also invalidates the justification below for deferring F7-12: that justification rested on the operator sequence being currently correct, and it was not.
- **Finding 8 — the pre-flight is now tracked, which it should have been from the start.** Also caught on #114: this entry made step 2 depend on "the pre-flight" while that gate existed only as an untracked file on one machine. `AGENTS.md:100` requires a prescribed verification to be confirmed runnable, and a one-machine artifact fails that on its face. It is committed here as **`scripts/phase7-preflight.sh`** and referenced from README step 2 and the US-071 row. Two caveats stated rather than buried: it was **committed by the planner rather than dispatched to a worker**, because it is read-only, offline, off the deploy path, and needed inside a notice window in which D-0049 bars coding; and it has **no `test-helpers.sh` coverage**, so adding assertions for it is a follow-up. It was manually falsified in two directions before being relied on — a wrong immutable value fails, and a broken extraction anchor reports `NOT CLEAR TO PROCEED` rather than passing.
- **Consequence (revised):** The next action is **step 2**, gated on **2026-08-22T20:00Z** and preceded by `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh` printing `ALL CHECKS PASSED`. Between now and then, **nothing should modify `.env.sepolia`** — F7-11 catches duplicates and empties, but no guard catches a wrong-but-well-formed value; if it is edited, re-run the pre-flight rather than assuming. **F7-12 is now clearly justified** — six instances, the last two caught by a bot rather than by the author, and the discipline proposed as an alternative failed on first contact. It still waits until after the wipe, but for a different and narrower reason than before: the runbooks are correct *as of this commit*, D-0049 bars coding inside the notice window, and the drift that remains is not the kind that misleads a single operator who already knows which step he is on. It must land before **step 8b**. Next free decision id is **D-0068**.
### D-0068 — **The wipe executed. 45 minutes, 0.108 ETH, step 8 green first try — and the first drift instance ever caught by a check instead of a person**
- **Context:** US-071 steps 2–4 and US-072 steps 5–9 executed 2026-08-22, inside the window D-0067 opened. This entry records the whole window: what was done, what the guards did, what the runbooks got wrong while it was happening, and the state the network is now in. It supersedes nothing; it is the execution record D-0067 pointed at.
- **Finding 1 — the pre-flight passed, then was not runnable when it mattered.** `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh` was run 2026-08-22 13:56 PDT and printed `ALL CHECKS PASSED` for all six checks (mode 600 / no duplicate assignments / six immutables `600 7200 3600 1800 1800 3600` / clock gate / F7-11 guards / F7-10 key pairing). It was then **not** re-run immediately before the apply, as README step 2 prescribes: the operator's two attempts both failed with `No such file or directory`. Two causes, both real. The file is committed **mode 644**, so `./scripts/phase7-preflight.sh` cannot execute; and README prescribes a bare `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh`, which is not a runnable command either. The correct invocation is `bash scripts/phase7-preflight.sh`. **The gate's purpose was met by a different route** — nothing modified `.env.sepolia` after 13:56, and all six immutables were afterwards verified directly in the `intent.toml` the apply actually consumed — but that is luck, not design. D-0067 Finding 8 committed this script specifically so a prescribed verification would be runnable; it was committed non-executable, so it was not. `AGENTS.md:100` is unsatisfied until the mode is fixed and the README command corrected.
- **Finding 2 — alerting suppression was N/A, and that is a recorded determination rather than an omission.** D-0067 Finding 4 named re-arming suppressed alerting as the most forgettable item in the whole sequence. It did not arise: nothing was running except ChainBank, so nothing was suppressed, so phases A and E of the execution run sheet were **legitimately skipped**. Recorded explicitly because a skipped phase and a forgotten phase are indistinguishable after the fact, and the next reset must not read this window's silence as precedent.
- **Finding 3 — the numbers nobody had ever measured.** Writers stopped ≈21:05Z; `op-deployer apply` completed **21:14:51Z**; both datadirs wiped by 21:40Z; Mac stack restarted 21:44Z; step 8 hash cross-check green by 21:50Z. **Outage ≈45 minutes** against an operator estimate of 1–2 hours. ADMIN spend **0.107813103668726942 ETH**, leaving 1.542 ETH against a 0.70 floor.
