Thirteen L1 proxies rotated; `AltDAChallengeProxy` and `L2OutputOracleProxy` stayed at the zero address, correct for a fault-proof deployment with AltDA disabled.
- **Finding 4 — step 8 matched first try.** Blocks 0, 1, 50, 100 and 200 carry identical hashes on the Mac sequencer and the Render replica. Genesis is `0xe242b1a3312b509e7df1496847f0bd0b115cb66676b1e973a355296c99e2386d` on both and in the `rollup.json` published to fortel2-replica at `bebddc3`. The one fault the run sheet said to watch for did not occur.
- **Finding 5 — the tracked `rollup.json` was stale, and the 8b gate would not have caught it.** `deployments/sepolia/rollup.json` survived the wipe (`DEPLOY_DIR` is `deployments/sepolia/.deployer`, so `rm -rf` never reached it) and still named the pre-wipe portal and system config. `.github/workflows/build-prestate.yml` defaults its `rollup-config` input to exactly that path. A prestate dispatched before the file was replaced would have committed to the **old** rollup config — and **the 8b(2) `cannon witness --input` gate does not cover this.** That gate proves the commitment matches the artifact built; it is silent on whether the config inside the artifact is the right one. A wrong-config prestate yields a valid, verifiable, wrong hash, and registration is once-only in both op-deployer's state check and on-chain (`SetDisputeGameImpl` requires `gameImpls(8) == address(0)`), so the recovery is a manual L1ProxyAdminOwner `setImplementation` or another network-wide wipe. **Committing the correct file before any prestate dispatch is the only control that exists.** Done in the same PR as this entry's v7 bump.
- **Finding 6 — drift instance seven: the PRD and the README number the same steps differently.** Across the three steps being executed. PRD: 1 announce, 2 stop writers, 3 redeploy, 4 send addresses. README: 2 announce, 3 stop writers, 4 redeploy. They realign at 5 and stay aligned through 8b. A consequence sat inside it: README step 2 says *"Immediately before step 3, run `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh`"*, and in README numbering step 3 is **stop writers**, placing the gate one step earlier than the irreversible spend it exists to guard. The same sentence is correct in PRD numbering. `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh` itself prints `clear to run step 2`, so the tooling speaks PRD.
- **Finding 7 — drift instance eight: the two runbooks disagree on the v7 trigger.** PRD step 9 gates the `rail-interface.json` bump on "after step 3 addresses are in git"; README step 8 gates it on "before calling the network healthy". The README's is stricter and was followed. Two independent numbering/gating disagreements found in a single window, in documents declared correct as of D-0067's commit two commits earlier.
- **Finding 8 — the replica runbook was incomplete in three ways, each of which cost time.** README step 6 says "every replica `/data`" and step 7 "all restart". In fact: **(a)** three Render services auto-deploy from the `fortel2-replica` repo — `fortel2-replica` (private, the only one with the 50 GB `/data` disk), `fortel2-replica-rpc` and `fortel2-sequencer-rpc` — while the runbook says "the replica" as though there were one. **(b)** A *restart* would not have worked: `Dockerfile:30-31` copies `config/genesis.json` and `config/rollup.json` into the image at build time, so only a **redeploy** picks up new artifacts. The operator reached the right action by having no restart button available; the next operator may find one. **(c)** The auto-deploy triggered by the config push is **guaranteed** to fail, because the push necessarily lands before the `/data` wipe — `dep-da519rk9` `update_failed` at 21:30:58Z, then the manual `dep-da51ee0u` `live` at 21:40:40Z. `entrypoint.sh:191` only runs `geth init` when `/data/geth` is absent, which is precisely why. Predictable and alarming; it should be predicted in the runbook rather than discovered.
- **Finding 9 — D-0016 is stale in a way that removes a constraint.** D-0016 records that no Mac-reachable `REPLICA_L2_RPC_URL` exists and that the sync drill must run from the Render Web Shell. The `fortel2-replica-rpc` gateway, created 2026-08-16 and therefore after that decision, answers `eth_chainId` `0x354` and `eth_blockNumber` from the mini. The step 8 cross-check in Finding 4 was performed through it. Web Shell remains the only route for **inspecting the Private Service itself**; it is no longer the only route for reading replica chain state.
- **Finding 10 — the first drift ever stopped by a check rather than by a person, and the model F7-12 should copy.** This entry's companion change was initially opened as **two** PRs, one bumping `rail-interface.json` to v7 and one committing `deployments/sepolia/deployments.json`. Both failed CI within twenty seconds, in opposite directions, naming each drifted field with both values: `scripts/rail-interface-check.sh` asserts that every `bridge.*` address equals its counterpart in `deployments.json`. **The two files are bound by an invariant and cannot move in separate commits.** Across eight recorded instances of documentation drift in this project, six were found after the fact and the last two by the Codex bot; **this is the first that could not merge.** It is also a working in-repo precedent: F7-12 does not need to be invented, it needs to be this script's pattern aimed at the five runbook gate locations instead of at two JSON files.
- **Finding 11 — 8b's signing prerequisite is verified on-chain, not assumed.** `ProxyAdmin.owner()` (via the portal's EIP-1967 admin slot, ProxyAdmin `0x2F6B90b7028C7b3C05BD268f10b8C30474cDB304`) and `SuperchainConfig.guardian()` both return **`0xBB3E19811B2c3423069B54BDFF3e90Dd8094bb0F`**, which is `ADMIN_ADDRESS`. `MakeRespected` calls `setRespectedGameType` and is Guardian-gated, so the second apply's requirement that the signer hold **both** roles is satisfied. `cannon` reports `cannon-8 → multithreaded64-5 (8)`, so the stateVersion 8 the MIPS64 VM requires is available for the witness gate. `cannon`, `kona-host` and `op-deployer` all exist but are **off PATH**, under `~/src/fortel2/`.
- **Finding 12 — the proposer is below its funding floor and nothing warned.** Post-restart, PROPOSER `0x350A0F7becCE56598962C501CaA02f900F256803` holds **0.0138 ETH against a 0.15 floor** (`sepolia-fund-check.sh` prints `NEED`), while posting a game every 5 minutes. Left alone it runs dry, the anchor stops advancing, and 8b's baseline degrades silently. Nothing in the reset sequence prompts a fund check after restart, and the roles that *were* checked before the window (ADMIN) are not the ones that spend continuously after it. HARVEST holds 2.45 ETH.
- **Finding 13 — no wallet addresses changed, which is worth stating because the instinct is that they did.** Every role address is derived from a private key in `.env.sepolia`, untouched by the wipe: ADMIN, BATCHER, PROPOSER, HARVEST, SEQUENCER, CHALLENGER, DEMO_A, DEMO_B are all identical to their pre-wipe values, and L1 balances are intact. **Only L2 balances went to zero**, since genesis carries no `fundDevAccounts` and L2 state was rewritten. No faucet run and no new treasury are required for recovery; refilling L2 is an L1→L2 deposit through the **new** portal `0xf8c7da6c009d5d05bb98f8cd8286b9b838a3b54e`.
- **Finding 14 — instance nine, in this entry, caught by the bot rather than the author.** The first draft of D-0068 declared US-071 and US-072 complete **in the decision log alone**. The binding `tasks/prd-phase-7-fault-proofs.md` still marked US-071 *In progress* and US-072 *Not started* and still instructed the operator to stop writers and run `FORCE_SEPOLIA_REDEPLOY=1`; `README.md` § Network reset procedure still presented those steps as pending. **An operator following the repository's declared authority could have wiped the network a second time.** Codex flagged it P1 on #117 against `AGENTS.md:127-130`. Two things make this the most serious instance recorded. **(a) The consequence is worse than its predecessors.** Instances one through eight left a reader *misinformed*; this one left the authoritative runbook actively instructing an irreversible destructive action that had already been performed. **(b) It occurred inside the commit whose entire subject is the previous eight instances**, written by an author who had just finished enumerating them and proposing F7-12 as the fix. D-0067 Finding 7 established that a discipline-based fix failed on first contact; this establishes that even *maximal* authorial attention to the exact failure mode does not prevent it. Nine instances, three now found by a bot, zero prevented by intention. Propagated in this commit to all five locations: the PRD operator-sequence rows 2–9 and both roadmap rows, a do-not-re-run banner above the PRD table and above the README procedure, README steps 3–8 struck through with their completion timestamps, `tasks/prd-l2-learning-chain.md:19` and `:51`, and the `.env.sepolia.example` pointer.
- **Decision:** **US-071 and US-072 steps 2–9 are complete.** Chain 852 is live on new L1 contracts, the replica derives the same chain, and `rail-interface.json` is at **v7** with the post-wipe proxies. Step 10 (SOS redeploy-or-adopt) is SOS-owned and outstanding. Step 8b is unstarted and deliberately deferred.
- **Consequence:** The next actions, in order. **(1)** Fund PROPOSER from HARVEST — Finding 12, and it is the only item with a clock on it. **(2)** Step 10, SOS-owned: SOS's `PaymentSettlement`, three `MockERC20`s and `TokenizedMMF` were L2 contracts and did not survive; the Render Secret File `deployments.fortel2-sepolia.json` now names dead addresses, and D-0036's cited transactions were at old-chain blocks 979,593 and 979,595 against a new chain currently under 2,000. **No SOS configuration changes** — `networkId`, chain 852, `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_READ_RPC_URL`, the Access write path and the Cloudflare tunnel all survive by design. **(3)** **F7-12 must land before step 8b**, per D-0067, now with *three* further instances (Findings 6, 7 and 14) and a working in-repo template (Finding 10). Finding 14 raises its priority above every other open item: it is the first instance whose failure mode is a repeated network-wide wipe rather than a misinformed reader, and it happened despite the author's undivided attention to precisely that risk. **(4)** Step 8b, not before F7-12 merges and not inside 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles; the second apply and the `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE=8` switch are **one unit of work**, because a proposer left on type 1 after the respected-type flip posts games that can neither prove a withdrawal nor advance the anchor, and `start_bg` returns 0 on a live pid so a plain re-run is a silent no-op. Still unwritten and not blockers: **F7-9** (D-0062), **F7-5** (D-0057), the `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` key/address pairing gap (D-0064 Finding 4) — about to be exercised for the first time by Finding 13's deposits — loader-level duplicate detection (D-0066 Finding 5), `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, and the mode-644 / README-command defects in Finding 1. Next free decision id is **D-0069**.

### D-0069 — **Step 10 done: SettlementOS settles again on 852. The old contract addresses are not dead — they are occupied by *different* contracts, and two of them invert token decimals**
- **Context:** US-071 step 10, SOS-owned, executed 2026-08-22 immediately after the wipe recorded in D-0068. SettlementOS's `PaymentSettlement`, three `MockERC20`s and `TokenizedMMF` were L2 contracts and did not survive the re-genesis. This entry records the recovery, the new addresses, and the two traps found doing it — both of which produce a confident success rather than an error.
- **Finding 1 — the settlement, and the pair that replaces D-0036's.** Payment `pay_75c2c0405867`, `onchainPaymentId` `0xd642196d4b763f84e2f27df4b9e6d5290ec33c370d511bdc6aaffc9a22826091`, `ent_acme_us` → `ent_tokyo_supplier`. **Escrow `0x1f75f25740ef25dfa094e2f8cb29143077ebe473fa2ae23cca5c002d08a54c96` (L2 block 4147)** and **settlement `0x4a17351c9f37a8c65e41837e8f4c1d9430ba89eea96103cfb6c1e4988166a5cc` (L2 block 4150)**, both `status=0x1`, both against the new `PaymentSettlement` at `0x57b4a70fef917a0a3de89427f5d6654be0181f04`. **Both blocks are final on L1** — confirmed once the finalized head passed them (4391 ≥ 4150); block 4147 is `0x7a4987fe3d33d051b9c057c13cc5b12b81a492250fe564eef0d8323cd3f226a9` and block 4150 is `0x5f08e3a2a679d89d0e32ac84c34c667a9b4a5732ccf3ca8868f04aad59574425`. **These supersede D-0036's cited pair** — escrow `0x48797d94…c3942b` at block 979,593 and settlement `0x876325b2…8045c7` at block 979,595 — which were on the pre-wipe chain and are unreachable. The new chain will not reach block 979,593 for roughly three weeks. The escrow log's `from`/`to` topics are the post-reseed entity wallets, which is independent confirmation the wallet re-seed took.
- **Finding 2 — the nonce shift moved every contract one slot along, so old addresses are occupied rather than empty.** The recovery deposit consumed deployer nonce 0 before the redeploy, so the `CREATE` sequence started one later than it had pre-wipe. The consequence is not the expected "old addresses are dead": three of them are **live contracts of a different type**, verified on 852 by `symbol()` and `decimals()`. `0x9d8b8b7c…56aa` was `PaymentSettlement` and is now **mockSGD**; `0x7d7b168c…7e63` was **mockJPY (0dp)** and is now **mockUSDC (6dp)**; `0x0b6fa033…691d` was **mockSGD (6dp)** and is now **mockJPY (0dp)**. The first fails loudly on an ABI mismatch. **The other two do not fail at all** — a stale reference reads a real ERC-20 and returns an amount wrong by a factor of 10^6. `rail-interface.json`'s reset policy warns that a re-genesis *expires* addresses; that framing is now incomplete and understates the hazard for any consumer that caches by address.
- **Finding 3 — the deploy script's mode selection is overlay-driven with no on-chain check, and produced a silent no-op.** `decideDeployMode()` reads `chain/deployments.<network>.json` and returned `noop` — "TokenizedMMF already present in overlay" — against a pre-wipe overlay naming five contracts that are all empty code on 852. It printed `Nothing to do` and sent no transactions, which is indistinguishable from success. `--force-full-deploy` does **not** override it: that flag only bypasses `assertAdoptableFullDeployAllowed`, which returns early for `fortel2-sepolia` since it is absent from `ADOPTABLE_NETWORKS`. The only lever is removing the overlay from the script's view. Note the asymmetry that makes this a defect rather than a quirk: **the `--adopt` path fetches bytecode for every registered address and aborts on empty code; the `noop` path checks nothing.** One `eth_getCode` on the same data would have turned a confident no-op into a loud failure. Recorded as a follow-up against `settlementos`.
- **Finding 4 — a planner error caught by the operator's own preflight, worth recording because the correction was the mistake.** The stale-overlay no-op was predicted, then talked away: `decideDeployMode()` was tested against the top level of the overlay file, which nests everything under `networks["fortel2-sepolia"]`, so the hand-rolled check returned `full` and the original warning was withdrawn as a false alarm. The operator ran `--preflight-only` — the tool that exists to answer exactly this — and it reported `noop`. **A hand-rolled restatement of a check is not the check**, which is the same failure D-0067 Finding 6 recorded for the pre-flight's extraction anchors, now in a second form.
- **Finding 5 — the explorer was mislabelling chain 852 and is re-keyed (settlementos-explorer#58).** `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_DEPLOYMENT.contracts` aliased `SHARED_CONTRACTS`, correct only while ForteL2 shared Base Sepolia's CREATE addresses. Fixed the way `address-book.ts` itself prescribes — a new `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_CONTRACTS` object, `SHARED_CONTRACTS` untouched. Six tests encoded the old sharing and were changed, each declared: two became **stronger** (`getEscrowAddress('fortel2-sepolia')` now also asserts `.not.toBe(PAYMENT_SETTLEMENT_ADDRESS)`, and the alias test now asserts ForteL2 does *not* re-alias). The `0xAEd29` prefix-collision test lost its premise — the collision was real pre-wipe and the re-key moved both addresses — so the distinctness guard was kept and only the prefix assertions dropped, rather than deleting a regression test that had become inconvenient. The pinned value-freeze was regenerated only after diffing rendered output against it: **31 rows, 21 unchanged, every changed row `fortel2-sepolia`**, and the final diff contains zero lines mentioning `base-sepolia` or `polygon-amoy`. Suite: **32 files / 226 tests green**, against a baseline of `1 failed | 31 passed` — `address-book.chain.test.ts` checks the book against the live chain and this fixes its 2 failures.
- **Finding 6 — the `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing gap (D-0064 Finding 4) was exercised for the first time, and the failure signature is exactly what makes it dangerous.** The recovery deposit was sent from an external wallet rather than the SOS deployer. `OptimismPortal.receive()` credits `msg.sender`, so the ETH minted on L2 to the sending wallet and the deployer stayed at zero: **a successful transaction and a zero balance, with nothing to point at the cause.** No funds were lost. Before the corrective deposit the key was paired against its address by hand — the four-line check F7-10 added to `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` — and it matched. That check still does not exist in `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh`.
- **Finding 7 — what did *not* need changing, verified rather than assumed.** SettlementOS has **no functional portal or bridge address constant** — grep across `*.ts`, `*.tsx`, `*.mjs`, `*.js`, `*.json` found only operator-facing help text. Runtime config is env-driven and every variable survives: `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_RPC_URL`, `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_READ_RPC_URL`, the Cloudflare tunnel, the Access app and policy. The database stores token **symbols**, not addresses (`asset String // token symbol`), so Finding 2's reassignment cannot reach SettlementOS's own data — it was confined to the explorer's address book. **No wallet addresses changed**: every role address derives from a key the wipe never touched, L1 balances were intact throughout, and only L2 balances zeroed. No faucet run and no new treasury were required, contrary to the initial assumption.
- **Decision:** **Step 10 is complete.** Chain 852 carries a settled payment end to end on redeployed contracts, the explorer labels it correctly, and `rail-interface.json` v7 is live. New addresses of record: `PaymentSettlement` `0x57b4a70fef917a0a3de89427f5d6654be0181f04`, `TokenizedMMF` `0x24f439f535e1484e1be044d8b079774f6dacb6c9`, `mockUSDC` `0x7d7b168cfab3dba1afc41f6160e886ffe9997e63` (6dp), `mockJPY` `0x0b6fa033c034d694e876b56f2dd8377a2be5691d` (0dp), `mockSGD` `0x9d8b8b7c476ab02306046f3da719d380fa0456aa` (6dp), treasury `0x3bE68C872f0FCB5d0b204eCeAFFff8C04F4c9E4E`.
- **Consequence:** With D-0068, the whole post-wipe sequence is closed except **step 8b**, which remains blocked on **F7-12** (dispatched). Open follow-ups, none blocking: the `deepmerge-ts` CVE-2026-40345 override, which currently fails Trivy and patchhog on **every** `settlementos` PR and is transitive via `@prisma/config`; an `eth_getCode` verification in `decideDeployMode` (Finding 3); the `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check (Finding 6); and `rail-interface.json`'s reset-policy wording, which says a re-genesis *expires* addresses and should say they may be *reassigned* (Finding 2). Still carried from D-0068: **F7-9**, **F7-5**, loader-level duplicate detection, `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, and the mode-644 / README-command defects on that script. Next free decision id is **D-0070**.

### D-0070 — **F7-12 merged: drift is now a merge-blocking check. It caught nothing on its first day, and instance ten happened anyway in a line it does not cover**
- **Context:** F7-12 landed as #118 after D-0067 made it a hard precondition for step 8b and D-0068 Finding 10 gave it a working in-repo template. This entry records what the check actually protects, the convention it imposes, the drift instance that occurred alongside it, and the standing fix-list — which is written here because it has now been carried verbally across several sessions and that is exactly how items get lost.
- **Finding 1 — what the check is, and the evidence it works.** `scripts/phase7-gate-parity.sh` reads declared facts from `tasks/phase7-gates.json` and asserts the five runbook locations agree: **completeness** for `tasks/prd-phase-7-fault-proofs.md` § Operator sequence and `README.md` § Network reset procedure; **non-contradiction** for `.env.sepolia.example`, the `prd-l2-learning-chain.md` glossary bullet and its Phase 7 roadmap row. Wired into the same CI job as `rail-interface-check.sh`, so it blocks merges. Reviewed with nine independent probes, all of which went red correctly: deleting README's executed-window banner (**drift instance nine's exact shape**), changing a notice timestamp in one location only, a deleted `phase7-gates.json`, a malformed one, an undeclared step, an imperative second-wipe instruction in a summary location the worker had not tested, and an undeclared `F7-N` likewise. **It fails closed when it cannot run** — missing file, unparseable JSON, or missing heading anchor — which is precisely the defect `phase7-preflight.sh` shipped in its first draft (D-0067 Finding 6) and the single most important property here. Gates reproduced independently: 60 PASS parity, 20 rail-interface, `test-helpers.sh` 189 → **194** (delta exactly +5).
- **Finding 2 — the convention the check imposes, which is load-bearing and was undocumented.** Step markers key on the **What** column of the PRD table, which is correct: the action is invariant and the status is not. The consequence is that marking a step complete **must** go in the *When* column. #117 did this for steps 2–9 by accident of style; #119 did not, rewriting step 10's What column and breaking the `sos-adopt` marker. The fix belonged in #119, not #118 — the worker's markers were correct as authored. **Steps 11–13 will each break the check as they complete unless the convention is followed**, and `phase7-gates.json` does not state it. Recorded as a fix-list item rather than left to be rediscovered three times.
- **Finding 3 — instance ten, in the same window, in a line the new check does not cover.** #119 propagated step 10's completion to the PRD operator sequence, the PRD roadmap, the PRD status banner and the learning-chain row — **but not to README**, whose Outstanding banner still named the SOS recovery, and whose SOS-facing prose still said a re-genesis merely *expires* addresses. Caught by Codex, not by the author or by F7-12. Two things worth separating. **(a) The cause was a bad search.** README was checked for a *numbered* step 10, found to have none, and declared unaffected; it has no numbered step 10 but does have a banner naming it. `AGENTS.md:132` says to search by **concept**, not by structure, and this was a structural search. **(b) F7-12 could not have caught it.** Its declared README banner fact is the "Steps 1 through 8 are complete" sentence, not the Outstanding sentence, and it has no notion of address semantics. That is not a defect in F7-12 — it is scoped to operator-sequence parity — but it marks the boundary of what the gate protects, and the boundary is narrower than "documentation drift". Fixed in #120.
- **Finding 3a — instance eleven, introduced by the fix for instance ten, in the same pull request.** #120's first draft replaced README's Outstanding banner with "**step 8b** only", while the PRD and the learning-chain roadmap both list step 8b **and steps 11–13**. The operator-facing mirror therefore implied the valid-game check, the deliberate bad proposal and the closeout were done. It also still described 8b as "blocked on F7-12" after #118 had merged. Caught by Codex on the same pull request, again not by the author and again not by F7-12 — the Outstanding sentence is not a declared fact. A second finding on the same commit corrected a factual overgeneralisation: the draft said an old address is "usually" occupied by a *different* contract, when repeating the `CREATE` sequence exactly reproduces the **same** contracts and reassignment follows only from a shifted nonce or order. The corrected text states the condition and tells the reader to revalidate code, type and decimals rather than assume. **Two instances in two consecutive commits, both while actively trying to prevent them, is the strongest available evidence for Finding 4's boundary.**
- **Finding 4 — what F7-12 does not cover, stated so nobody over-trusts the green.** It verifies the runbooks agree *with each other and with a declared file*. It cannot verify the declared file matches reality: if `phase7-gates.json` goes stale, every check passes while every document is wrong. This is the correct scope — all eleven recorded instances were propagation failures, not first-write errors — but the green means "these documents are consistent", not "these documents are true". Silencing it turned out to be harder than the design guaranteed: editing one declared field to match a drifted document still left a failure, so capitulation requires deliberate multi-field edits rather than one line. `F7-N` open/closed status vocabulary remains free prose and is still unchecked (deferred by the brief).
- **Decision:** **F7-12 is closed and step 8b is unblocked.** D-0067's precondition is satisfied. Nothing else stands between the current state and registering the type-8 game.
- **The standing fix-list.** None of these block step 8b. They are written down here because they have been carried conversationally and that is how items are lost. In priority order:
  1. **`deepmerge-ts` CVE-2026-40345** — HIGH, transitive via `@prisma/config` which pins `7.1.5` exactly, fixed in `8.0.0`. Pre-existing on `settlementos` `main` and **currently fails Trivy and patchhog on every PR in that repo**, which is the real cost; actual exposure is low (stack exhaustion merging recursive object graphs, reached only by local trusted config). The repo already uses an `overrides` block for exactly this pattern. A major-version override on the Prisma config loader needs a real install and test run, not a blind bump.
  2. **`decideDeployMode` performs no on-chain verification** (`settlementos`) — D-0069 Finding 3. It returned `noop` against five addresses that are empty code on 852 and printed `Nothing to do`. The asymmetry that makes it a defect rather than a quirk: the `--adopt` path bytecode-verifies every registered address and aborts on empty code; the `noop` path checks nothing. One `eth_getCode` closes it.
  3. **`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` has no key/address pairing check** — D-0064 Finding 4, exercised for real in D-0069 Finding 6. The failure signature is a *successful* transaction and a zero balance, with nothing to point at the cause. The four-line check F7-10 added to `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh` is the fix.
  4. **`rail-interface.json` `resetPolicy` says a re-genesis "expires" addresses** — incomplete per D-0069 Finding 2, and this is the machine-readable copy SettlementOS consumes. Correcting it is a **version-bump decision** under that file's own `$schema_note`, and bumping to v8 hours after v7 is an operator call. **This is the only fix-list item that needs a human decision before it can be done.**
  5. **`phase7-gates.json` should state the What/When marker convention** — Finding 2. Cheap, and it prevents steps 11–13 each rediscovering it.
  6. Carried from D-0068 and earlier, unchanged: **F7-9** (negative-control prestate build, D-0062), **F7-5** (preflight prestate-commitment check, D-0057), loader-level duplicate detection in `lib.sh` (D-0066 Finding 5), `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh`, and that script's **mode 644** plus README's non-runnable bare-path invocation (D-0068 Finding 1).
- **Consequence:** The next action is **step 8b**, per README § Network reset procedure and the PRD Operator sequence — not before F7-12's merge (done) and not inside the nightly 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles window. Its four parts are one unit of work: commit the post-wipe `rollup.json` (already on `main` via #115), build the Kona prestate in CI from it, verify with `cannon witness --input` as a **gate**, then the second apply and the `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE=8` switch **together** — a proposer left on type 1 after the respected-game-type flip posts games that can neither prove a withdrawal nor advance the anchor, and `start_bg` returns 0 on a live pid so a plain re-run is a silent no-op. Both signing roles were verified on-chain in D-0068 Finding 11. Next free decision id is **D-0071**.

### D-0071 — **Fix-list execution: two items closed, item 4 decided by the operator, and item 2 turns out to need a different shape than "add a check to `decideDeployMode`"**
- **Context:** D-0070 wrote the standing fix-list down precisely so it would stop being carried conversationally. This records what has been executed against it, one operator decision that unblocks an item, and a design constraint found while scoping the next item — which changes the work enough to be worth recording before it is attempted.
- **Finding 1 — item 1 closed (settlementos#89). The obvious safer fix was not available.** `deepmerge-ts` 7.1.6 exists and looked like a patch-level escape from a major bump, but GHSA-ggr8-5vv4-36mx gives the vulnerable range as `< 8.0.0`; 7.1.6 is **not** patched. Overridden to `^8.0.2` using the repo's existing `overrides` block. Of 8.0.0's four breaking changes, only Map-value deep-merging is runtime-relevant, and `@prisma/config` constructs no `Map` and calls only the plain `deepmerge` across three sites; the two type renames are compile-time and it ships compiled JS. Verified with a real install rather than a version bump on faith: `npx prisma generate` exit 0, suite 73 files / 636 tests green, lockfile churn exactly one package. **Trivy passes for the first time in this cycle**, which was the actual cost — the vulnerability itself is stack exhaustion reached only through local trusted config, but it was gating every pull request in that repository.
- **Finding 2 — the old ForteL2 payments were *not* deleted, and the number that settled it.** The operator asked to remove the 10 pre-wipe `fortel2-sepolia` payments so a demo click could not land on a dead explorer link. Counted against production first: **120 of 146 audit-chain events hang off those 10 payments**, plus 70 compliance checks, 10 ledger credits, 10 reservations. `AuditEvent` is an append-only hash chain whose hash covers `paymentId`, and `AuditCheckpoint` signs the tip with `AUDIT_ANCHOR_KEY`, which the database does not hold. A delete fails on three required foreign keys, and forcing past them would null `paymentId` on 82% of the log and make `verifyAuditChain()` return `valid: false` permanently and unrepairably. The schema states the intent itself: *"a dangling anchor means events were deleted, and reporting that is the point."* **The records are also not wrong** — those payments settled, on a chain wiped on schedule with notice.
- **Finding 3 — the fix instead (settlementos#90), and where it was initially incomplete.** `explorerTxUrl()` turned out to be a single choke point every call site funnels through, and `Hash` already renders plain text for a falsy `href`, so suppressing a dead link needed no new rendering path. Added `FORTEL2_SEPOLIA_REGENESIS_AT` (`2026-08-22T21:14:48Z`, taken from the post-wipe `rollup.json` `genesis.l2_time`, not hand-typed) and `isSupersededByRegenesis()`; the third argument is optional so callers that pass nothing are unaffected. **Cursor Bugbot caught the incomplete half**: the explanatory `note` was wired at the two list call sites only, leaving the three `Hash` calls on payment detail and one in the stuck repair list rendering deliberately-dead hashes with no explanation — the same "reads as broken" failure, relocated to the page a demo is *more* likely to open. Now wired at all of them, with the bridge-payout row correctly evaluating `destinationNetwork` rather than `sourceNetwork`, since a cross-chain payment can have a superseded leg on either side. Suite 73 files / 643 tests green.
- **Finding 4 — operator decision: the `rail-interface.json` wording correction does *not* warrant a version bump.** D-0069 Finding 2 established that `resetPolicy`'s "a re-genesis **expires** every ForteL2 address" understates the hazard, because an old address is frequently *reassigned* rather than emptied. D-0070 listed correcting it as fix-list item 4 and flagged the version question as the one item needing a human call: the file's own `$schema_note` says the version bumps when "chain ID, bridge proxies, reset policy, or the reachability of the read/write paths change". **Operator decision, 2026-08-23: no bump.** The reasoning is that the reset *policy* has not changed — only its description has become more accurate — and a v8 four hours after v7 would signal an event to SettlementOS that did not occur, which is precisely the false-event failure the `$schema_note` exists to prevent. **Item 4 is therefore unblocked and is now an ordinary prose edit**, leaving `version` at `7`. The README half of this correction already landed in #120.
- **Finding 5 — item 2 is not "add a check to `decideDeployMode`", and attempting it that way would break a deliberate contract.** `decideDeployMode()` is documented *"(pure — no I/O)"* and its unit tests depend on that; putting an `eth_getCode` call inside it would make a pure decision function async and network-dependent. The verification has to sit **at the call site in `main()`**, after the mode is resolved and **before the `--preflight-only` early return**, so that a dry run reports the same finding a real run would. There is an in-repo precedent to copy rather than invent: the `--adopt` path already runs a bytecode gate over every registered address and aborts on empty code, using `publicClient.getBytecode()`. The asymmetry recorded in D-0069 Finding 3 — adopt verifies, `noop` does not — is closed by giving the non-adopt paths the same gate. **One design question is deliberately left open for whoever implements it: on detecting recorded-but-absent contracts, fail loudly or silently escalate the mode to `full`?** The recommendation is **fail loudly** — auto-escalating turns a safety check into an unrequested deployment, and this project's established pattern is fail-closed with a message naming what was found.
- **Decision:** Fix-list items **1** and **4** are closed and decided respectively; the pre-re-genesis link hazard is fixed in both SettlementOS surfaces and the explorer. No pull request is open in any of the three repositories.
- **Consequence:** The next action is fix-list **item 2**, in `settlementos`, shaped per Finding 5. Then item 3 (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check, ForteL2), item 4 (now an ordinary prose edit, no bump), item 5 (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention), and item 6's carried set. **None of these block step 8b**, which is unblocked as of D-0070 and waits only on the operator's window. Two follow-ups opened by this entry and not yet on the list: the explorer still returns a bare 404 for a pre-re-genesis hash typed directly, where a "this chain was re-genesised on 2026-08-22" page would be friendlier; and hiding rather than merely unlinking superseded payments was deliberately not done, on the grounds that hiding records is adjacent to the deletion Finding 2 argues against. Next free decision id is **D-0072**.

### D-0072 — **Item 2 rescoped a second time on contact with the code: the bytecode gate D-0071 specified would pass three of the five stale addresses, because the re-genesis reoccupied them**
- **Context:** D-0071 Finding 5 rescoped fix-list item 2 from "add a check to `decideDeployMode`" to "copy the `--adopt` path's bytecode gate to the call site in `main()`", and left one design question open. Scoping the task against `settlementos@513352a` to write the worker brief produced a second rescope, two settled operator decisions, and drift instance twelve. This entry is written **before** the work comes back, for the same reason D-0071 was written before item 2 was attempted: the findings are what make the task correct, and they are worthless if they arrive with the review.
- **Finding 1 — bytecode presence is not identity, and D-0069 already contains the proof.** D-0071 Finding 5 specified the gate as a copy of the adopt path's `publicClient.getBytecode()` check, aborting on empty code. That closes the D-0069 Finding 3 case exactly as recorded — five overlay addresses, all empty code on 852, a `noop` and `Nothing to do`. It does **not** close D-0069's headline. Finding 2 of that entry records that the recovery deposit consumed deployer nonce 0, shifting the `CREATE` sequence one slot, so the old addresses are *occupied* rather than empty. Cross-referencing Finding 2's reassignment list against the same entry's Decision line — the new addresses of record — confirms all three, and they are the same addresses on both sides:

  | Stale overlay entry | Address | Now holds |
  |---|---|---|
  | `PaymentSettlement` | `0x9d8b8b7c476ab02306046f3da719d380fa0456aa` | `mockSGD` (6dp) |
  | `mockJPY` (0dp) | `0x7d7b168cfab3dba1afc41f6160e886ffe9997e63` | `mockUSDC` (6dp) |
  | `mockSGD` (6dp) | `0x0b6fa033c034d694e876b56f2dd8377a2be5691d` | `mockJPY` (0dp) |

  **Those three return non-empty bytecode and a presence-only gate passes them.** Three of the five stale entries, in today's live state. The overall run would still abort on the remaining two, so the specified gate is not useless — but it would abort for the wrong reason and report the wrong finding, and against any overlay whose empty entries had also been reoccupied it would pass clean. D-0069 Finding 2 states the consequence plainly: the first of the three fails loudly on an ABI mismatch, "the other two do not fail at all — a stale reference reads a real ERC-20 and returns an amount wrong by a factor of 10^6."
- **Finding 2 — the strong check was already named in the same finding, and the data to run it is already recorded.** D-0069 Finding 2 verified the reassignments on 852 "by `symbol()` and `decimals()`". The overlay slice and `ADOPTABLE_NETWORKS` both already store `decimals` per token (`mockUSDC: { address, decimals: 6 }`), so an identity check needs no new source of truth: assert the on-chain `decimals()` equals the recorded value. Symbol is **not** available the same way — the overlay's key is the label (`mockUSDC`), not the on-chain symbol — so a symbol check would require inventing a source of truth and was left out. **Operator decision, 2026-08-23: the gate checks presence *and* decimals.** The residual gap is stated rather than closed: `PaymentSettlement` and `TokenizedMMF` carry no recorded `decimals`, so they remain presence-checked only, and a reassigned `PaymentSettlement` that happens to hold code still passes.
- **Finding 3 — the design question D-0071 left open is decided, and a mechanical fact it did not have removes the alternative.** D-0071 Finding 5 asked: on finding recorded-but-absent contracts, fail loudly or silently escalate the mode to `full`? **Operator decision, 2026-08-23: fail loudly**, per that entry's own recommendation — auto-escalating turns a safety check into an unrequested deployment that spends gas and mints new addresses. A second decision was needed that D-0071 did not anticipate: whether `--force-full-deploy` should bypass the new gate. It should not, and the reason is mechanical rather than a matter of taste. **The flag does not change the resolved mode.** It only returns early from `assertAdoptableFullDeployAllowed`; `decideDeployMode()` still returns `noop`, so a stale overlay stays `noop` with the flag set. This is consistent with D-0069 Finding 3's observation that the flag "does not override it", and it means there is no existing bypass to preserve — wiring one would be new behaviour. **The single operator remedy is therefore moving the overlay aside**, which is the same remedy `assertAdoptableFullDeployAllowed` already prints, so the new abort message reuses its phrasing rather than offering a second one.
- **Finding 4 — drift instance twelve, in a third location and a different repository.** `AGENTS.md:551` in `settlementos` — the "Where addresses come from, and when they change" table, ForteL2 re-genesis row — says "Every ForteL2 contract address above **expires**, including the backed-up overlay's contract entries." That is the identical understatement D-0069 Finding 2 identified in `rail-interface.json` and that fix-list item 4 exists to correct. Item 4 named only `rail-interface.json`; the README half landed in #120. **Item 4 is therefore a two-repository prose edit**, not a one-file one, and `version` still stays at `7` per D-0071 Finding 4. Two things are worth recording about how this was found. **F7-12 does not and cannot cover it**: the parity gate asserts agreement across five ForteL2 locations and has no visibility into another repository at all — a reminder that it protects operator-sequence parity, not documentation drift generally. And it was found by searching **by concept** ("expires") rather than by structure, which is the method D-0070 Finding 2 prescribed after instance ten was missed by grepping for a numbered step that existed only as a banner. The method worked; the coverage assumption did not.
- **Finding 5 — the task id is `T8`, and the numbering is a trap worth writing down.** `settlementos` uses a `T`-series for worker tasks, and a reader deriving "highest plus one" gets it wrong twice over. `T1`–`T7` are consumed — `T7` completed 2026-08-07 as PR #46 (`CLAUDE.md:203`) — but the worker-prompts file stops at `T6`, so the file understates the high-water mark. A parallel `J1`–`J9` series in `tasks/hosting-worker-plan.md` is separately fully consumed. **`T8` is allocated to this task** and the brief states it overrides any derive-it-yourself convention.
- **Finding 6 — one worker prompt was untracked, and the way the *wrong* count was reached is the more useful half.** `tasks/worker-prompts/T8-deploy-overlay-onchain-gate.md` existed only in the operator's working tree and is committed by this entry's own change set, closing for it the defect D-0067 Finding 8 recorded as "the untracked pre-flight": a file that governs work but is not in the repository cannot be reviewed, cannot be cited by a reviewer, and disappears with the checkout. **`F7-12-gate-parity-check.md` was initially recorded here as untracked too, and was not** — it landed in `761c915` ("docs: propagate step 10 completion to the runbooks"), and the blob built for it matched `main` byte for byte, which is how the error surfaced. The claim came from a session-start `git status` snapshot taken while the local checkout was at `1713a7e`, six commits behind `main` at `6abdc1c`. **A stale working tree is not the repository**, and this is the same class of error as D-0069 Finding 4 and D-0067 Finding 6 — a restatement of the state substituted for the state. The instruction the T8 brief gives its worker, *trust the repository over this brief*, applies with no less force to the planner writing it.
- **Decision:** **Fix-list item 2 is dispatch-ready as `T8`, in `settlementos`, and carries no open decisions.** The gate verifies presence **and** decimals-identity for every recorded contract in a non-adopt run, sits at the call site in `main()` after mode resolution and **before** the `--preflight-only` early return, and preserves `decideDeployMode()`'s documented `(pure — no I/O)` contract — the constraint D-0071 Finding 5 established and the reason this is not a change to that function. **Fix-list item 4 is expanded** to cover `AGENTS.md:551` in `settlementos` alongside `rail-interface.json`, so both prose corrections land together with consistent wording.
- **Consequence:** The next action is dispatching **T8**; it does not block and is not blocked by **step 8b**, which remains unblocked as of D-0070 and waits only on the operator's window. Remaining fix-list, unchanged in substance: **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check, ForteL2), **4** (now two repositories, no version bump), **5** (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention), and **6**'s carried set — F7-9, F7-5, loader-level duplicate detection, `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, and that script's mode 644 plus README's non-runnable bare-path invocation. Item **7** is not needed — Finding 6's untracked prompt is committed by this entry's own change set. Carried follow-ups from D-0071, still not on the list: the explorer's bare 404 for a pre-re-genesis hash, and superseded payments being unlinked but not hidden. Next free decision id is **D-0073**.

### D-0073 — **T8 verified and merged; the review found a booby trap and a hole the gate cannot close. Then the planner dispatched a brief containing a false claim, and it degraded the work it was meant to guide**
- **Context:** T8 (fix-list item 2) came back as settlementos#91 and was reviewed against its invariants rather than its own test suite. Item 4 was then dispatched as R-11/T9 and came back as ForteL2#123 and settlementos#92. This entry records what the review verified, two findings it produced, and a planner error that reached a dispatched brief and from there reached two pull requests. The error is written up at length because its shape has now recurred three times and the previous two write-ups did not stop it.
- **Finding 1 — T8 verified independently; every claimed number reproduced.** Re-run in an isolated clone at `58e6e71`, never in the operator's checkout: `tsc --noEmit` and `lint` clean, targeted file **67 tests**, full suite **73 files / 652 tests**, and the base at `513352a` **73 / 643** — delta exactly **+9**, as claimed. Scope was the four permitted files; the docs touches were append-only (0 deletions) and the fenced-off `AGENTS.md` re-genesis row was untouched (`grep -c expires` on the diff returned 0). The test file was **+166/−0**, so `EXISTING CHECKS MODIFIED: none` is structurally confirmed rather than taken on trust. The gate sits where the brief required — inside the non-adopt branch, after mode resolution, before the `--preflight-only` early return — and `decideDeployMode()` remains pure and synchronous. **Probed rather than read:** a reassigned token with code present and decimals 0→6 aborts and names both values; `decimals: 0` survives extraction as `expectedDecimals === 0` and still aborts against an on-chain 6, which is the case most likely to have been lost to a truthy check and is precisely `mockJPY`, one of the two tokens that fail silently; three offenders are all named in one message; a null overlay leaves the gate silent; `--preflight-only` verifies while sending nothing. The worker also exceeded the brief by extracting `planNonAdoptExecution` as a pure function, making the ordering property assertable instead of merely inspectable.
- **Finding 2 — the adopt path was protected only by a destructure, and nothing encoded why.** `listAdoptContractAddresses()` now emits `expectedDecimals` for every token on **both** paths. The adopt call site survives only because it destructures the field away (`for (const { label, address } of toCheck)`). Rewritten as the spread form a later edit would naturally reach for, `--adopt` aborts on every token with `decimals unreadable` — measured, not reasoned — on the one path whose purpose is recovering a network whose overlay was lost. Current behaviour was correct; the guarantee was unencoded. Closed by T9's additive regression test (settlementos#92), which asserts the real call-site shape passes and names what it protects.
- **Finding 3 — the gate cannot detect a reassignment between two tokens of equal decimals, and the live deploy contains such a pair.** Identity is checked by comparing on-chain `decimals()` against the overlay's recorded value. **`mockUSDC` and `mockSGD` are both 6dp**, so a swap between those two passes the gate clean — verified by probe. D-0069's actual nonce shift happened to move `mockJPY` (0dp) and `mockSGD` (6dp), which the gate does catch, so the event that motivated T8 is covered; the class is not. Closing it needs a `symbol()` check and a new source of truth for expected symbols, since the overlay stores the label as its key rather than the on-chain symbol. **Left open deliberately.** Documented in `AGENTS.md` beside the gate it qualifies, which is where a reader meets the gate.
- **Finding 4 — the planner asserted a fact from truncated grep output, wrote it into a dispatched brief, and made the resulting work worse.** D-0071 Finding 4 recorded that the README half of the item-4 correction had already landed in #120. **That was true.** README carries the canonical treatment: *"Expired does not mean empty… repeat it exactly and the old addresses come back holding the same contracts; shift it… and they come back holding different ones. Either way an old address may be live, so revalidate code, type and decimals rather than assuming it is dead."* While scoping item 4, the planner searched README by concept, piped the result through `cut -c1-200`, saw only an unrelated fragment of an enormous line, and concluded README had never made the claim. On that basis D-0071 was declared wrong, and the R-11/T9 brief carried a prominent section headed *"A correction to the record you will otherwise trip over"* instructing the worker that **no README precedent existed and not to look for one.** The worker complied exactly. Deprived of the canonical phrasing, it invented new wording and folded it into the middle of an existing parallel list in `rail-interface.json` — the machine-readable string SettlementOS consumes — turning *"every bridge proxy is new, every address is expired, cited tx hashes are dead, and the rows must be rebuilt"* into a run-on, and orphaning *"including the backed-up overlay's contract entries"* in `AGENTS.md`. **Facts survived; structure did not.** Corrected by the planner directly on both branches rather than sent back, since the wrong premise was the planner's; both commits say so and the worker's regression test was left untouched because it was right. **The shape is the same as D-0069 Finding 4 and D-0067 Finding 6 — a restatement of the evidence substituted for the evidence — and this is its third occurrence.** Two properties are new and worth naming. It survived one deliberate check: the operator was told about the "correction" in conversation and it went unchallenged, because it was plausible. And **the blast radius was larger than a wrong belief** — a false claim inside a brief is executed by a worker who has been told to trust it, so the planner's error arrives as the worker's output. The rule that follows: **a claim that a document does *not* say something must be verified against untruncated output, and a brief that contradicts a decision-log entry must re-read that entry's cited evidence before overriding it.**
- **Finding 5 — item 4's real scope was four locations, not the two D-0072 recorded.** README (done, #120), `deployments/rail-interface.json` `resetPolicy`, `tasks/coordination-settlementos.md` (the SOS-facing coordination row, found only by concept-search and named in no prior entry), and `settlementos` `AGENTS.md`. D-0072 said "a two-repository prose edit" and named two files; the count was low because the coordination row had not been found and README had been wrongly reclassified as outstanding. The three remaining locations are corrected in ForteL2#123 and settlementos#92, all now phrased to match README rather than to compete with it. **`version` stays `7`** per D-0071 Finding 4.
- **Decision:** **T8 is merged (settlementos#91) and fix-list item 2 is closed.** Item 4 is complete pending review of **ForteL2#123** and **settlementos#92**. Finding 2 is closed by T9's test. **Finding 3 is accepted and left open** — the gate is a strict improvement, not a closure, and the residual is documented where the gate is met rather than only in this log.
- **Consequence:** The next actions are the operator's: review and merge **#123** and **#92**. Remaining fix-list: **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check, ForteL2 — still the four-line check F7-10 added to `02-deploy-contracts-sepolia.sh`), **5** (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention), and **6**'s carried set — F7-9, F7-5, loader-level duplicate detection, `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, and that script's mode 644 plus README's non-runnable bare-path invocation. **Item 5 should land before steps 11–13**, since each of those steps breaks `phase7-gate-parity.sh` as it completes and the marker convention is what prevents three avoidable red gates. **Step 8b remains unblocked and waits only on the operator's window**, outside 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles. Open follow-ups not on the list: Finding 3's equal-decimals hole, the explorer's bare 404 for a pre-re-genesis hash, and superseded payments being unlinked but not hidden. Next free decision id is **D-0074**.

### D-0074 — **The proposer was not burning 23 ETH/day. It was posting a recoverable bond, and nobody had ever collected one. The funding crisis was a bookkeeping error about who holds the money**
- **Context:** The operator raised, on 2026-08-23, that the proposer was consuming **0.08 ETH every 5 minutes** — ~23 ETH/day against a realistic faucet supply of ~0.3 ETH/day and a ChainBank reconciler capable of at most ~2.4 ETH/day. It had already run dry. Three tasks followed: **R-12** (diagnosis, ForteL2#125), **R-13** (recovery proof, #126) and **R-14** (recovery tool + execution, #127). This entry records what the money actually was, three planner errors the work exposed, and the operating decisions taken. It is written while R-14's withdrawal legs are still outstanding, and says so where that matters.
- **Finding 1 — the drain was the type-1 init bond, not gas, and the brief that dispatched R-12 asserted the opposite.** The proposer runs `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE=1`. `initBonds(1) = 80000000000000000` (0.08 ETH), and that is the `value` on every `create()` — verified independently to the wei, along with `gameCount`, the DelayedWETH balance and a sampled receipt. **Gas is ~1.3% of the drain.** The R-12 brief stated "type-8 games carry zero bond, so this isn't bond cost." `initBonds(8) = 0` is true, but the proposer is not on type 8, and carrying that claim across to the configured game type would have produced a confident wrong diagnosis. The worker refused to, and queried `initBonds(1)` instead. This is the same paraphrase-as-verification failure recorded in D-0067 Finding 6, D-0069 Finding 4 and D-0073 Finding 4 — **the first instance where the discipline held and the trap was avoided rather than recorded after the fact.**
- **Finding 2 — the bond is not spent, and until 2026-08-23 nobody had ever reclaimed one.** Each `create` deposits 0.08 ETH into `DelayedWethPermissionedGameProxy` `0xa9db650cd2959a127083bf6448074e6b01b14b80`. Every game sampled read `status = 0 (IN_PROGRESS)`, `resolvedAt = 0`. The wallet therefore behaved exactly as if the ETH were burned, because the recovery path existed and was never run. R-13 proved it end to end on game 0: `resolveClaim(0,0)` → `resolve()` → finality wait → `claimCredit` (closeGame + unlock) → DelayedWETH wait → `claimCredit` (withdraw), **`status()` resolving to `2` DEFENDER_WINS and 0.08 ETH returning to the proposer** at a total cost of **0.000512078 ETH/game**. Confirmed without historical state, which the public RPC has pruned: DelayedWETH read 3.20 ETH at `gameCount 40`; five further games (+0.40) minus game 0's withdrawal (−0.08) predicts **3.52**, and measured **3.52** exactly.
- **Finding 3 — the correct funding number is a recycling float, and the planner got it wrong twice before the worker corrected it.** If bonds return, the requirement is working capital, not a consumption rate. The R-12 review put the 1h float at **0.24 ETH**. Both errors in that figure were the planner's. **(a)** It used a window of `maxClock 7200 + DelayedWETH delay 3600 = 10800s`, missing the **1800s `disputeGameFinalityDelaySeconds` airgap** that sits between `resolve` and `claimCredit`; the true window is **12600s**. R-13 measured the two gaps at 1872s and 3696s, both just over their minimums. **(b)** It took a time-average where a funding floor was needed: `12600/3624 = 3.477` games in flight, but 3.477 bonds cannot be funded — the fourth game needs a whole bond before the first returns, so the floor is `⌈3.477⌉ × 0.08 = **0.32 ETH**`. The second error is the subtler one and would have under-funded the wallet in practice.
- **Finding 4 — the review's own stop condition tested a mechanism that does not exist.** The R-13 brief instructed the worker to halt if `anchors(8)` moved, on the assumption of per-type anchor slots. It moved; the worker reported it, characterised it as a display artifact, and continued. The characterisation was right, proved adversarially: **`anchors(999)` — a game type that cannot exist — returns the same value as `anchors(1)` and `anchors(8)`**, because this AnchorStateRegistry has a single shared `anchorGame()` and the accessor ignores its argument. The invariants that actually mattered survived and were confirmed: `gameImpls(8)` remained `0x0` and `respectedGameType()` remained `1`. **A stop condition is only as good as the model behind it**, and a worker who obeys a wrong one silently is worse than one who investigates and reports.
- **Finding 5 — resolution and claiming are permissionless, which decouples recovery from the proposer's own budget.** Verified by static call from `0x…dEaD`, a wallet with no role in this system: `resolveClaim` succeeds, and `claimCredit(address)` reaches business logic (`NoCreditToClaim()`, `0x17bfe5f7`) rather than an ACL rejection. `claimCredit` takes the **recipient as a parameter**, so any wallet can pay the gas while the 0.08 ETH still lands on `PROPOSER_ADDRESS`. R-14 used **ADMIN** `0xBB3E19811B2c3423069B54BDFF3e90Dd8094bb0F` as sender, leaving the proposer's nonce untouched and avoiding a race with its hourly `create` calls. Whether ADMIN is the permanent arrangement is undecided.
- **Finding 6 — R-14 built the tool and the recovery is under way but not finished.** `scripts/resolve-games-sepolia.sh` (mode 755) selects legs from on-chain state, **defaults to dry-run** — verified by control-flow reading, not by trusting the flag: all three `cast send` calls sit behind `send_leg()` → `execute_selected()`, reachable only past an `EXECUTE -eq 0 → exit 0` guard — and is safely re-runnable, which matters because the two waits make an interrupted run the normal case. `test-helpers.sh` **194 → 203 PASS**, reproduced. At the time of writing **43 of 46 games are resolved** (games 0–42; game 0 already withdrawn in R-13, so **42 hold claimable credit worth 3.36 ETH**), DelayedWETH holds **3.60 ETH** — the extra 0.24 ETH being the three unresolved games — and the proposer sits at **0.2018 ETH** — below the 0.32 ETH float floor, with the withdrawal legs still to run.
- **Finding 7 — two operating hazards, both cheap and both cost real time today.** **(a)** The operator ran the script while an agent was still writing it and got `line 838: :-/tmp}/…: No such file or directory`. The `mktemp` line sits at 833 in two commits and 866 in the third; **838 matches none**, which is what identified the file as mid-save rather than broken. The committed script was correct throughout. **Do not run a script an agent is still writing, and do not share a checkout between a manual run and a live agent.** **(b)** R-14's handoff reported "game 1 only; games 2–42 still IN_PROGRESS" while the chain showed 43 resolved — `--execute` was re-run after the report was written. **A handoff is a snapshot, and on a live chain it decays in minutes**; the reviewer measured rather than trusted, which is the only reason the true state is in this entry.
- **Decision:** **The funding crisis is closed as diagnosed.** The steady state at a 1h interval with recovery running is a **0.32 ETH recycling float** plus roughly **0.037 ETH/day** of gas (`create` ~0.025 + recovery ~0.012) — about **12% of the ~0.3 ETH/day faucet budget**, against the ~23 ETH/day that opened the investigation. **Operator decisions taken 2026-08-23:** the proposer interval is **1h**, not 5m (R-13 step 0, verified on-chain at 3624s cadence rather than from a log line); recovery gas is paid by **ADMIN**, not the proposer; the proposer stays on **game type 1** and `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE` is unchanged.
- **Consequence:** The immediate action is the operator's: **re-run `resolve-games-sepolia.sh --execute` after each wait** until a dry run reports no `ACTION` legs — only `WAIT` and `SKIP`. **That, not a DelayedWETH balance, is the stopping condition**, and the distinction matters: while the proposer keeps posting hourly, roughly four bonds (~0.32 ETH) are always in flight, so **DelayedWETH never drains to zero** and an operator watching for that would re-run forever. Measured claimable at the time of writing: `credit(PROPOSER_ADDRESS)` summed across all 46 games is **3.3600 ETH** — 42 resolved-and-unclaimed games — against a DelayedWETH balance of 3.60 ETH, the 0.24 ETH difference being the three still-unresolved games. Until the claim completes the proposer remains below its float floor and will keep running dry. **Three decisions remain open**, all now answerable with measured numbers rather than estimates: the **scheduling cadence** for recovery (launchd vs manual re-run — manual is the current de-facto state and is what let the wallet run dry); whether **ADMIN** is the long-term gas wallet; and **what a much newer shared anchor means for step 8b**, which R-14 could not answer because `closeGame` was not reached during its session. **Step 8b remains unblocked** and now also affordable, waiting only on a window outside 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles and a funded proposer. Fix-list unchanged: **3**, **5** (before steps 11–13), and **6**'s carried set. **New fix-list item 8, raised by Codex on this entry's own pull request:** the 1h cadence is a standing decision here but every operator-facing surface still says `5m` — `.env.sepolia.example:94`, `scripts/06-start-proposer-sepolia.sh:26` and the README table at `README.md:723`, all three confirmed. Recreating `.env.sepolia` from the template silently restores twelve times the posting rate, which needs a **3.36 ETH** float rather than 0.32 and recreates the dry-wallet failure this entry exists to close. **A decision recorded only in the log is not propagated**, which is the same drift class recorded twelve times in this project. Still open from R-12 and not addressed since: `funding-watch.sh` returns **FAIL** with the funder health endpoint reporting `status=failing`, while the batcher wallet itself is healthy. Next free decision id is **D-0075**.

### D-0075 — **Recovery is automated and the money is home: 3.50 ETH recovered, the hourly agent has run unattended, and the funding arc that started as "23 ETH/day" is closed**
- **Context:** D-0074 closed the *diagnosis* — the drain was a recoverable type-1 bond — but left the recovery manual and three operator-facing surfaces still advertising a 5m proposer. **R-15** (ForteL2#129) implemented the 2026-08-23 operator decision: recovery runs hourly, and the 1h cadence is propagated everywhere. This entry records the measured outcome, one planner error that a worker overrode correctly for the third consecutive task, and one narrow weakening accepted knowingly.
- **Finding 1 — the money came back, measured rather than projected.** The proposer went from **0.1206 ETH to 3.6184 ETH — a delta of +3.4978** — while `DelayedWethPermissionedGameProxy` fell from 3.60 ETH to **0.48 ETH** — six in-flight bonds, verified individually (games 43–46 resolved awaiting claim legs, 47–48 still unresolved). **That is six bonds, not the four D-0074 predicted, and the difference is the entry's own correction to itself.** D-0074's 0.32 ETH figure is the *protocol-delay lower bound*, which assumes every recovery leg lands the instant it becomes eligible. The hourly agent fires only at `:00`, so each wait is quantized up to the next hour boundary: the 1800s finality airgap costs a full hour, and the 3600s DelayedWETH delay costs one or two depending on where the `+24s` block overshoot lands. Create-to-withdrawn is therefore **4–6 hours, not 3.5**, and at one game per hour that is **4–6 bonds in flight**. **The operational float to budget is the measured 0.48 ETH, not 0.32** — budgeting the lower bound would leave the fifth and sixth in-flight proposals unfunded. Games 0–42 now read `skip zero_credit`, the script's terminal state for a game whose bond is fully withdrawn (`status == 2 and credit == 0 and weth_amount == 0`). **The ~23 ETH/day figure that opened this arc is closed at a ~0.48 ETH recycling float plus roughly 0.037 ETH/day of gas — about 12% of the ~0.3 ETH/day faucet budget.** The gas figure is unaffected by scheduling; only the float moved.
- **Finding 2 — the hourly agent has run unattended and done real work, which is the only evidence that mattered.** `com.steve.fortel2-resolve-games` uses `StartCalendarInterval` with `Minute=0` only, deliberately over `StartInterval=3600`: the latter *misses* firings that fall while the machine sleeps, which would skip the 00:00–02:00 recoveries during the `com.steve.fortel2-sleep` window and add roughly three games of locked float for nothing. Recovery is **L1-only** — `require_sepolia_env` validates `L2_CHAIN_ID=852` as a config value, not L2 reachability — so it runs correctly with the L2 stack down. The proof, from `~/Library/Logs/fortel2-resolve-games.out.log`:

  ```
  now=1787532360  ->  2026-08-23 17:46:00   RunAtLoad,  txs_sent=0
  now=1787533200  ->  2026-08-23 18:00:00   CALENDAR,   txs_sent=3
  ```

  The 18:00 firing unlocked game 45 and resolved game 46 with nobody watching, for `gas_spent_eth=0.000411918961297926`. **R-15's handoff declared this gap open** — "first unattended `:00` has not been observed" — and it was true when written; the firing happened while the review was being conducted, and the reviewer measured it rather than inheriting the claim. A second operating note in the same shape as D-0074 Finding 7(b): **a handoff is a snapshot, and this one decayed favourably within the hour.**
- **Finding 3 — the planner asserted a checker was dynamic when it was hardcoded, and the worker overrode the instruction correctly.** The R-15 brief stated that `scripts/check-launchd.sh` "enumerates `com.steve.fortel2-*.plist` dynamically and should pick your plist up with no edit", and explicitly forbade editing it: *"do not adjust the checker to accept your file."* At `eead943` the main loop read `for short in fortel2-health fortel2-sleep fortel2-wake`. The planner had read the file's *other* glob — the reverse stale-host walk at line 200 — and generalised it to the main enumeration. **Obeying the instruction literally would have left the recovery agent permanently invisible to the drift check**, which is the precise silent failure that check exists to prevent. The worker generalised the loop to a glob, reported it in full, and was right. **This is the third consecutive task in which a worker disproved a premise the planner wrote confidently** (D-0074 Finding 1 on game type; D-0074 Finding 3 on the float window; this). The pattern is now frequent enough to be a standing expectation rather than a series of incidents: **a brief's claims about code the planner did not read line by line are hypotheses, and should be labelled as such.**
- **Finding 4 — one narrow weakening, accepted knowingly rather than discovered later.** Making `plist_script` take the last non-flag path-like argument was necessary — the old "last `ProgramArguments` entry" logic would report `--execute` as the script path. But it costs a detection. Probed during review with two plists differing only by a dropped `--execute`: **old logic → `--execute` vs the script path → FAIL; new logic → both resolve to the script path → falls through to the full-args comparison → WARN**, carrying the message *"ProgramArguments wrapper-prefixed… LaunchControl fdautil is a third-party dependency."* That message is wrong for this case and indistinguishable from the pre-existing health-agent warning a reader has been trained to ignore. **Consequence if it ever fires: recovery runs dry-run forever, bonds accumulate, and the proposer drains — the exact failure this arc closed — behind a warning that looks familiar.** It requires someone to hand-edit the installed plist without re-copying, so the exposure is narrow. **Accepted, with the fix recorded rather than done:** the args mismatch should FAIL when the installed args are not a strict *prefix* extension of the repo args, since a wrapper prepends and a dropped flag does not.
- **Finding 5 — fix-list item 8 is closed on the three surfaces that mattered, and a fourth was found and left.** `.env.sepolia.example:94`, `scripts/06-start-proposer-sepolia.sh:26` and `README.md:723` all now read **1h**, each confirmed by direct read. A fourth reference survives at `scripts/demo-checklist.sh:337` — `${SEPOLIA_PROPOSER_INTERVAL:-5m}` inside a `skip` **message**, not a functional default, so its impact is a misleading hint rather than a wrong posting rate. It was found and disclosed by the worker rather than by a later reader, which is the outcome the concept-search discipline exists to produce. The live `.env.sepolia` was untouched (R-13 set it) and **the running proposer was not restarted**, so the new script default takes effect only on its next start.
- **Decision:** **The funding arc is closed.** Bond recovery is automated hourly, verified running unattended, and the proposer holds 3.6184 ETH against a **measured 0.48 ETH operational float** — roughly **7.5×** headroom. The 0.32 ETH protocol lower bound recorded in D-0074 stands as a statement about the protocol, and is superseded for budgeting purposes by this measurement. `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE` remains **1**, the interval remains **1h**, and **ADMIN** remains the gas wallet for recovery, with its permanence still an open question rather than a settled one. Fix-list item **8** is closed.
- **Consequence:** **Step 8b is the next action and is now genuinely unblocked** — a funded proposer, a stable float and an automated recovery loop were the practical preconditions, and all three hold. It waits only on an operator window outside 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles. Note that 8b registers game type **8**, whose `initBonds(8)` is **0**, so a successful 8b **deletes the bond-float question entirely** and makes this entire arc's machinery a bridge rather than a permanent fixture — the hourly agent would then be recovering a shrinking tail of type-1 games. **Item 5 (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention) should still land before steps 11–13**, or each completing step breaks `phase7-gate-parity.sh`. Remaining fix-list: **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check), **5**, and **6**'s carried set. New follow-ups from this entry: the `plist_script` prefix-vs-suffix fix (Finding 4), the `demo-checklist.sh` 5m straggler (Finding 5), and **alerting on a silently dead recovery agent** — same class as `funding-watch.sh`'s FAIL, which reports the funder health endpoint `status=failing` and remains unaddressed since R-12. Next free decision id is **D-0076**.

### D-0076 — **Recovery run cost is now flat instead of linear, and step 8b's preconditions are verified. Two operating hazards recorded: the hourly agent runs whatever branch the checkout is on, and 8b is once-only twice over**
- **Context:** D-0075 closed the funding arc with an hourly recovery agent, but that agent re-scanned every game from index 0 on every run. **R-16** (ForteL2#132) made the run cost a function of the in-flight working set. This entry records the measured outcome, the probe discipline that validated it, two operating hazards found during review, and the verified preconditions for **step 8b**, which is the next action and is operator-run rather than dispatchable.
- **Finding 1 — the scan was on a calendar to break, and now is not.** `fetch_snapshot()` iterated `range(game_count)` at 8 `cast call`s per game. Measured before the change: **`gameCount 61` → 57s → 0.934 s/game**. At 24 games/day that reaches ~31 min at 2000 games (~83 days) and **crosses one hour at ~3800 games, about five months out** — at which point the hourly agent would start a second instance while the first was still sending, racing on ADMIN's nonce. R-15 judged that overlap risk remote *because a run took 42 seconds*; the judgement had an expiry date. After R-16, measured independently by the reviewer: **warm 6 games / 14s vs cold 62 games / 57s**, with the warm path tracking the working set rather than the history. At the ~8760 games a year produces, that is ~14s instead of ~2.2 hours.
- **Finding 2 — the safety property was probed and mutation-tested, not read.** The hazard was never performance; it was a watermark advancing one index too far, silently skipping a live game so its bond never returns with nothing in the output explaining why. Sixteen probes were driven directly against the extracted `next_low_water` / `load_watermark`: contiguous advance; **non-contiguous (index 4 waiting, 5–6 terminal) correctly stopping at 4**; a hole in the decision map stopping the walk; `wait finality`, `wait weth_delay` and `action` all blocking advance; and eight malformed-input paths — missing, malformed, non-dict, string mark, **boolean `true`**, negative, out-of-range, factory mismatch — every one falling back to `scan_from 0`. **Fail-safe, never fail-skip.** The `isinstance(mark, bool)` guard is a genuine catch, since `True` is an `int` in Python and would otherwise be accepted as mark `1`. **The probes were then mutation-tested**: adding `weth_delay` to the terminal set made the non-contiguous probe return **7 instead of 4**, walking past a live mid-pipeline game — the exact stranding bug. A probe that goes red on the defect it was written for is worth something; one that has never been shown to fail is not.
- **Finding 3 — terminality is source-proven, not bytecode-proven, and the worker said so.** The brief offered a deliberately lagging watermark as the safe fallback if `zero_credit` could not be shown terminal. Instead the worker grounded the claim in the local contracts — `move`/`resolveClaim` require `IN_PROGRESS`, `claimCredit` zeroes both maps, `hasUnlockedCredit` blocks a second unlock — and shipped an **exact** mark with `--full-scan` as the escape hatch. **The deployed bytecode was never diffed against that source tree**, and the report labels the claim accordingly rather than implying more certainty than it has. That distinction is the finding: an accurate label on a strong-but-incomplete proof beats both an unlabelled assertion and an unnecessary hedge.
- **Finding 4 — the hourly agent executes whatever branch the checkout happens to be on.** `com.steve.fortel2-resolve-games` runs `/Users/steveforte/ForteL2/scripts/resolve-games-sepolia.sh` from the **working tree**, not from a pinned revision. During the R-16 review the tree sat on `feat/r-16-recovery-watermark` while `main` had **zero** `low_water` references, so **the production agent was running unmerged code** — it behaved correctly (`scan_from=56`, `games_examined=5`, `txs_sent=0`), and #132 has since merged, so the exposure is closed for now. But the general hazard stands: **a checkout to any other branch silently changes what a money-moving scheduled job does**, with no signal anywhere. Recorded as a follow-up; the plist arguably should not be branch-sensitive.
- **Finding 5 — a watermark file can predate the code that validates it.** Codex raised two P2s on #132, both fixed in `606bf43`: bind the watermark to its factory, and fail loudly on persist I/O (now `watermark_persist=failed`, stderr, **exit 1**, so launchd records a nonzero last-exit instead of silently reverting to full scans). The live file was written at **10:00**, two minutes before that commit at **10:02:21**, and therefore carried no `factory` field. `load_watermark` compares factories only when the record has one, so a legacy file is accepted rather than rejected — a deliberate backward-compatibility choice that self-heals on the next write. The realistic re-genesis case is still caught: a replacement factory starts at `gameCount 0`, so a stale high mark trips `out_of_range` and falls back to a full scan. The residual exposure is the narrow window where a new factory's `gameCount` grows past a stale mark before that mark is rewritten. **Accepted and left**, recorded so it is a known shape rather than a surprise.
- **Finding 6 — step 8b's preconditions, verified rather than assumed (2026-08-24).** `gameImpls(8)` reads **`0x0`**, which is what `SetDisputeGameImpl`'s "SDGI-10" requires; `respectedGameType()` reads **`1`**, pre-flip; `deployments/sepolia/rollup.json` is committed (1475 bytes, #115); `.github/workflows/build-prestate.yml` exists (F7-7); the proposer holds **3.6043 ETH**. **The signer must hold both `L1ProxyAdminOwner` and `Guardian`** — `MakeRespected` calls `setRespectedGameType`, which is Guardian-gated (D-0063 Finding 3a) — and that pairing **is now verified (2026-08-24)**: `OpChainProxyAdmin` `0x2f6b90b7028c7b3c05bd268f10b8c30474cdb304` — confirmed as the DisputeGameFactoryProxy's admin via its EIP-1967 admin slot, not inferred from op-deployer's naming — reads `owner()` = `0xBB3E19811B2c3423069B54BDFF3e90Dd8094bb0F`, and `SuperchainConfigProxy` `0x9901…7f64` reads `guardian()` = the same address, which is `ADMIN_ADDRESS` (key↔address derivation enforced by F7-10). All 8b preconditions are therefore verified; nothing remains but the operator window. **8b is operator-run, not dispatchable**, because registration is once-only twice over: `shouldDeployAdditionalDisputeGames` skips when state already holds a game, and on-chain `SetDisputeGameImpl` requires `gameImpls(8) == address(0)`. **A wrong prestate hash cannot be corrected by re-running apply** — the fix is a manual L1PAO `setImplementation` or another network-wide wipe (D-0063 Finding 5). The `cannon witness --input` check in part (2) is therefore the last reversible moment, and is a gate rather than a formality.
- **Decision:** **R-16 is merged and recovery run cost is flat.** The watermark is an exact low-water mark bound to the factory address, advancing only across contiguous terminal games, failing open to a full scan on every malformed input, with `--full-scan` as the operator escape hatch. **The overlap lock stays unbuilt** — R-16 removed the calendar pressure that made it urgent, so it drops from "five months out" to genuinely optional.
- **Consequence:** The next action is **step 8b**, operator-run, waiting only on a window outside 23:45–03:00 America/Los_Angeles — the L1PAO+Guardian pairing is confirmed (Finding 6).
