Its four parts are **one unit of work**; parts (3) and (4) especially, since the apply flips the respected type 1→8 and a proposer left on type 1 posts games that cannot prove a withdrawal or advance the anchor (D-0063 Finding 3c), and `start_bg` returns 0 on a live pid so a plain restart is a silent no-op (`scripts/lib.sh:122-125`). **After 8b the recovery machinery becomes a bridge rather than a fixture**: `initBonds(8)` is **0**, so the bond float stops growing and the hourly agent drains a shrinking type-1 tail before idling. The watermark is bound to the factory, which 8b does not replace, so no watermark reset is needed. **Item 5 (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention) should still land before steps 11–13.** Remaining fix-list: **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check), **5**, and **6**'s carried set. Follow-ups now carried: the branch-sensitive agent path (Finding 4), the `plist_script` prefix-vs-suffix weakening (D-0075 Finding 4), the `demo-checklist.sh` 5m straggler, alerting on a silently dead recovery agent, and `funding-watch.sh`'s standing **FAIL** with the funder health endpoint reporting `status=failing` — unaddressed since R-12. Next free decision id is **D-0077**.

### D-0077 — **Step 8b is done: the type-8 Kona game is registered, respected, and producing live games with the verified prestate — the bond float is now a closed, draining set**
- **Context:** Executed by the operator 2026-08-24, 10:40–11:21 PDT, planner verifying each gate — the first operator-run step since the wipe, per D-0076's "operator-run, not dispatchable." All four parts landed in one sitting as required. CI build: run 32758660527.
- **Finding 1 — the prestate's chain of custody is closed at every link.** CI checks 1 and 2 passed (witnessHash == `.pre`, stateVersion 8, chain-852 genesis found in the image, sentinel control absent, registry comparison `no` as expected for a custom build). The gate was then re-run **locally** with the pinned `multicannon untagged-da197e45` — same monorepo SHA as CI — on the downloaded artifact: witnessHash **`0x034c90f083e8c86bb6bf18236d653d4aa42fac0653f013c780448000b9796b8d`**, equal to `.pre`, with the hash-named artifact byte-identical to `prestate.bin.gz`. The genesis discriminator was additionally checked against the **live chain**, not just the committed file: `cast block 0` on the sequencer returns `0xe242b1a3…2386d`, identical to `rollup.json` and to the hash CI found baked in the image. Commitment pinned in `.env.sepolia` (backup taken outside the repo first).
- **Finding 2 — registration verified on-chain, including one trap worth its own sentence: at monorepo pin `da197e45`, reading `absolutePrestate()` or `gameType()` on a game *implementation* returns zero, and that is correct behavior.** First reads after the apply showed `gameImpls(8) = 0x84c0889A10E2f120F9fE6a27cEE8c6Cf735A8584` (23 KB of code) and `respectedGameType() = 8`, but the impl's `absolutePrestate()` read `0x0` — alarming until a control read on the long-proven type-1 impl returned the same zeros. Cause, from the pinned source: `FaultDisputeGame` uses **clone-with-immutable-args** (`absolutePrestate()` is `_getArgBytes32(120)`); per-type params live in the factory's **`gameArgs(gameType)`**, not the impl. `gameArgs(8)` decodes to exactly the five expected values: the pinned commitment, vm `0x71b4b694a5f522f8ecc6e4f7ac2e966a8ead0f73`, ASR `0x8f98…f480`, DelayedWETH `0xa9db…4b80`, l2ChainId **852**. **A future reader who calls `absolutePrestate()` on an impl, sees `0x0`, and concludes the registration is wrong will be re-living this finding — read `gameArgs` instead.** `initBonds(8)` confirmed **0**.
- **Finding 3 — the README's part-4 acceptance check does not exist, and behavioral verification replaced it.** README § 8b says to confirm the proposer startup line reports `game-type=8`; op-proposer at this version prints **no such line** (startup logs only the DGF connection and "Proposer started"). Acceptance was instead proven by conduct: the restarted proposer (fresh pid, old one killed via the kill-switch idiom — `start_bg`'s silent no-op avoided) immediately proposed, and **game 63** reads `gameType = 8`, clone `absolutePrestate()` == the pinned commitment, `wasRespectedGameTypeWhenCreated = true`, bond necessarily 0 (`create` reverts on wrong value; the tx succeeded). **Follow-up (docs): fix README § 8b part (4)'s acceptance sentence** to point at on-chain game inspection rather than a log line that never appears.
- **Finding 4 — the cutover wasted nothing.** Game 62 — the old type-1 proposer's final hourly create, 11:02:12, racing the apply — carries `wasRespectedGameTypeWhenCreated = true`: it beat the flip. Every type-1 game ever created was respected at creation; game 63 is the first type-8. The apply was deliberately run after 11:02 to stay off ADMIN's nonce while the `:00` recovery agent fired, and the proposer flip landed 19 minutes after the respected-type flip.
- **Decision:** **Step 8b is complete and verified.** The chain's respected game type is **8** with the Kona prestate `0x034c90f0…796b8d`; the proposer posts type-8, zero-bond games hourly. `PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE=8` in `.env.sepolia`; recovery agent untouched — the watermark binds to the factory address, which 8b did not change.
- **Consequence:** **The bond-float question is deleted, as D-0075/D-0076 anticipated.** The type-1 tail is now a closed set (games ≤ 62, ~0.48 ETH in flight at cutover) that the hourly agent drains to zero, after which it idles at ~14s/run; the D-0074→D-0076 machinery is formally a bridge. **Next: steps 11–13, with fix-list item 5 (`phase7-gates.json` marker convention) landing first** — each of those steps otherwise breaks `phase7-gate-parity.sh` as it completes. Remaining fix-list: **3**, **5**, **6**'s carried set. Follow-ups carried, plus one new: the README § 8b part-(4) acceptance-line fix (Finding 3). Still standing: branch-sensitive agent path, `plist_script` prefix-vs-suffix, `demo-checklist.sh` 5m straggler, dead-recovery-agent alerting, and `funding-watch.sh`'s **FAIL** (funder endpoint `status=failing`, unaddressed since R-12). Next free decision id is **D-0078**.

### D-0078 — **Item 5 closed: the marker convention is stated in `phase7-gates.json`, the checker teaches it on failure, and a hole in the check itself was found and shut on the way**
- **Context:** Fix-list item 5 (D-0070 Finding 2), dispatched as **item-5** and returned as ForteL2#136. This entry is the review record, written by the planner onto the worker's branch so one merge closes the item and its record together.
- **Finding 1 — the deliverable exceeds the brief, and the excess is a verified strengthening.** The brief froze the checker's matching logic; a Codex P2 on the PR showed the old `When+What` concatenation left a hole the new convention itself describes: a completion edit could move the action phrase into the When column, rewrite What freely, and stay green. The worker split the parse into `(When, What)`, searches `prdMustContain` in **What only**, and kept the v7-trigger check on `When+What` (that trigger legitimately lives in When). The reviewer proved the hole existed rather than taking it on faith: the **old** checker run against a fixture with the sos-adopt marker moved into When and What rewritten to "Recovery complete" **exits 0 and prints `PASS sos-adopt PRD step 10`**; the new checker fails it with the convention pointer. A tightening prompted by a bot, verified by probe, accepted.
- **Finding 2 — independent verification, all counts reproduced except one benign miscount.** Parity: **60 PASS, exit 0** on repo files. Helpers: baseline **215** measured on `main`, branch **219** — the diff adds **four** PASS-emitting tests, not the report's "218 / 3 additive"; a transcription miscount, all four present and correct. Fail-closed probed by hand beyond the suite: deleting `markerConvention` → `FAIL missing key`, exit 1; blanking `onRed` → `FAIL markerConvention missing onRed`, exit 1. Row 8b's What cell is **byte-identical** to `main`; only the When cell gained `**DONE 2026-08-24 (D-0077)**`, per the convention it demonstrates.
- **Finding 3 — reporting gaps, named so the return format keeps its meaning.** EXISTING CHECKS MODIFIED claimed "matching … unchanged" while the What-only narrowing *is* a matching change — disclosed in the PR thread ("Fixed in e14f2e1") and in code comments, but not in the field built for it. "review threads — none" meant none *unresolved*. Neither hides anything; both made the reviewer re-derive facts the report should have carried.
- **Decision:** **Item 5 is closed on merge of #136.** The convention lives in `tasks/phase7-gates.json` `markerConvention` (fail-closed if deleted or emptied), the relevant FAIL lines cite it, and the 8b row is the worked example. Remaining fix-list: **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check) and **6**'s carried set.
- **Consequence:** Steps **11–13** can now each be marked complete without breaking `phase7-gate-parity.sh`, provided the marker goes in the When column — and the failure message now says so if anyone forgets. Next sequence action: **step 11** (stock proposer posts a valid game; `op-challenger` does not fault it), which requires op-challenger running against the type-8 game — scoping is the planner's next task. Carried follow-ups unchanged from D-0077, including the README § 8b part-(4) acceptance-line fix and `funding-watch.sh`'s standing FAIL. Next free decision id is **D-0079**.

### D-0079 — **Step 11's code prep is done, and F7-5 closed itself on the way: the challenger preflight now verifies the playable type against the chain, three-state per D-0057**
- **Context:** Task **step11-preflight**, returned as ForteL2#137, reviewed here. It maps `cannon-kona → 8` in `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh` (E-F7-2-1's deferral made obsolete by D-0077's on-chain facts) and adds the local-prestate witness comparison.

Codex and Bugbot both flagged the first draft (`73b69d5`) for hard-exiting when the witness hash *cannot be computed* — and they were right, because **D-0057 (operator decision, 2026-08-19) already prescribes three states**: computed match → proceed; computed mismatch → refuse; cannot-compute (`.json` artifact, unparseable version, URL-only mode) → loud WARN naming the reason, proceed. Head `1948764` implements exactly that. **This closes F7-5**, carried unwritten since D-0057 on fix-list item 6. Both bot threads target the draft commit; the head fixes them — verified by diff, not by trusting "addressed."
- **Finding 2 — independent verification, all counts reproduced.** Scratch clone: `bash -n` pass; parity exit 0; helpers **224 PASS** (219 baseline + 5 additive, matching the report exactly this time). Planner-run mutation probe: deleting the `cannon-kona) echo 8` mapping line turns the suite red (`FAIL … must map cannon-kona to game type 8 (D-0077)`). The mismatch-refusal and skip-path behaviors are covered by the worker's mocked tests inside the 224. The test-helpers frozen-hex tripwire was extended with two entries — the real commitment `0x034c…796b8d` and an obvious fixture — which is that check's sanctioned mechanism, not a weakening.
- **Finding 3 — what is deliberately not covered.** The live-RPC preflight and the challenger start are operator-run, post-merge; the mocked harness proves logic, not the wire. The comparison is scoped to the local-file `CHALLENGER_PRESTATE` form; `CHALLENGER_PRESTATES_URL`-only mode has no file to hash and WARNs by construction (D-0057's own scoping). Unmapped types still skip with the mapped list visible.
- **Decision:** **#137 approved.** Merging it closes step 11's code prep and **fix-list 6's F7-5**.
- **Consequence:** The operator half is staged and next: `.env.sepolia` already carries `CHALLENGER_TRACE_TYPE=cannon-kona` and `CHALLENGER_PRESTATE=/Users/steveforte/src/fortel2/prestates/chain852-8b/prestate.bin.gz` (artifact witness-verified against the pinned commitment on 2026-08-24, stored outside the wipe path). After merge + pull: start via `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh`, expect the full `gameImpls(8)`/`gameArgs(8)` preflight and the witness match line, then observe ≥1 full type-8 game lifecycle with zero challenger attack transactions, record proxy/rootClaim/L2 sequence with `cast`. That observation ticks US-073's remaining boxes and gets its own entry. Remaining fix-list: **3**, and **6** minus F7-5 (F7-9, loader-level duplicate detection, `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, that script's mode 644 + README bare-path invocation). Next free decision id is **D-0080**.

### D-0080 — **The op-node liveness probe is fixed and proven against the live node: the first end-to-end challenger start exposed a latent wait that could never succeed**
- **Context:** Task **opnode-wait**, returned as ForteL2#138, reviewed here. The first-ever end-to-end run of `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh` (step 11's operator half, 2026-08-24) hung on `wait_for_rpc` against op-node: the probe is `eth_blockNumber`, op-node serves only the `optimism` namespace, and the wait times out against a perfectly healthy node — the D-0055/D-0057 defect shape, latent because the two op-node waiters (this script and `create-bad-proposal-sepolia.sh`) had never run past their earlier gates. The failure was fail-closed: nothing started.
- **Finding 1 — the fix is proven against the real service, not only the mock.** The worker could not hit the live node from its environment and said so. The reviewer ran both waits from the PR branch against the live `127.0.0.1:9547`: `wait_for_opnode_rpc` → **"op-node (live probe) is up (L1 block 11559265)", exit 0**, immediately; old `wait_for_rpc` → timeout, exit 1. The residual gap in the handoff is closed by measurement.
- **Finding 2 — the probe is strict in the right direction.** `wait_for_opnode_rpc` succeeds only on a JSON-RPC **result** from `optimism_syncStatus` — the parser rejects null results, error envelopes and non-JSON, so an HTTP-200-without-result endpoint keeps waiting (covered by an additive test). `wait_for_rpc` itself is byte-identical; every eth-endpoint caller is untouched. Both op-node call sites switched (the handoff's SHARED FILES under-listed them; they were in its bash -n line and are in the diff).
- **Finding 3 — verification reproduced exactly.** Scratch clone: bash -n pass on all three scripts; parity exit 0; helpers **228 PASS** (224 baseline + 4 additive, matching the report). Worker's mutation (probe swapped back to `eth_blockNumber`) reported red; the reviewer's live dual-probe is the stronger equivalent and was run instead. CI green including Bugbot; `.github/CODEOWNERS:3` does route `scripts/lib.sh` to the operator, as the handoff noted — merge is the human review.
- **Decision:** **#138 approved.** One process note recorded, not as a defect: the operator checkout was found sitting on the PR branch (clean) — with `com.steve.fortel2-resolve-games` executing from that checkout hourly (D-0076 Finding 4), testing branches in place is safe only while the branch is a superset of `main`, which this one was. Return the checkout to `main` after merging.
- **Consequence:** The operator half of step 11 resumes where it stopped: merge, `git checkout main && git pull`, re-run `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh`, expect the full preflight (gameImpls(8), gameArgs(8) decode, witness match) and then a live challenger. The observation window — one full type-8 game lifecycle, zero attack transactions, `cast` records — ticks US-073's remaining boxes and gets its own entry. Follow-up carried from the handoff, low priority: a TCP-only listener (accepts connections, never answers JSON-RPC) still waits out the full timeout rather than failing fast — acceptable, the timeout is loud. Next free decision id is **D-0081**.

### D-0081 — **The batch-splitting L1 proxy is live-proven and merged; one process slip (merge before re-review) cost nothing because verification moved to main**
- **Context:** Task **l1-batch-proxy** (ForteL2#139) built `scripts/l1-batch-proxy.py` + start wrapper so the pinned op-challenger — hardcoded `DefaultBatchSize=100`, no flag (`op-service/sources/batching/multicall.go:12`, `op-challenger/game/service.go:206`) — can enumerate the 65-and-growing game set without tripping QuickNode's 50/s sub-call cap. This entry is the review record, including a changes-requested round and a post-merge re-verification.
- **Finding 1 — the fix was proven against the real endpoint with the real call shape, both directions.** Reviewer probe, 2026-08-24: a 65×`eth_call(gameAtIndex)` batch sent **direct** to QuickNode returned **50 ok / 15 per-entry rate-limit errors** — the challenger's exact failure, reproduced on demand; the identical batch **via the proxy** returned **65/65 results, ids in request order**. An earlier probe with 65×`eth_chainId` did NOT trip the cap — QuickNode weights methods, so any future rate-limit reproduction must use the real method mix, not a cheap stand-in.
- **Finding 2 — three bot findings on the head commit, one of each verdict, all adjudicated against code rather than labels.** Codex P2 (timeouts not converted to per-entry errors): **refuted** — `_forward_raw` catches HTTPError/URLError/TimeoutError in one try, `resp.read()` included; every shape returns a status tuple. Codex P1 (start_bg silently no-ops when switching `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL` on a live challenger): **real, accepted as documented** — same hazard class and same treatment as PRD § 8b part (4); an args-aware start_bg remains an unclaimed follow-up. Bugbot Low (unquoted heredoc): **confirmed and made the single blocker** — the mock runner baked an exported `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL` at write time, so the no-network suite would hit the live endpoint on the operator's machine while staying green in CI.
- **Finding 3 — the blocker's fix landed, but the merge happened before re-review.** Operator merged #139 with the fix commit (`e6b0427`) unreviewed. Verification moved to merged `main` (`47f5285`): the fix is exactly the prescribed escape (`CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL="\$L1_RPC_URL"`), and the probe that catches the original bug — full suite with `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL=http://127.0.0.1:1/poison` exported — passes **232/232 with zero FAILs**, identical to the clean run. **No harm done this time; the general rule stands: a merged-early PR turns re-review into main-review, and anything found becomes a follow-up PR rather than a push to the branch.**
- **Finding 4 — design decisions verified as correct.** Merged split batches return HTTP 200 with per-entry JSON-RPC errors (earlier Bugbot P1, fixed in `2010685`) — required for go-ethereum's client to decode partial failure; per-chunk length-mismatch guards; loopback-only listen; upstream from env only, redacted in logs and the status endpoint. Money paths (batcher, proposer, recovery) stay on the direct connection by scope.
- **Decision:** **#139 stands merged and verified.** The proxy is a **bridge, not a fixture**: its retirement condition is the pin moving to an op-challenger with a batch-size knob (or an RPC plan without per-sub-call caps), at which point removal is deleting the `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL` line and the proxy scripts.
- **Consequence:** Step 11's observation window opens on the operator's next action: stop the blind challenger, start the proxy (`start-l1-batch-proxy-sepolia.sh`), set `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL=http://127.0.0.1:9549`, restart the challenger, and watch ≥1 full type-8 game lifecycle with zero attack transactions. Follow-ups carried: args-aware start_bg (Finding 2), `.env.sepolia.example` challenger/proxy documentation, and the D-0079/D-0080 sets unchanged. Next free decision id is **D-0082**.

### D-0082 — **Step 11 is closed by measurement: an honest game ran its full lifecycle under a judging challenger and drew zero attacks — after a third latent integration defect (safedb) was found and fixed inside the window**
- **Context:** The operator half of step 11 (US-073), executed 2026-08-24 13:09–16:23 PDT. Three never-run-end-to-end integration paths broke in sequence — the op-node wait probe (D-0080, #138), the QuickNode batch cap (D-0081, #139), and op-node's missing safe-head database (this entry) — each diagnosed live, fixed, and recorded. This entry closes US-073 and supersedes one sentence of D-0081.
- **Finding 1 — the challenger could start but not judge: op-node had no safe-head database.** With proxy and preflight green, every type-8 move calculation failed with `failed to get safe head at l1 block …: safe head database not enabled` — `op-challenger` verifies a root claim by asking op-node for the safe head at the game's L1 head via `optimism_safeHeadAtL1Block`, which requires op-node to run with `--safedb.path`. The flag has an env form (`OP_NODE_SAFEDB_PATH`), so no script change was needed: set to `$DATA_DIR/safedb` in `.env.sepolia` (derived data; wipe-safe by location), op-node restarted, `optimism_safeHeadAtL1Block` verified answering with real safe heads. **Games 63–65 remain permanently un-analyzable** — each game pins its L1 head at creation and safedb records only from enablement — tolerated as log noise until they leave the challenger's 12h game window; every post-restart game is judgeable.
- **Finding 2 — the planner's restart instruction was wrong, and the recovery was the sanctioned full cycle.** The planner's block killed op-node then ran `04-start-sequencer-sepolia.sh` on the assumption it would no-op past the live op-geth. It refused instead: `assert_l2_ports_free` (lib.sh:442) requires op-geth's three ports, op-node's RPC, **and the batcher/proposer admin ports** all free — the start scripts are cold-start-only by design, and there is no sanctioned single-service restart. The sequencer sat dead until a full `stop-all-sepolia.sh` + `start-all-sepolia.sh` + proxy + challenger cycle (~2 minutes, no losses; the recovery agent is L1-only and unaffected). **The planner's error, recorded as such: read the guard before writing the block.** A hand-built op-node command was deliberately rejected in favor of the script-sanctioned cycle.
- **Finding 3 — the observation, all of it measured.** Target: **game 66** at `0xBEBDC78fA5BcBCfB9a31510036718eD8B6c27609`, created 14:21:36 — the first game whose L1 head postdates safedb. Its `rootClaim` `0xa0718701b1e1a3bec244495e29893cb3a9f1521f8a7ed73db482bf23def2b771` **equals op-node's own `outputRoot` at L2 block 86560** (`extraData` `0x…15220`), so the proposal is provably honest; `wasRespectedGameTypeWhenCreated = true`; prestate = the pinned `0x034c90f0…`. The challenger tracked it with **no trace-calculation errors** (unlike 63–65 — it computed its verdict), the claim count stayed **1** for the whole 7200s clock, and the challenger itself resolved it 96 seconds after expiry: `resolveClaim` `0x47cdbc0d…`, `resolve` `0xbd734f48…`, **status 2 DEFENDER_WINS at 16:23:12**. The challenger's complete transaction ledger for the day (nonces 2–10, every selector decoded and verified): three games × (`resolveClaim` + `resolve` + `closeGame`) — resolution bookkeeping only, **zero move/attack/step calls**, ~0.00195 ETH total gas, balance 0.198 ETH. The batcher's rate-limit error count did not move after the proxy went in.
- **Finding 4 — supersedes D-0081's retirement sentence (raised by Codex on #140, verified against the tree).** Proxy retirement touches **five surfaces**, not two: the `CHALLENGER_L1_RPC_URL` env line, both proxy scripts, the `l1-batch-proxy` entry in `stop-all-sepolia.sh`, the proxy paragraph in README § Phase 7 challenger, and the `test-helpers.sh` wiring/property tests (~lines 1512–1545 as of #139) that require and execute those files. The suite hard-fails on a partial deletion — the tripwire working as designed.
- **Decision:** **US-073 is closed and step 11 is DONE.** All four remaining acceptance criteria are met with evidence: stock proposer (`USE_CUSTOM_PROPOSER` unset) posted accepted games 63–68; the challenger is README-documented (start/stop/logs/keys, extended by #139); the challenger did not attack a valid game it had positively judged; the game's proxy/rootClaim/L2 sequence are recorded here via `cast`, no explorer. **The challenger stays running** — it is now the fastest resolver on the chain (30s poll vs the recovery agent's hourly), which is useful, and its gas cost is negligible.
- **Consequence:** Next is **step 12** (US-074, deliberate bad proposal) — the first genuine trace-generation exercise, and `create-bad-proposal-sepolia.sh` has never run end-to-end, so the step-11 pattern (latent integration defects surfacing live) should be the planning assumption, not a surprise; a planner review of that script precedes any window. Then **step 13** (closeout note) ends the regen. New follow-ups: `.env.sepolia.example` documentation for the challenger/proxy/safedb env set, and the args-aware `start_bg` idea (D-0081 Finding 2). Games 63–65 keep logging judge-errors until they exit the game window (~01:20–03:20 tonight) — known noise, not a fault. Next free decision id is **D-0083**.

### D-0083 — **Step 12 is done: a deliberately false claim on the respected game type was detected, countered with the true root, and defeated — after the tool's own no-arg default crashed on the operator's bash**
- **Context:** US-074, executed 2026-08-24 16:45–18:52 PDT. The bad-proposal tool posts one type-8 game with a corrupted output root, waits, self-checks, and exits; the honest challenger must counter it before its 7200s clock or the false claim resolves as valid on the *respected* type. This is the first genuine trace-generation exercise on the chain. Both preconditions the planner review named held (challenger pre-funded to 0.548 ETH; game-type resolves to 8 from env), and the step-11 prediction — that a never-run-end-to-end path breaks live — held too.
- **Finding 1 — the tool is unrunnable in its documented no-arg form on the operator's shell, reproduced live.** `create-bad-proposal-sepolia.sh` builds an allowlisted `FORWARD=()` array and expands `"${FORWARD[@]}"` at line 86 under `set -u`. On **bash 3.2.57** (macOS default) an empty array expanded under `set -u` throws `unbound variable`; the documented invocation (no `-block`, one-shot, uses the current safe head) therefore dies before broadcast — verified: nothing was sent, no game created. The tool's own tests missed it because `main_test.go` exercises the Go binary, not the shell wrapper, and the wrapper's only test-helpers coverage is a **structural grep** (the op-node-wait check at ~line 275), which cannot catch a runtime crash. **Workaround used:** pass `-block <current safe head>` explicitly, making `FORWARD` non-empty. **Fix queued** (`"${FORWARD[@]+"${FORWARD[@]}"}"`), plus the latent game-type default below.
- **Finding 2 — the pre-run bond analysis was right, including which number was a red herring.** The planner review measured the type-8 bond ladder at MAX_GAME_DEPTH 73 summing to ~631 ETH for a fully-contested game and nearly called step 12 infeasible; the correction — that a *one-shot* proposer forfeits by silence, so the honest challenger wins with a single depth-1 counter (~0.088 ETH) — is exactly what happened. The challenger attacked at 16:50:12 (3 min after creation), claim 1 posted by the challenger key `0x4f05…C79fd` with bond **0.0876 ETH** and value `0x9fe3…f285` — **the true output root**, contradicting the proposer's corrupted `0x9fe3…f27a` (real root XOR 0xFF on the last byte). No deeper play; the proposer stayed silent.
- **Finding 3 — resolution and integrity, measured.** Game **69** at `0xd39B5353e3A9bbc3547160407F1CE427E73B9349`, L2 sequence 90970, create tx `0xe669394f…55ac`, mined L1 11560362. **Full dispute tx set** (challenger key `0x4f05…C79fd`, all on game 69): counter-claim `attack(bytes32,uint256,bytes32)` **`0x95fe72bb…7b36`** posting the true root at depth 1 (2026-08-24 16:50:24 PDT); resolution `resolveClaim` **`0x1708686a…a910`** and **`0xaf69a2ce…d4d6`** then `resolve()` **`0x27afa0fd…9e32`** (18:51–18:52 PDT), the last matching `resolvedAt 1787622744`. Resolved **status 1 CHALLENGER_WINS** at 18:52:24 (`resolvedAt 1787622744`, 7368s after creation — the 7200s clock plus resolution), with claim 0's `counteredBy` set to the challenger. **Integrity held**: `respectedGameType` remained **8** throughout, the false claim never advanced the anchor, and the challenger discriminated it from honest games (it left every valid type-8 game alone, as in D-0082). The challenger's 0.0876 ETH is locked as game credit and refunds via `claimCredit` — recovery-agent territory, unclaimed as of this entry.
- **Finding 4 — one operating slip, recorded without blame.** The runbook's block 3 (restart the stock proposer) was not run after the create, so honest proposing was paused **~2 hours** (16:45–18:53) and the anchor did not advance in that window — surfaced by the planner's integrity check at resolution, not noticed at the time. Restarted; no loss on a test chain, but on a production chain a 2h proposer gap is a real availability event. The runbook did say to restart immediately after the create; the lesson is that a multi-block operator runbook needs the restart adjacent to the fire step, not after the observation setup.
- **Finding 5 — the latent game-type default, still open.** Both the wrapper (`-game-type "${PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE:-1}"`) and the Go tool (`envOrUint("PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE", 1)`) fall back to type **1** if the env var is unset — which post-8b would post a silently inert, unchallenged bad game (type 1 is no longer respected). Harmless today because the env is set to 8, but it is the wrong default now; folded into the fix task.
- **Decision:** **US-074 is closed and step 12 is DONE.** All four acceptance criteria are met with evidence: bad proposal created in an isolated, documented one-shot (`-i-understand…`/`CONFIRM_…` double gate); challenger took the honest side and the game resolved for the true claim; tx hashes and game address recorded here; the one-shot leaves nothing running and the stock proposer is again the only writer. The challenger stays running as the chain's fastest resolver.
- **Consequence:** The regen's last step is **13** (US-075, closeout note — what the fault proof proved and what is still trusted: sequencer honesty, DA permanence, key custody). New task: the bad-proposal fix (empty-array crash + game-type default + a wrapper execution test that catches the crash on bash 3.2). Carried follow-ups unchanged: `.env.sepolia.example` challenger/proxy/safedb docs, args-aware `start_bg`, fix-list 3 and the item-6 remainder, `funding-watch.sh`'s standing FAIL. Next free decision id is **D-0084**.

### D-0084 — **The bad-proposal tool's bash-3.2 crash and wrong game-type default are fixed, with a regression test proven red on real bash 3.2 — not just asserted**
- **Context:** Fix for the two defects D-0083 recorded — the empty-array crash and the silent type-1 default — returned as ForteL2#143, reviewed here on a bash-3.2 host (the crash's actual environment, which CI does not have).
- **Finding 1 — the crash fix is correct and the regression genuinely bites.** The wrapper now expands `${FORWARD[@]+"${FORWARD[@]}"}` (unquoted outer, so an empty array yields **zero** args, not one blank arg — a Cursor High on the first draft's outer-quoted form, fixed in `12cd461`). Reviewer mutation on a bash-3.2 scratch clone: reverting to the bare `"${FORWARD[@]}"` turns the suite **red two ways** — the execution test (`no-arg form must reach go without crashing`, ec=1, bash 3.2 only) and a structural revert-check (`must not revert to bare "${FORWARD[@]}"`) that fails on **any** bash, so CI's 5.2 catches a future regression even though it cannot reproduce the crash itself. That structural belt-and-braces answers Codex's standing P2 (CI can't run 3.2). Both go green with the fix in place.
- **Finding 2 — the game-type default is fail-closed, and the worker declined two tempting shortcuts correctly.** Both wrapper and Go tool now **refuse** a missing/unparseable game type rather than defaulting to 1: `resolveGameType` returns an error, the wrapper exits before `go run`. Reviewer mutation: reinstating `${PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE:-1}` turns `unset PROPOSER_GAME_TYPE must refuse before go run` red. The worker did **not** hardcode 8 (wrong after the next respected-type change) and did **not** add an on-chain `respectedGameType` read to the proven create path (RPC/ABI risk) — an explicit type-1 remains the operator's choice, never a silent one. Correct calls, both flagged in the handoff.
- **Finding 3 — verification reproduced, on the environment that matters.** Scratch clone on **bash 3.2.57**: `bash -n` pass, parity exit 0, helpers **239 PASS** (232 + 7 additive, matching the report), `go test ./cmd/bad-proposal` ok. The BASH_ENV stub approach (stubbing go/cast/jq so the real wrapper reaches `go run` without broadcasting) is sound — it was necessary because lib.sh prepends `/opt/homebrew/bin` to PATH, so plain PATH stubs would lose to a real `go`; the handoff disclosed exactly this. The create/self-check/corruptRoot logic was untouched (proven on-chain in D-0083) and not re-run.
- **Decision:** **#143 approved.** The tool is now runnable in its documented no-arg form on the operator's shell and cannot silently post a non-respected game type. No existing check was modified (confirmed: SHARED FILES additive only).
- **Consequence:** The only remaining regen step is **13** (US-075, closeout note). Carried follow-ups unchanged: `.env.sepolia.example` challenger/proxy/safedb docs, args-aware `start_bg`, fix-list 3 and the item-6 remainder, `funding-watch.sh` FAIL. Next free decision id is **D-0085**.

### D-0085 — **Step 13 written: the closeout note ships, and the whole regen arc — wipe through fault-proof demonstration — is complete**
- **Context:** US-075, the operator-voice trust-model note, is the last step of the Phase 7 regen. Drafted by the planner, reviewed and approved in-voice by the operator, shipped as a README subsection.
- **Decision:** **Step 13 is done and Phase 7's operator sequence is complete.** README § "Fault proofs: what this proved, and what I still trust (US-075)" records both demonstrated games (66 honest/unattacked, 69 false/defeated) with their addresses and the reproduce/pass guidance, and states the trust boundary plainly: the fault proof judges state validity only — sequencer ordering/liveness, Sepolia DA permanence, and single-key custody (the ADMIN key holding L1ProxyAdminOwner + Guardian is the sharpest remaining single point of failure) are still trusted. The `derivation/` leftover is stated explicitly: it still compares to the operator's own node, so a won dispute is not independent verification for a counterparty until US-P7-005. Both US-075 acceptance criteria met.
- **Consequence:** **The regen is closed.** Every step from the 2026-08-22 wipe (D-0068) through the type-8 registration (D-0077), the valid-game watch (D-0082), the bad-proposal defeat (D-0083), and this closeout is done. What remains is only the non-blocking cleanup tail, none of it gating anything: `.env.sepolia.example` documentation for the challenger/proxy/safedb env set; the args-aware `start_bg` idea (D-0081 Finding 2); fix-list **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check) and the item-**6** remainder (F7-9, loader-level duplicate detection, `test-helpers.sh` coverage for `phase7-preflight.sh`, that script's mode 644 + README bare-path invocation); and `funding-watch.sh`'s standing **FAIL** with the funder endpoint `status=failing`, unaddressed since R-12. The recovery machinery (D-0074→D-0076) remains a shrinking type-1 tail draining toward idle, as anticipated. Next free decision id is **D-0086**.

### D-0086 — **The Phase 7 challenger/proxy/safedb env set is templated, secret-safe, and now has a tripwire against future leaks**
- **Context:** The `.env.sepolia.example` cleanup carried since D-0082/D-0085 — templating the challenger, batch-proxy, safedb, and 8b vars this session added to the live env by hand. Returned as ForteL2#145; reviewed here. Low-stakes docs, one real hazard (leaking a secret into the tracked template), which is why it got the hard look.

The `DEMO_A/B_PRIVATE_KEY` keys a loose review grep first flagged as "present" are **pre-existing commented placeholders** (main lines 81–84), untouched by this PR — the only DEMO reference in the diff is the worker's comment explaining they are deliberately excluded. `FUNDING_HEALTH_TOKEN` absent. All twelve in-scope challenger/proxy/safedb/8b keys present (active or commented, as fits each), no duplicate active keys.
- **Finding 2 — two Codex P2s, both fixed at head and confirmed by reading the code.** (a) The tripwire's first draft used a non-quiet `grep -nE '(PRIVATE_KEY|TOKEN)=.+'` that would print a leaked secret to CI logs; head (`e707e47`) switched to `grep -q` for detection and prints **names only** via `sed 's/=.*$//'`. (b) A second `L1_BEACON_URL` placeholder risked the D-0065 "last empty assignment wins on source" hazard; head drops it, leaving one commented placeholder near the L1-RPC block. Both threads target the draft `33809de`; the head fixes are real, not label-resolved.
- **Finding 3 — verification reproduced.** Scratch clone: `set -a; source .env.sepolia.example` exits 0, parity exit 0, `test-helpers.sh` **241 PASS** (239 + 2 additive, matching the report). The two additive tests assert the in-scope keys are documented and that `PRIVATE_KEY`/`TOKEN` lines stay empty — a standing tripwire against a future secret paste, with the redaction from Finding 2.
- **Decision:** **#145 approved.** The status is complete-with-caveats, but the caveats are all sound design disclosures (optional knobs left commented so an empty `KEY=` cannot override a binary default; `FAULT_GAME_ABSOLUTE_PRESTATE` commented per F7-6; `L1_BEACON_URL` documented via the single commented placeholder), not defects.
- **Consequence:** The challenger/proxy/safedb setup is now reproducible from the template. Remaining cleanup tail: fix-list **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check), the item-**6** remainder (F7-9, loader duplicate detection, `phase7-preflight.sh` coverage + mode 644), the pre-existing `DEMO_*`/`FUNDING_*` template gap (two are secrets — a separate careful task), and **`funding-watch.sh`'s standing FAIL** (funder endpoint `status=failing`), unaddressed since R-12 and next in the queue. Next free decision id is **D-0087**.

### D-0087 — **The R-12 funding-watch FAIL is closed: it was a self-recovered funder outage plus a real cry-wolf defect, and the defect is now fixed**
- **Context:** `funding-watch.sh`'s standing FAIL, carried since R-12, was the oldest open item. The planner measured it before dispatching (verdict is **OK today** — the ChainBank funder recovered on its own: endpoint `status=ok`, our wallet ok, batcher 1.78 ETH, last run 3.7 h ago) and found the real, latent defect underneath. Fixed as ForteL2#146.
- **Finding 1 — the defect was the script escalating an advisory aggregate label to a hard FAIL, against its own trust model.** At the old L271–274 the script did `if endpoint status == "failing": emit FAIL` unconditionally — while the line's own comment and the header (L41–48) both say that rollup label aggregates four wallets and is advisory. Every fact-based FAIL (stale run, our wallet blocked/failed, not_reconciled-and-draining) already ran above it. So a `failing` label about *someone else's* wallet forced a ForteL2 FAIL even with our wallet ok and balance healthy — the R-12 symptom, and a cry-wolf that trains the operator to ignore the one monitor guarding silent funder death. The fix folds `failing` into the existing `degraded`→WARN branch **inside** the `balance >= policy` guard; below policy, the advisory label no longer short-circuits the existing WARN/FAIL ladder.
- **Finding 2 — the fact-based FAILs were verified preserved, by probe, not by trust.** Reviewer ran four offline fixtures against the fixed script (fresh vs stale run, wallet ok vs blocked, above vs below policy): `failing`+fresh+ok+above-policy → **WARN, exit 0** (the fix); `failing`+wallet-blocked → **FAIL** (fact); `failing`+stale-run → **FAIL** (fact); `failing`+below-policy-27.8h → **FAIL** (ladder). Exit contract confirmed directly: WARN→0, FAIL→1, so launchd still sees a nonzero on a real failure.
- **Finding 3 — the existing test was strengthened, correctly and disclosed.** The old `ep-failing.json` assertion matched the substring `status=failing` in output — present regardless of verdict — and was named "fails ... despite healthy balance," which it never actually tested (its FAIL came from a months-stale `lastRun`, not the label). It now asserts `VERDICT: FAIL` + `last finished run`, correctly pinning it to the stale-run fact path, and a new fixture isolates the advisory→WARN case. A loose assertion made precise: a strengthening.
- **Verification:** scratch clone, `bash -n` pass, parity exit 0, `test-helpers.sh` **244 PASS** (241 + 3 additive, matching the report). CI green including Bugbot; no threads.
- **Decision:** **#146 approved; the R-12 funding-watch item is closed.** The advisory aggregate label is now WARN-not-FAIL when our own facts are healthy; a genuinely broken funder still FAILs on the facts.
- **Consequence:** Remaining cleanup tail, none blocking: fix-list **3** (`deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` pairing check), the item-**6** remainder (F7-9, loader duplicate detection, `phase7-preflight.sh` coverage + mode 644), the pre-existing `DEMO_*`/`FUNDING_*` template gap (two are secrets — a careful task), and the still-open **no alerting on a dead recovery agent / on funding-watch's own verdict** (there is no notifier wired to either; a genuinely broken funder now returns FAIL/exit 1 but nothing watches that exit). Next free decision id is **D-0088**.

### D-0088 — **The deposit script now refuses a wrong signer before it moves value — fix-list 3 closed, the last silent-failure gap on a money path**
- **Context:** Fix-list item 3 (D-0064 Finding 4, exercised for real in D-0069 Finding 6): `deposit-eth-sepolia.sh` sent an L1→L2 deposit signed by `ADMIN_PRIVATE_KEY` but never checked the key derived `ADMIN_ADDRESS`. Because `OptimismPortal.receive()` credits `msg.sender`, a mismatched key mints ETH to the wrong L2 wallet — a successful tx and a zero balance, with nothing pointing at the cause. Returned as ForteL2#147.
- **Finding 1 — the fix mirrors F7-10 and gates the send, verified by ordering and by probe.** `require_admin_key_matches_address` (the same shape as the 02-deploy and 09-challenger copies) is called at L39, before `wait_for_rpc` (L57) and the `cast send` (L64) — so a wrong signer is refused before any network call or value movement. Reviewer probe on the extracted function, offline: a matching key/address → exit 0; a mismatch → exit 1 naming the derived and configured **addresses**. Refuse-before-send holds.
- **Finding 2 — no key leak, asserted independently.** The mismatch error prints addresses only; the reviewer confirmed the private key does not appear in the output — not in full and not as any 8-character slice — matching the F7-10 no-leak property the worker's tests also assert (`_f710_key_leaked`). `cast wallet address --private-key` puts the key on argv for one short-lived process, the same class of bounded exposure the deposit `cast send` already has — not new.
- **Finding 3 — verification reproduced.** Scratch clone: `bash -n` pass, parity exit 0, `test-helpers.sh` **249 PASS** (244 + 5 additive, matching the report). Existing checks unmodified; scope was the two permitted files. The can-fail proof (commenting the call turns the ordering and mismatch tests red) is in the handoff and consistent with the code.
- **Finding 4 — one deferred micro-follow-up, correctly not silently widened.** Codex asked to document the pairing precondition in README / SOS onboarding; the worker left it (the brief scoped to the script + tests), replied, and resolved the thread. A reasonable separate doc follow-up, not a defect.
- **Decision:** **#147 approved; fix-list 3 is closed.** All three value-moving Sepolia scripts (02-deploy, 09-challenger, deposit) now pair key to address before spending.
- **Consequence:** Remaining cleanup tail, none blocking: the **lib.sh dedup** of the now-three copies of `require_admin_key_matches_address` (deferred deliberately — CODEOWNERS-gated lib.sh + proven-script risk); the item-**6** set (F7-9, loader duplicate detection, `phase7-preflight.sh` coverage + mode 644); the **DEMO_*/FUNDING_* template gap** (two are secrets); a README sentence on the deposit pairing precondition (Finding 4); and **alerting** on funding-watch's FAIL/exit-1 and a dead recovery agent — the highest-value remaining item, but it needs an operator decision on the alert destination before it is dispatchable. Next free decision id is **D-0089**.

### D-0089 — **Ops alerting ships: a funding-watch FAIL and a dead recovery agent now notify the operator instead of waiting to be noticed**
- **Context:** The highest-value item in D-0088's cleanup tail — funding-watch's verdict was correct but unobserved (`refresh_health.sh` swallows the exit with `|| true`; R-12 proved a FAIL can stand for days), and the hourly bond-recovery agent could die silently. Operator settled the destinations (macOS banner + email) and the mechanism (Resend HTTP API, existing account; secret deliberately named `RESEND_API_TOKEN` so the #145 tripwire covers it). Returned as ForteL2#148; reviewed here.
- **Finding 1 — fail-closed bias verified by probe, not prose.** FAIL verdict → banner + email with the verdict reason; an *unrecognized* verdict alerts as health-stale (head commit strengthened this from silently passing); missing `RESEND_API_TOKEN` → banners still fire, email skipped with a visible warning, exit 1 so launchd's err log records it. OK/WARN/INSUFFICIENT stay silent (the cry-wolf class #146 removed).
- **Finding 2 — cooldown correctness probed.** An immediate rerun of the same condition sends nothing (`to send: 0`); a distinct second condition alerts immediately while the first is inside its cooldown; keys are per condition × channel, and cooldown entries are pruned when a condition clears so a recurrence alerts at once.
- **Finding 3 — no-leak and live launchd parse verified independently.** Token absent from shim-recorded curl argv and from curl's child environment (`env -u`), present only on stdin (`--header @-`). On the operator's Mac, `launchctl print` emits exactly `last exit code = 0` and a missing job returns rc 113 — the read-only liveness parse matches real output, closing the "never-run-live" gap for that path. The live Resend send and the launchd install remain deliberately operator-owned (`--test` shakeout).
- **Finding 4 — verification reproduced, delta explained.** Scratch clone at head `e01534b`: test-helpers **263 PASS 0 FAIL** (main: 249; report said 260 because it was written at `f8ab95c` — the follow-up commit is a self-caught strengthening: resolve-games threshold 2.5 h → 2 h, without which the 02:30 run would miss the second missed cycle and defer the alert to 03:30, plus the unknown-verdict alerting, plus 3 tests). Parity 60 PASS exit 0; `bash -n`, `plutil -lint`, example-env source all pass. Shared files additive only; no existing checks modified.
- **Decision:** **#148 approved.** The caveats are the operator-owned shakeout steps, not defects.
- **Consequence:** The R-12 blind-spot class is closed once the operator installs the agent and runs `--test` (README § Ops alerting: fill `RESEND_API_TOKEN` / `ALERT_EMAIL_TO` in local `.env.sepolia`, copy plist, bootout+bootstrap, `--test`). Whether `onboarding@resend.dev` delivers to the configured recipient is answered by that `--test`; if it bounces, swap `ALERT_EMAIL_FROM` after domain verification. Remaining tail unchanged: lib.sh dedup of `require_admin_key_matches_address`, the item-6 set, the DEMO_*/FUNDING_* template gap, the README deposit-pairing sentence. Next free decision id is **D-0090**.

### D-0090 — **The pairing guard lives in lib.sh once, the loader refuses duplicate assignments for every script, and the preflight gate survived the move fail-closed**
- **Context:** The guard-consolidation task from D-0088/D-0089's cleanup tail: dedup the F7-10 key/address pairing (measured as two named copies plus one challenger inline variant, not the "three identical copies" earlier entries recorded), add loader-level duplicate detection (D-0066 Finding 5 — duplicates belong in the loader, absence does not), and repoint the two consumers that awk-extract the guard's body from script text (`phase7-preflight.sh`, the `test-helpers.sh` F7-10 block). Returned as ForteL2#151; reviewed here.
- **Finding 1 — the guarded properties survived the dedup, probed not assumed.** `require_key_matches_address <KEY_VAR> <ADDR_VAR> [extra]` in lib.sh: refusal ordering unchanged at all three call sites (before spend/wipe/daemon start); mismatch names both addresses and the variable names with zero key leakage (probed: no substring of a known key in output); the challenger's role-warning line preserved via the third argument; message text byte-identical to the old copies for the ADMIN sites.
- **Finding 2 — the loader refusal is surgical in both directions.** Probes beyond the shipped tests: an `export`-prefixed duplicate is caught (names + line numbers, exit 1); a value containing `=` does not false-refuse; a duplicated secret **value** appears nowhere in the refusal output; and with the refusal deliberately neutered the duplicate loads silently — the probe can fail. Known edge, recorded not fixed: a multi-line quoted value whose continuation line looks like `name=value` would miscount; committed env convention is single-line.
- **Finding 3 — preflight is still a fail-closed gate after the repoint, and got two hardening fixes beyond the brief.** Fixture-root runs: green path executes all six checks (`ALL CHECKS PASSED`); renaming the lib.sh helper yields `COULD NOT RUN — treat as failure` → `NOT CLEAR TO PROCEED`. The F7-10 check no longer puts the admin key on argv **or** in any temp file (child reads the env file by path and `eval`s the two `ADMIN_*` lines — closes Codex P1); the mode-600 check is now GNU-portable via Python `S_IMODE` (Cursor's finding: `stat -f` is `--file-system` on Ubuntu, so the old fallback never ran). Mode 100755 is in the commit.
- **Finding 4 — verification reproduced; bot threads verified against head; one repeat process nit.** Scratch clone at `b884dff`: **281 PASS 0 FAIL** (main: 263; 278 at report-time `ea65830` — the +3 and the two undeclared README additions are the post-report commit answering Codex P2/Cursor). All CI green on ubuntu (empirical cross-platform proof for the new tests). All three bot findings are real fixes at head, not label-resolutions. Process nit, second occurrence after #148: refresh the handoff fields when pushing after the report is written.
- **Decision:** **#151 approved.** lib.sh (CODEOWNERS) changes reviewed here; merge is the operator's.
- **Consequence:** The F7-10 guard has one implementation; every script that sources lib.sh — including the hourly bond-recovery agent — now refuses a duplicated active env assignment at load, names-only. **Operator post-merge shakeout (unproven against the live env file by design, D-0049):** one ordinary script run (e.g. `status.sh`) to prove the loader accepts the real `.env.sepolia`, then one `scripts/phase7-preflight.sh` run to prove the repointed gate passes live — if the loader refuses, the error names the duplicated variable and the fix is deleting the extra line. Remaining tail: sibling tasks A (docs/deposit-note-and-funding-env) and C (ci/prestate-negative-control) in review. lib.sh dedup and item-6's loader/preflight/mode items are closed by this entry; F7-9 closes with C. Next free decision id is **D-0091**.

### D-0092 — **F7-9 lands: the prestate build's check 2 is a decisive negative control, and the custom artifact cannot be clobbered by the stock build**
- **Context:** The last item of the cleanup tail: replace `build-prestate.yml`'s probabilistic image-grep (page-straddle false-FAIL risk, encoding drift) with a second stock-config build whose commitment must differ — the design D-0062 named and D-0063 Finding 5 costed (~4 min per build, not 45–90). Returned as ForteL2#150; reviewed here.
- **Finding 1 — the decisive check ran for real, on the PR head.** Workflow run `32861647266` (sha `5a131eda`, 6m33s): custom `0x034c90f0…9796b8d`, stock `0x03a7916a…9844a0f` — present, well-formed, different; check 1 gates against the preserved custom outputs; the summary labels the stock hash "negative control; not for pinning" and keeps the smoke-test caveat. Run-log values match the handoff exactly.
- **Finding 2 — the D-0063-Finding-5 trap is closed by construction, twice.** Both builds share one justfile OUTPUT dir; the workflow copies each build's outputs to `prestate-custom/` / `prestate-stock/` immediately after each build (custom is preserved before the stock build exists), and upload + summary + check 1 read only `prestate-custom/`. A dedicated step additionally asserts the upload source's `.pre` equals check 1's commitment. A stock prestate can no longer ship under the trusted artifact name.
- **Finding 3 — the injection toggle was verified against the pinned justfile, not assumed.** The build gates on a *non-empty* `KONA_CUSTOM_CONFIGS_DIR`; `KONA_CUSTOM_CONFIGS=false` with the dir still set would still inject. The stock step `unset`s both — and the differing hashes are the empirical proof injection was actually off (identical hashes would have failed the job).
- **Finding 4 — the unexercised fail direction was closed in review.** The worker skipped proving "identical ⇒ red" (a second full Docker pair). Reviewer probe on the check-2 logic with fabricated proof files: identical → exit 1, check1-mismatch → exit 1, malformed → exit 1, missing file → exit 1, different → exit 0. The grep and its stock-source discriminator were removed rather than kept — keeping the grep would reintroduce the false-FAIL F7-9 exists to remove; declared and accepted as a strengthening.
- **Decision:** **#150 approved.** `workflow_dispatch`-only, the pinned monorepo SHA, digest-pinned actions, `contents: read`, and the kona-client-int exclusion all survive.
- **Consequence:** **The Phase 7 cleanup tail is empty.** With #148 (alerting), #149 (docs/env), #151 (guard consolidation) and this merge, everything carried from D-0088 is closed: F7-9 was the last item. At the next real step-8b-class prestate build, the operator gets a decisive custom≠stock proof instead of a grep. Open micro-items, unassigned: funding-watch `--help` sed range (D-0091), the multi-line-quoted-value scanner edge (D-0090). Next free decision id is **D-0093**.

### D-0093 — **help-range-and-scanner-edge lands: --help is content-anchored and the dup scanner survives quoted multi-line values, trailing comments, and escaped quotes**
- **Context:** The two unassigned micro-items from the cleanup tail (D-0091 3a: funding-watch `--help` sed range; D-0090 F2: multi-line-quoted-value scanner edge). Returned as ForteL2#152 off `a27c625`; reviewed here.
- **Finding 1 — gate recomputed at head in an isolated clone:** 290 PASS 0 FAIL (281 on main + 9 additive; the handoff said 288 — the extra 2 are tests added by the post-report fix commit `e7379c5`, the same pattern as #148/#151). `phase7-gate-parity.sh` 60 PASS exit 0; `bash -n` clean; all five CI checks green at head.
- **Finding 2 — the scanner fix probed in both directions:** main's scanner false-refuses the double-quoted multi-line fixture naming BAR (ec=1, probe-can-fail proven); the new scanner emits FOO/BAZ only and accepts (ec=0); a real duplicate after a *closed* multi-line value is still refused naming the right variable and lines.
- **Finding 3 — bots caught a real in-flight fail-open; fixed before this review:** Codex (P1) and Cursor (Medium) both flagged, at `224ac9d`, that an apostrophe in a trailing comment (`FOO=one # don't …`) left the scanner in single-quote state, silently skipping every later assignment — a genuine bypass of the duplicate guard. `e7379c5` stops quote tracking at an unquoted `#` comment and honors `\` escapes inside double quotes, with refusal tests for both shapes. Verified fixed at head; these were correct findings fixed in response, not false positives.
- **Finding 4 — the remaining shell-emulation gaps are fail-closed:** where the scanner still diverges from bash (unclosed quote at EOF; a mid-word `#` whose apostrophe opens a quote, e.g. `FOO=bar#don't`), the scan accepts but `source` itself fails loudly (unexpected EOF, ec=2) — the loader still refuses the file, with bash's error instead of the guard's. The handoff's residual note "escaped `\"` not emulated" is stale in the safe direction: `e7379c5` does emulate it, with a test.
- **Finding 5 — the help fix went wider than the brief, correctly:** `alert-watch.sh --help` was also truncated (sed `2,50p` vs a header ending at line 54, omitting `ALERT_WATCH_HEALTH_STALE_SECS`/`SLEEP_GRACE_SECS` and the `CURL|OSASCRIPT|LAUNCHCTL` test knobs). Both scripts now use the same content-anchored awk bound; tests assert the full env-key table, exit 0, no script-body dump, and a regression test refuses any future hard-coded `sed -n '2,Np'` range.
- **Decision:** **#152 approved.**
- **Consequence:** D-0090 F2 and D-0091 3a are closed; **the backlog is empty.** One portability fact worth carrying: `_scan_quote_state_after` uses bash-only `${s:i:1}` substring indexing — every lib.sh consumer has a bash shebang, so fine today, but lib.sh must not be sourced from zsh (a reviewer probe in zsh failed on exactly this). Next free decision id is **D-0094**.

### D-0094 — **US-P7-005 commits to Path A: self-derived genesis replay, compared against the operator's on-chain proposals**
- **Context:** Backlog empty after #152; scoping the counterparty-verification gap D-0085 left open. Code reading found the verifier tethered to the operator's node **twice**: the expected hash comes from `-ref-l2` (the operator's own EL, `verify.go:84`), and mid-chain anchors are copies of the operator's datadir. Path B (counterparty-replica anchor) alone fixes neither honestly: independence would rest on stock op-node, it needs counterparty infra that is itself blocked on this story, and the answer key would still be the operator's hashes.
- **Decision:** **Path A.** One-time genesis replay (chain is 3 days post-wipe, ~130k blocks — it will never be cheaper), then the derivation EL's own datadir is the anchor forever (self-anchored incremental audits). The comparison oracle becomes the operator's **type-8 dispute-game proposals on L1** (root claim vs output root computed from the seal EL) — the staked claim is what a counterparty needs audited. Spec: `tasks/frd-us-p7-005-independent-derivation.md` (T1 replay spike → T2 proposal-compare → T3 docs; T1∥T2 allowed, disjoint files). PRD checkbox 1 ("spec chooses one") is satisfied by this entry + the FRD.
- **Consequence:** Counterparty docs keep consistency-only language until T3 (PRD checkbox 3 unchanged). The replica pack stays blocked until T3. T2 gets the metered Codex review (honesty path). Review ids allocate sequentially from here. Next free decision id is **D-0095**.

### D-0095 — **public-viewer lands: the pipeline viewer gains a hosted read-only build on the two D-0047 gateways**
- **Context:** PR #154 (`feat/public-viewer`). Probes from the planning session showed the D-0045 gateways are CORS-open (`access-control-allow-origin: *`) but their method allowlist rejects `txpool_status` and `optimism_syncStatus`, while `eth_getBlockByNumber` block tags (`latest`/`safe`/`finalized`) work on the replica — enough to rebuild the sequencer panel without a new public RPC. Reviewer round 1 found the build script's destructive-path refusal incomplete: `PUBLIC_VIEWER_OUT=scripts` deleted the tracked `scripts/` directory and exited 0. Fix (`fe4a63a`): the destination must be absent, empty, or carry the `Content-Security-Policy.txt` marker from a previous bundle; marker is written first so a crashed rebuild stays regenerable.
- **Decision:** The public build is assembled by `scripts/build-public-viewer.sh` from **committed public constants only** (`viewer/config.public.js`) — never from `.env*`, the deploy tree, or gitignored `viewer/config.js`. Origins are exactly the D-0047 pair plus `https://ethereum-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com` for L1, enforced by a build-time allowlist guard and unit tests; public mode never calls the two forbidden methods (mempool shows n/a; sequencer heads derive from block tags) and has a 30 s poll floor. The local loopback flow (`gen-viewer-config.sh` → `serve-viewer.sh` → :8081) is unchanged. Hosting is an operator step on Render: static site, build command `./scripts/build-public-viewer.sh`, publish dir `viewer/public`, watch paths `viewer/**`.
- **Consequence:** No third public RPC exists — D-0047's cap holds. Reviewer verification on the main-merged base: script helpers **297/0**, node tests **49/0**; adversarial probes (`PUBLIC_VIEWER_OUT=scripts`, `viewer/fonts`, `viewer`, `.`, trailing slash, `/.`, `../` traversal, symlink-to-`scripts`) all refuse with the tree intact; QuickNode-shaped and rogue-origin injections fail the build; live render against the real gateways verified in round 1. After the Phase 7 wipe, the committed addresses in `viewer/config.public.js` must be updated with the new deployment before the hosted page is truthful again. Next free decision id is **D-0096**.

### D-0096 — **viewer-polish lands: fold-friendly layout, honest batcher window, clean console**
- **Context:** PR #156 (`feat/viewer-polish`), four riders on the D-0095 public viewer. The hero pushed all four status panels below the fold; the public batcher panel scanned 12 L1 blocks against a measured ~25-block (~5 min) posting cadence and read "0 / none yet" half the time; the live site 404'd on `/favicon.ico`; and the meta-delivered `frame-ancestors` logged a browser warning on every load.
- **Decision:** `viewerL1ScanBlocks` is mode-split — **public 36 / local 12 / Anvil 40** — so the public panel always covers a full batching interval while the local viewer's QuickNode L1 spend is unchanged (the split is unit-tested, not commented). The hero collapses to one line with the lag/nightly facts in a footer; favicon ships as committed `.ico`+`.svg` (no new origin); the build's meta-CSP injection strips exactly `frame-ancestors` while `Content-Security-Policy.txt` keeps the full policy as the future dashboard-header source. Reviewer rider: `viewer/public/` is gitignored (generated output was triggering uncommitted-changes prompts on branch switch).
- **Consequence:** Reviewer verification on the main-merged base: node tests **54/0**, script helpers **299/0**, destructive-path refusals still hold (`PUBLIC_VIEWER_OUT=scripts` → exit 1, tree intact), meta CSP lost only `frame-ancestors`, and a real 1366×768 browser render showed all four panels above the fold with a zero-message console and the batcher reporting "1 in last 36 L1 blocks". D-0095's other invariants are unchanged. The live static site picks this up on the next manual deploy. Next free decision id is **D-0097**.



### D-0097 — **T1 self-anchor lands complete-with-caveats: the mechanism is proven by test and probe, the live measurement is a follow-up, and one disclosed scope excursion is accepted**
- **Context:** US-P7-005 T1 (FRD §4), returned as ForteL2#157 off `8faa389`. Handoff arrived off-format but complete in substance: code done, CI green, live measurement unfinished (two window-1 runs died in `cmd/verify` on an empty L1 JSON-RPC result after the ~50–58 min genesis→tip inbox scan; the Mac's ~23:45 sleep threatens long runs). Operator directed merge-on-green with the measurement as follow-up.
- **Finding 1 — gate recomputed at head (`b51cb4e`) in an isolated clone:** 307 PASS 0 FAIL (299 main + 8), parity 60 exit 0, `go test ./...` green, `bash -n` clean, all five CI checks green.
- **Finding 2 — the invariant probed adversarially:** contiguity refuses re-deriving the last block (start=head), gaps (head+2), restart-from-1 over a populated datadir, and start>1 on an empty datadir; only exact head+1 (or 1 at genesis) resumes. Mutual exclusion with `--anchor-datadir`/`--make-anchor` errors by name; the reference-copy helper is unreachable in self-anchor mode; legacy windows are byte-identical (safe_l2 window logic moved intact under `SELF_ANCHOR=0`).
- **Finding 3 — the scope excursion is accepted, with attribution:** the brief said `derivation/*.go` untouched (T2's surface), the worker edited `derivation/rpc.go` anyway because the live crash was there. Mitigating: disclosed prominently, no `feat/proposal-compare` branch existed to collide with, and the change is a strict strengthening — empty JSON-RPC envelopes previously died as `json.Unmarshal("")`; now a named error, retried 3× with 200 ms backoff and context cancelation, then failed closed. Legitimate `"result":null` responses keep their old semantics (probed in tests). T2 therefore cuts from post-merge main and owns `derivation/*.go` from there.
- **Finding 4 — Codex P1 correctly declined as a landing gate:** it asked for the live ≥1000-block stop/resume before merge — a measurement the brief itself scheduled, not a correctness proof; the properties it would exercise are covered by tests and reviewer probes. Declined-with-reasoning, not ignored.
- **Decision:** **#157 approved for merge on green.** STATUS `complete-with-caveats` accepted; MEASUREMENT is an open follow-up, unassigned: seal rate over ≥1000 real blocks, projected genesis→head, live stop/resume proof (W1/W2 in flight, may die at sleep — that outcome is data, not failure).
- **Consequence:** T2 (`feat/proposal-compare`) unblocks on merge, baselined on post-merge main. Two notes carried to T2/T3: (a) the self-anchor resume head-hash check and the `--end-l2 ≤ safe_l2` bound both consult the **reference** node — correct in consistency mode, but proposal mode must demote them to informational or the operator oracle returns as a gate; (b) the ~50–58 min genesis→tip L1 scan dominates run cost and recurs per window — a persisted scan checkpoint is a candidate optimization, **operator decision after the numbers land, not scope for T2**. The checkpointing question (>12 h projection) stays open pending real measurement. Next free decision id is **D-0098**.

### D-0098 — **T2 proposal-compare lands complete-with-caveats: tether 1 is cut in code and fixture, the live factory audit is the named residual, and it gates T3's close**
- **Context:** US-P7-005 T2 (FRD §4 as corrected by #155), returned as ForteL2#158 off `7facf0e` (base still current at review). Live shakeout did not run — T1's W1 seal EL still held :19645/:19651 and a second seal EL must not start beside it; the worker isolated itself in a worktree (`/tmp/fortel2-proposal-compare`) rather than disturb W1, and left the shared checkout untouched.
