- **Finding 2 — the output-root preimage verified independently:** `cast keccak` over the 128-byte v0 preimage (32 zero bytes ‖ 0x11…‖ 0x22… ‖ 0x33…) reproduces the committed hand vector `0xd50bf2ff…78a3b2` exactly; layout matches the OP Stack v0 spec ordering. A wrong preimage here would have meant false MISMATCH against an honest operator forever — it is right.
- **Finding 3 — the fail-closed and no-default invariants hold in code and probe:** MISMATCH returns an error after the full report is built → exit 1 (`TestProposalMismatchIsError` plus code read); `-game-type` empty resolves `AnchorStateRegistry.respectedGameType()` on-chain, no numeric default anywhere (D-0063 3c / D-0083 honored); proposal mode blanks `-ref-l2`/`-ref-node` so the operator node is not an authority (D-0097 note implemented); `eth_getProof` refuses empty/zero codeHash (non-existent account ⇒ no plausible-wrong storage root). Reviewer probe: window bounds inclusive at exactly start and end, skip at start−1/end+1.
- **Finding 4 — the SAST fix is a strengthening inside owned surface:** pre-existing `exec.Command(bin)` in `runCmd`/`startCmd` now pins argv0 to `op-geth` with a basename refusal, tested both directions. Legacy `VerifyReport` JSON is unchanged (pointers + omitempty; zero game type still emitted — Bugbot's finding, fixed and tested).
- **Finding 5 — bot-thread dispositions verified:** Bugbot omitempty/type-0 and Codex P2 zero-Address were fixed in later commits (not false positives); Codex P1 (root README trust-bullet) was *declined with reasoning* — a partial trust-language edit while `derivation/README.md` Limitations still describes the old oracle is exactly the drift T3 exists to close. Correct call.
- **Decision:** **#158 approved for merge on green, complete-with-caveats.** The two DECISIONS NEEDED are resolved as follows: full-factory enumeration (`0..gameCount-1`) is accepted for this chain and recorded as a future scale item, unassigned; the Codex P1 decline stands. **The live `-compare proposals` run against the real Sepolia factory is a hard precondition to closing T3 / US-P7-005** — it is not waived, only sequenced: it runs after T1's W1/W2 frees the seal EL, from the worker's worktree, and its per-proposal lines land in the record before T3's review closes.
- **Consequence:** Tether 1 (operator node as answer key) is cut in code; what remains before US-P7-005 closes: T1's measurement follow-up (D-0097), the live T2 factory audit above, then T3 (docs/derivation-independence — Limitations rewrite, counterparty runbook, root README trust bullet, PRD checkboxes 2–3). The worktree `/tmp/fortel2-proposal-compare` stays until the live audit runs, then is removed. Next free decision id is **D-0099**.

### D-0099 — **The derivation RPC client survives throttled endpoints: timeout, rate-limit retries with backoff, and operator pacing — and the L1 endpoint duty is split**
- **Context:** Three live self-anchor runs died at the same phase boundary; the third hung ≥30 min (no HTTP timeout, `http.DefaultClient`) while the endpoint (QuickNode, behind Cloudflare) rate-limited — the scans had driven the QuickNode monthly quota to 100%. Returned as ForteL2#159 in two rounds: round 1 implemented a superseded brief (timeout only — reviewer probe: a 429 HTML body died on the first attempt, unretried); changes requested with the amended scope; round 2 (`ad2e822`) delivered it.
- **Finding 1 — gate recomputed at head in an isolated clone:** 311 PASS 0 FAIL (no movement vs main, as claimed), parity 60 exit 0, `go test ./...` green, CI green.
- **Finding 2 — the retryable class probed independently:** 429-with-HTML-body retries and succeeds (3 attempts observed); on exhausted retries the HTML body is never echoed into the error; a 60 s `Retry-After` wait is interrupted promptly by cancelation. Defaults: 10 attempts, 1 s→30 s exponential backoff with jitter (worst case ~3 min per call, logged to stderr so patience is distinguishable from a hang — the failure mode that cost 30 minutes).
- **Finding 3 — modified existing tests are harness-only:** `rpc_redact_test.go` switched to a tight test client (ms backoff) with zero assertion changes — verified by diff, not by the report.
- **Finding 4 — pacing is operator-owned:** `DERIVATION_RPC_MAX_RPS` (env, default unpaced) wired through `derivation-check.sh` in one additive line. Accepted residuals: JSON-RPC error codes (e.g. -32005) are not in the retryable class (HTTP 429/5xx and non-JSON bodies cover the providers in use); pacing is a min-interval, not a burst bucket. Both fine at this scale; revisit only if a provider throttles via JSON-RPC codes at HTTP 200.
- **Decision:** **#159 approved.** Also recorded here, operator ops decision of 2026-08-26: **L1 endpoint duty is split.** The stack was bridged to a free Alchemy Sepolia endpoint (`L1_RPC_URL`) + PublicNode beacon (`L1_BEACON_URL`) after the QuickNode cap; at QuickNode's billing refill the stack may return to QuickNode, but **genesis-scale scans never run against QuickNode again** — scans use the Alchemy key with `DERIVATION_RPC_MAX_RPS` set under its free-tier rate.
- **Consequence:** The US-P7-005 measurement rerun is unblocked on the merged client. Remaining before US-P7-005 closes: the L1 scan-checkpoint task (scan only the delta since last run — the recurring-cost fix the operator's budget objection decided), then the measurement, the live factory audit (D-0098 precondition), then T3. Next free decision id is **D-0100**.

### D-0100 — **l1-scan-checkpoint lands complete-with-caveats: the resumed scan bound is derived, spec-correct, and probed; the live delta proof rides the measurement rerun**
- **Context:** The recurring-cost fix D-0099 queued: every window rescanned L1 genesis→tip (~50–58 min, the endpoint-budget killer). Returned as ForteL2#160 off `95ca07dd` (base current at review), STATUS complete-with-caveats: the live resumed-window proof was still in flight at hand-back (W1 genesis run at `DERIVATION_RPC_MAX_RPS=2`).
- **Finding 1 — gate recomputed at head (`6a5cf10`) in an isolated clone:** 318 PASS 0 FAIL (311 + 7 additive), parity 60 exit 0, `go test ./...` green, `bash -n` clean.
- **Finding 2 — the design is the briefed one, verified in code:** no stored checkpoint — the bound is derived every run from the sealed head's own L1-info (`resolveResumeInboxScan` parses block M's first tx; the sealed head cannot lie about what was derived); `channel_timeout` read from the rollup config with a hard refusal when 0; explicit `-from-l1`, `--channel-tx`, and start≤1 all bypass it; legacy lookback byte-identical when the flag is off; wired into both consistency and proposal modes.
- **Finding 3 — the spec inequality and edges probed independently:** channel timed out iff `current > open + CHANNEL_TIMEOUT` ⇒ a channel open at origin(M) opened at ≥ origin(M)−timeout; scan start origin(M)−timeout−1 includes it. Reviewer probes: normal case, exact clamp at genesis.l1, one-above-genesis, origin below the subtrahend, and the uint64 overflow guard — all correct. Under-scan stays loud ("missing derived block N in window"), over-scan is only cost.
- **Finding 4 — bot dispositions:** Codex's `--channel-tx` interaction was real and fixed in `6a5cf10` (the ChannelTx short-circuit); the root-README ask was correctly declined to T3 (same reasoning as D-0098 Finding 5). The modified test-helpers check is a strengthening (combined-options gate), verified by diff and count.
- **Decision:** **#160 approved for merge on green CI at `6a5cf10`.** The live caveat is sequenced, not waived: the resumed-window delta proof (scan start at the derived bound on a head with origin(M) > genesis.l1+301, wall-clock vs the ~50–58 min baseline) lands with the operator's measurement rerun and is recorded before US-P7-005 closes.
- **Consequence:** The plumbing for Path A is complete. Operator sequence from here: merge → rerun W1 from main at a saner pace (the in-flight W1 at 2 RPS projects ~10+ h and risks the 23:45 sleep; ~8–10 RPS stays under the Alchemy free tier and finishes in ~2–3 h) → resumed ≥1000-block W2 (delivers the D-0097 measurement AND this delta proof in one run) → live factory audit (D-0098) → T3 brief. Next free decision id is **D-0101**.

### D-0101 — **The epoch-hash fetch storm is fixed and regression-gated: window-scoped resolution plus a header cache turn a 25–50 h decode phase into minutes**
- **Context:** The root cause of the 2026-08-26 endpoint-cost incident, diagnosed by the planner mid-run from process evidence (3 h of continuous `eth_getBlockByNumber` after the inbox scan completed, stack samples hot in JSON string decode, ~70 KB/s inbound, seal EL parked at 0). `deriveBlockInputs` resolved `EpochHash` **before** the window filter: span batches carry epoch numbers not hashes, so a genesis-scale decode fetched one L1 header per L2 block of the entire ~185k-block history — ~25–50 h at the throttled rate, and the same storm unpaced is what exhausted the QuickNode monthly quota across three earlier runs. A cost/scale defect, not a correctness one. Returned as ForteL2#161 off `b90f0364`.
- **Finding 1 — the fix is the briefed shape, verified in the diff:** the window classification (`blockNumberFromTimestamp` + start/end filter) now precedes the `EpochHash` resolution, with a comment recording *why* the order matters; elements are still not dropped inside span decode (the trap the brief named — continuity preserved). A `headerCache` by number and hash was added to `L1Client`, mirroring the existing `blobFeeCache`, so repeated origins (≈6 L2 blocks share one L1 origin) cost one fetch.
- **Finding 2 — the budget test was proven able to fail (the decisive check, since no live run gates this PR).** Reviewer reverted the reordering in an isolated clone and re-ran the test: **FAIL, "header fetches=2 want 1"** on the window cases; restoring the fix returns it to green. It is a genuine regression gate, not decoration. Construction inspected: a real `httptest` server counts actual `eth_getBlockByNumber` calls, and each case asserts both the input count and the fetch count, so a future refactor cannot silently reintroduce the storm.
- **Finding 3 — gate recomputed at head (`71000c8`) in an isolated clone:** `go test ./...` green with **zero modified assertions** (fixture-derived hashes unchanged ⇒ correctness property holds), `test-helpers.sh` 318 PASS 0 FAIL (no movement), parity 60 exit 0, CI green.
- **Finding 4 — the cache is bounded by the fix itself:** because only in-window origins are fetched, the memo holds O(K) headers (~370 for a 2,200-block window), not one per historical block; the storm and the unbounded-growth risk are removed by the same change.
- **Decision:** **#161 approved.** STATUS complete-with-caveats accepted: the live 1–2200 run is deliberately **operator-owned** (the operator amended the brief to reclaim the terminal after a day of unsteerable runs — the right call; the worker's premature scan was killed after ~1 min with the seal EL at genesis and the datadir intact, correctly disclosed).
- **Consequence:** The measurement rerun becomes affordable: the ~2¼ h inbox scan at 10 RPS is the only remaining fixed cost, and the post-scan phase should show **seal-EL liftoff within minutes** — that is the operator's live falsification test for this fix; if the height sits at 0 for ~30 min again, stop immediately rather than burning another evening. Remaining to close US-P7-005: W1 (measurement, D-0097), W2 resumed delta window (also proves D-0100 live), the factory audit (D-0098), then T3. Also recorded: two corrections to earlier entries — the 23:45 window is the **chain dev-sleep** (scheduled stack stop), not a Mac sleep, and its risk to long runs is the reference EL disappearing mid-seal, not frozen processes; and the #159 pacer shows a post-backoff burst (min-interval credit accumulation re-triggering 429s), noted unassigned. Next free decision id is **D-0102**.

### D-0102 — **US-P7-005 is parked; the L1 endpoint policy is reverted to QuickNode; the balance-read defect is the one item that survives**
- **Context:** Two days (2026-08-26/27) spent trying to land the US-P7-005 live proofs ended with the QuickNode monthly quota exhausted, a ~6 h chain outage, and no measurement. The operator called a stop. This entry records the decision, the incident, and the state everything is left in.
- **The planning failure, recorded so it is not repeated:** genesis-scale live runs were dispatched **without first establishing that genesis replay had ever worked on this chain.** It had not — the Sepolia golden fixtures are mid-chain windows from the *pre-wipe* chain, and the post-wipe redeploy (D-0068) has Holocene+Jovian active at genesis. A 20-block local window or one `--channel-tx` decode would have surfaced both defects below in minutes instead of after repeated ~2¼ h scans against a metered endpoint. **Rule for future work: before any run that costs money or hours, run the cheapest test that could falsify the plan.** A second rule from the same days: unmeasured durations must be reported as unmeasured, never as estimates the operator will schedule around.
- **Incident 1 — QuickNode exhausted.** Root cause was the latent epoch-hash fetch storm (D-0101): `deriveBlockInputs` resolved one L1 header per L2 block of the entire ~185k-block history before window filtering. Three genesis-scale runs into that pattern took the monthly quota to 100%. Fixed in #161, but the quota is spent.
- **Incident 2 — ~6 h chain outage (03:00 failed wake → 05:45 discovery).** The stack had been bridged to a **free Alchemy tier** as a stopgap; a 24/7 OP Stack (op-node polling, batcher, proposer, challenger, hourly agents) is not what a development tier is sized for. The 03:00 wake's funding preflight read **false balances** (batcher 0.145, proposer 0.0087) and fail-closed, tearing down the whole stack. **The wallets were never low:** archive reads at the exact block the wake logged (11577135) show proposer 4.034788608877127862 ETH and batcher 1.739993085535783340 ETH, and a control read at 11570000 returns a *different* historical value (4.053661808106809022), proving the endpoint serves true archive rather than echoing latest. The outage was caused by a bad *reading*, not a bad balance.
- **Incident 2, corrected diagnosis (Codex P1 on #162, verified — the planner's first diagnosis was wrong and is superseded here).** The initial claim was that `require_min_balance_eth` discards `cast balance`'s exit status, so an RPC failure yields an empty value and a false "underfunded". **That is not what happens.** Every caller runs `set -euo pipefail`, and `bal_wei="$(cast balance ...)"` is a plain assignment, so a failing `cast` propagates its status and **aborts the script outright** - verified: the statement after such an assignment never executes (exit 2). Therefore the observed messages, which carry specific numbers, prove `cast balance` **succeeded and returned incorrect values**. The real defect is that the gate trusts a *successful-but-wrong* read; making failures louder would not have prevented this outage. Most probable cause, consistent with the numbers: a **lagging backend** behind the provider's load balancer serving stale state (the proposer held ~0 before its funding and 4.05 after, so 0.0087 fits a stale view from that window). Provenance is not proven - the public endpoint lacks the retention to test it directly.
- **Decision (operator, 2026-08-27):** **US-P7-005 is parked**, not cancelled. Nothing depends on it; it closes a gap that only becomes real when a pilot counterparty asks for independent verification. **L1 endpoint policy reverts to QuickNode for the live stack** (D-0099's split is withdrawn in the direction that mattered: free-tier endpoints are for one-off scans, never for the 24/7 stack).
- **What is already merged and stays:** #157 self-anchor + resume contiguity, #158 proposal-compare (the independence mechanism itself, fixture-proven), #159 RPC timeout/rate-limit/pacing (**valuable independent of US-P7-005** — the client can no longer hang forever), #160 L1 delta-scan bound, #161 the fetch-storm fix. All are dormant, not broken.
- **What is diagnosed but unfixed, and deliberately left so:** the **eip1559Params/extraData defect** — `attrs.go` passes `system_config.eip1559Params` verbatim, but this chain's value is zero, which per Holocene means "use the canyon defaults" (`chain_op_config`: denominator 250, elasticity 6, confirmed against reference block 1's `extraData` `0x01000000fa000000060000000000000000`). Derivation reads `chain_op_config` **nowhere**. Every derived block on this chain therefore mismatches. This blocks any future genesis or self-anchored derivation and must be fixed **first** if US-P7-005 is ever resumed. Also left: the poisoned derivation datadir (`$DATA_DIR/l2/derivation-op-geth` holds a block 1 with the wrong hash — wipe before any future run), the self-anchor's missing resume-vs-reference sanity check, and the #159 pacer's post-backoff burst.
- **Consequence:** **The one item that survives as active work is the balance-gate staleness defect** (`require_min_balance_eth`, `scripts/lib.sh`): the gate accepts any syntactically valid balance from a single unpinned read and will tear down the entire stack on it. It guards five call sites (batcher, proposer, admin, challenger, start-all). The corrective direction is to **pin the block** - reading balance at an explicit recent L1 head forces a lagging node to serve that block's true state or error, converting silent staleness into a loud failure - plus corroboration before a fail-closed teardown, since refusing to start is high-consequence. It is unrelated to US-P7-005, it caused Incident 2, and it will recur. Brief: `tasks/worker-prompts/balance-gate-staleness.md`. Next free decision id is **D-0103**.

### D-0103 — **The funding gate pins its L1 reads and corroborates before fail-closing; the 2026-08-27 outage mode is closed**
- **Context:** D-0102's surviving item. The 03:00 wake read false balances from an endpoint whose **head was current while its state was stale**, reported underfunded, and fail-closed the whole stack for ~6 h while the wallets held 4.03/1.74 ETH. Returned as ForteL2#163.
- **Finding 1 — the fix addresses this incident specifically, established by measurement.** The open question was whether pinning helps given the worker's disclosed "uniformly stale pool" residual. It does, because that was not this incident's mode: block **11577135**, the head the failed wake logged, has timestamp **10:00Z 2026-08-27** — exactly the wake time. The endpoint's head was current; only its `latest` state was stale. Pinning to head−3 forces that backend to answer at a current block or error.
- **Finding 2 — gate recomputed at head in an isolated clone:** 323 PASS 0 FAIL (318 + 5), parity 60 exit 0, `bash -n` clean under the real **bash 3.2.57**, CI green.
- **Finding 3 — the three outcomes probed against a stub endpoint:** node lacking the pinned block → outcome (c), distinct message quoting **no figure**; genuinely low with agreeing reads → outcome (b), original underfunded message preserved; healthy → passes. The `set -e` reasoning is correct and load-bearing (status captured in `if`, never a plain assignment — precisely what D-0102's first, wrong diagnosis missed).
- **Finding 4 — planner-authored cleanup, attributed:** the planner pushed two commits to the branch — `0fed8b3` merging main in, and `ff35280` removing `tasks/worker-prompts/balance-read-fail-loud.md`, the superseded brief whose diagnosis was disproven. That brief was planner-authored; leaving it in `worker-prompts/` would have re-seeded the wrong theory.
- **Decision:** **#163 approved.** Accepted residuals: a uniformly stale pool (head *and* state behind) still corroborates into a false underfunded — real, but demonstrably not this incident's mode; and an endpoint reporting an implausibly low head degrades the pin to block 0, which on a real chain would read zero and corroborate — low probability, unreproducible against a real endpoint, worth a floor whenever the file is next touched.
- **Consequence:** **This does not stop the outage recurring.** Outcome (c) still refuses to start, so a persistent read problem still leaves the stack down — correctly labelled rather than falsely accused, but down. The blackout itself belongs to the next task: nothing alerted for six hours, and the challenger/proxy asymmetry below is the same defect class. Next free decision id is **D-0104**.

### D-0104 — **Start/stop symmetry is enforced by a test, challenger failures degrade instead of aborting, absence alerts within 30 minutes, and CI caches Go modules**
- **Context:** D-0102/D-0103's remaining operational gaps. `op-challenger` had been down since **2026-08-24 23:45:05**, stopped by that night's dev-sleep and never restarted — three days with the fault-proof defense off, and nothing alerted. Cause: `stop-all-sepolia.sh` stops seven services; `start-all-sepolia.sh` started five. Nothing in the repo invoked `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh`. Returned as ForteL2#164.
- **Finding 1 — no loss occurred, established before the fix was written.** The respected game type is **8** and `proposer()` reverts on a live game instance, so these are **permissionless** games: anyone may create one. The planner compared all **45 games created during the down window** against the node's output root at each game's `l2BlockNumber` — **45 match, 0 mismatch**. The exposure was real; it did not materialise.
- **Finding 2 — gate recomputed at head (`2796d9f`) in an isolated clone:** 334 PASS 0 FAIL (323 + 11 appended, none deleted or skipped), parity 60 exit 0, CI green.
- **Finding 3 — the symmetry guard was proven able to fail.** Removing the challenger's script reference from `start-all-sepolia.sh` turns it red (`FAIL start/stop Sepolia service lists must match`), and restoring returns it to green. Note its shape: it matches on the *script name being referenced*, so a regression that kept the name but dropped the invocation would slip past **that** test — but the behavioural tests catch exactly that case (a separate probe removing only the call produced three failures). Combined coverage is sound; neither check alone is.
- **Finding 4 — the alert window closes this morning's six-hour gap, verified empirically.** The suppression window is silent 23:45–02:59 PT and alerting from **03:00** onward; the agent runs hourly at **:30**, so a failed 03:00 wake alerts at **03:30**. The planner drove the window function directly across ten boundary times to confirm, rather than reading it. Today the same failure was discovered by a human at 05:45.
- **Finding 5 — degrade-don't-abort holds, and fail-closed survives for core services.** The optional fault-proof block runs *after* the pre-existing `trap - ERR` — the disarm point is unchanged from main, so no new hole — with status captured via `|| optional_rc=$?` (a plain assignment would abort under `set -e`, the D-0103 lesson applied). A dedicated test asserts core starts stay inside the ERR trap while challenger/proxy starts sit outside it. On failure the summary prints `Challenger: not running (optional; core stack is up)` and yells that fault-proof defense is OFF.
- **Finding 6 — CI now caches Go modules.** `cache-dependency-path` names all three `go.sum` files; the pinned action SHA and Go version are unchanged. This was folded in because #163 — touching no Go code — failed CI twice on `proxy.golang.org` stream errors while downloading modules the runner had no cache for.
- **Decision:** **#164 approved.** Codex/Bugbot's `PID_DIR` argv defect was real and fixed in `2796d9f`. Deferred verification, both legitimate and both the planner's to close: the warm-cache hit is only observable after merge to main (a PR branch restores from a base that lacks the config), and the full stop/start cycle was deliberately not run against the operator's live stack.
- **Consequence:** **Tonight's 23:45 sleep → 03:00 wake is the live proof** — if `op-challenger` is running tomorrow morning, the fix works end-to-end; if the wake fails, an alert should arrive at 03:30 rather than nothing. Non-blocking follow-ups, unassigned: the sleep window is hardcoded (`23:45–03:00`) rather than derived from the launchd plists, so a schedule change silently desynchronises it; auto-restart after a failed wake remains deliberately out of scope pending confidence in alerting. Still queued: the op-node call-rate levers (`--l1.rpckind=standard` today; the flag's own help says provider kinds exist to reduce cost), and `resolve-games` skipping every game as `not_type_1` while the respected type is 8. Next free decision id is **D-0105**.

### D-0105 — **Measurements for the two queued tasks: op-node dominates L1 traffic on the least efficient RPC mode, and the resolve-games type filter is a latent bug with no money stranded**
- **Context:** After the 2026-08-27 incidents closed (D-0102–D-0104), two items remained queued. Both were **measured before being briefed**, so the next session dispatches from evidence rather than from a plausible story — the discipline this week's failures earned.
- **Finding 1 — the Go-module cache is confirmed working on main (D-0104's deferred item, now closed).** The first post-merge run on `175c5ad` logged `Cache saved with the key: setup-go-Linux-x64-ubuntu24-go-1.26.7-32fbdbef…` at **405.03 MiB**, present in `gh cache list`. Subsequent runs restore rather than re-downloading from `proxy.golang.org` — the failure class that broke #163 twice.
- **Finding 2 — L1 call-rate measured per component** (60 s window, two independent passes agreeing; external traffic only): **op-node ~702 KB in / 174 KB out**, op-batcher ~90 KB / 11 KB, op-proposer ~10 KB / 4.6 KB. **op-node is ~85–90 % of L1 traffic.** Configured (deterministic, read from scripts): op-node `--l1.http-poll-interval=12s`, `--l1.rpc-rate-limit=20`; batcher and proposer poll 12 s. **Derived, NOT measured:** ~7–10 req/s from op-node (≈600–900 k/day) — the conversion assumes a request size and the call mix is unobservable without provider-side data; treat as order-of-magnitude only. **What this does and does not establish (Codex review on #165, accepted):** bytes measure traffic share, not QuickNode credit spend — response sizes vary by method, and providers bill per call, so the credit ranking is inferred, not proven; the poll intervals are script defaults and env-overridable, so the shakeout must confirm the effective live values. The call-rate task's shakeout captures provider-side request/credit counts (QuickNode dashboard) before/after as its primary metric.
- **Finding 3 — the cheapest lever is a one-line flag.** `scripts/04-start-sequencer-sepolia.sh` hardcodes `--l1.rpckind=standard`. The binary's own help states the flag is "used to inform optimal transactions receipts fetching, **and thus reduce costs**. Valid options: alchemy, quicknode, infura, parity, nethermind, debug_geth, erigon, basic, any, standard". `standard` fetches receipts the expensive way; the stack is back on QuickNode (D-0102), so `quicknode` is the matching kind. **The saving has not been measured** — expected largest single win because receipts dominate, but that is a prediction. **Risk to respect:** changing the kind changes which methods op-node calls; a provider that does not serve the optimised method breaks derivation. Needs a live shakeout with a named rollback, not a fire-and-forget edit.
- **Finding 4 — the resolve-games type filter is a real bug with zero measured financial impact.** `scripts/resolve-games-sepolia.sh` `decide_game()` hardcodes `if game_type != 1: skip("not_type_1")`, while the same script's snapshot already reads `respected_game_type` (currently **8**) and ignores it for filtering — so every current game is skipped and the hourly agent recovers nothing. **But nothing is stranded:** seven games sampled across indexes 60–115, including two *resolved* type-8 games, all return `credit(proposer) = 0`, and the proposer balance (4.03 ETH) is not draining. The fix should resolve the respected type on-chain rather than hardcoding — the policy D-0063 Finding 3c / D-0083 already enforce elsewhere — but it is **correctness and future-proofing, not a leak**. Priority accordingly below Finding 3.
- **Decision:** Queue order is **(1) the op-node call-rate levers, (2) the resolve-games type filter** — likely-money before latent correctness. The ranking rests on measured byte share plus the flag's own stated cost purpose, not on proven credit share; if the shakeout's provider-side counts contradict it, the order is wrong, not the evidence. Neither is dispatched yet.
- **Consequence:** **Tonight's 23:45 sleep → 03:00 wake is the outstanding live proof of D-0104.** Expected: `op-challenger` running tomorrow morning with no intervention. If the wake fails instead, an alert should arrive at **03:30** (window verified empirically: silent 23:45–02:59 PT, alerting from 03:00; agent runs hourly at :30). Silence *and* a down stack is the only outcome meaning the fix did not take. Next free decision id is **D-0106**.

### D-0106 — **The 2026-08-27 03:00 wake aborted on stale Alchemy balance reads — funds were on-chain all along; the 03:30 alert delivered; D-0104's live proof moves to tonight**
- **Context:** The 23:45 sleep ran clean. The 03:00 wake aborted at the funding gate: it read BATCHER **0.145** ETH and PROPOSER **0.0087** ETH (< 0.15 floor) and stopped the partial stack (`~/Library/Logs/fortel2-wake.err.log`). `.env.sepolia` (untracked) still pointed L1 at **Alchemy** at 03:00 — the D-0102 stopgap was still live in the env file despite the repo-side revert.
- **Finding — the reads were stale provider state, not missing funds.** At the wake's own logged block **11577135**, two independent providers agree: PROPOSER **4.0348 ETH**, BATCHER **1.7400 ETH** (QuickNode and PublicNode, `cast balance --block 11577135`, run 12:2x PT same day; both balances slightly above current, consistent with normal gas spend since). The #163 defence — pinned block + corroborating re-read — passed anyway because both reads hit the *same* provider: same-provider corroboration cannot catch provider-level staleness. The endpoint was degraded generally: op-proposer logged `429 Too Many Requests` from Alchemy during the ~06:15 restart attempt.
- **Finding — the alert path delivered as designed.** Email "ForteL2 resolve-games agent failing" at **03:30:04 PT** (Resend, condition `resolve-games-nonzero`). Indirect — `alert-watch.sh` has no stack-down condition; the stalled resolve-games agent is the proxy signal — but exactly inside D-0104's verified window. Worth knowing when reading future alerts: that resolve-games subject line is currently the *only* overnight symptom of a dead stack.
- **Operator recovery:** restart attempt on Alchemy ~06:15 hit the 429 storm; `.env.sepolia` switched to QuickNode at 06:21; stack stable from 09:42 (all six services RUNNING). Down window ≈ 6.7 h (03:00 → 09:42).
- **Decision:** Tonight's 23:45 → 03:00 cycle is **re-armed as D-0104's live proof**, now on QuickNode with balances verified correct at a pinned block. A third task joins the queue behind D-0105's two: the funding gate should corroborate a *below-floor* read against a second independent RPC before aborting a start — same-provider staleness is now a demonstrated failure mode that turned a healthy, funded stack into a 6.7 h outage.
- **Consequence:** Next free decision id is **D-0107**.

### D-0107 — **rpckind is credit-neutral; eth_call is ~86% of QuickNode credits; challenger init dies fatally on 429; first unattended wake half-passed with a direct alert on the missing service**
- **Context:** Outcomes of 2026-08-27 afternoon and the 2026-08-28 overnight cycle: D-0104's proof, #166's shakeout, the corrected cost model, the explorer outage, and a new challenger defect. All numbers below are measured (QuickNode dashboard per-hour tooltips; logs quoted from disk).
- **Finding 1 — D-0104 is closed.** Two manual sleep→wake cycles on 2026-08-27 (13:09 and 13:25, triggered from LaunchControl — the jobs never sleep the Mac, so a manual back-to-back run is a faithful replay) brought up the full stack including op-challenger and l1-batch-proxy, funding gate clean on QuickNode.
- **Finding 2 — #166 shakeout verdict: credit-neutral; keep it.** `kind=quicknode` live from 13:25; 15-minute watch clean (0 op-node errors, safe/unsafe advancing). Method swap confirmed 1:1: `eth_getBlockReceipts` 290 calls/5.8K credits per hour (12:00, standard) → `debug_getRawReceipts` 292 calls/5.8K credits (18:00, quicknode) — same ~20 credits/call. No saving, no regression, no rollback. The receipts path was ~1.6% of spend, so this was the expected ceiling (Codex's bytes-vs-credits objection on #165 was correct in practice).
- **Finding 3 — the real spend is eth_call.** Dashboard, RED-DIMENSIONAL-DREAM: 12:00 on standard = 20K calls/hr with eth_call 17K (85%); 18:00 on quicknode = 24K calls/hr with eth_call 21K (88%); credits 391K/hr → 475K/hr with eth_call 331K → 411K (86%). $2.04 overage already this billing period. Attribution — *amended 2026-08-28, test run*: **tested and confirmed** (06:01–06:11 PT): stopping op-challenger for 10 minutes dropped the l1-batch-proxy — whose only consumer on this host is the challenger (D-0081) — from 48 batched POSTs/min to **0**, and the QuickNode minute-view fell from ~400 calls/min to a ~50/min floor, recovering on restart. The challenger is ~85–90% of call volume; the 06:01 spike inside the window is the hourly resolve-games agent.
- **Finding 4 — new defect: challenger init treats 429 as fatal.** Overnight 03:00 wake started five of six services plus the challenger, which exited one second later: `lvl=crit … failed to register cannon-kona game type: … 429 Too Many Requests` (03:00:22). The runtime scan loop retries 429s; the init path does not. Nothing restarts it. Defense was off 03:00–05:42 (planner restarted via `09-start-challenger-sepolia.sh`). **Correction to D-0106:** alert-watch *does* have a direct `stack-service-missing` condition (added in #164; the PID_DIR argv fix was in exactly this check) and it fired at 03:30:04 — "ForteL2 stack service missing … not running: op-challenger". D-0106's "no stack-down condition" claim came from reading only the file's header comment; the header is stale, the code is not.
- **Finding 5 — explorer outage post-mortem (2026-08-27, unrelated to RPC providers).** The public RPC chain (explorer → fortel2-sequencer-rpc.onrender.com → Cloudflare tunnel → Mac :9555) returned 530/1033 because the `com.cloudflare.cloudflared` LaunchDaemon had crash-looped since the Aug 26 10:19 reboot: its token file (`/Library/Application Support/com.cloudflare.cloudflared/token`) was missing (directory emptied Aug 12; the pre-reboot process predated the deletion). Token restored from shell history, daemon kickstarted, verified end-to-end: `eth_chainId` serves, `admin_`/`debug_` refused — the write filter is intact. `check-launchd.sh` does not cover this daemon.
- **Decision:** Queue re-ranked on credit evidence: **(1) challenger 429-resilience + scan tuning** (the money and the defense gap — brief dispatched separately), **(2) funding-gate cross-provider corroboration** (D-0106), **(3) resolve-games type filter** (D-0105 Finding 4), **(4) check-launchd coverage for the cloudflared daemon**. `SEPOLIA_L1_RPC_KIND=quicknode` stays as merged.
- **Consequence:** D-0108 is reserved for the challenger-task verdict. Next free decision id is **D-0109**.

### D-0108 — **#168 live verdict: clean unattended wake, retry armed but not needed, challenger L1 traffic down ~96%; operator env overrides predate the runbook and include a shrunken game-window**
- **Context:** First unattended 23:45 → 03:00 cycle with #168 merged (2026-08-28 → 29). Verdict recorded per the reservation in D-0107.
- **Finding 1 — the wake passed end-to-end with no intervention.** All six services up; `op-challenger start attempt 1/3 (grace 15s)` → `survived 15s post-start grace` (03:00:24). The retry machinery ran and was not needed beyond attempt 1 — consistent with the gentler scan profile reducing the very burst that used to kill init. Wake err log untouched; no alert.
- **Finding 2 — challenger L1 traffic fell ~96%.** l1-batch-proxy (challenger-only, D-0081): **48 batched POSTs/min before #168 → 2/min after** (120 s count, 2026-08-29 ~09:20). Challenger 429 errors: 29 so far today vs 12–21/hour before. Provider-side credit delta — *appended 2026-08-29 via the QuickNode MCP*: steady-hours comparison shows **eth_call 410.8K → 64.9K credits/hr (−84%)** and **total ~490K → ~143K credits/hr (−71%)** (Aug 28 16:00–22:00 PT vs Aug 29 04:00–09:00 PT, method-level `get-rpc-usage`; matches the browser-read 411K baseline). ≈9.8M → ≈2.9M credits/day at current duty cycle, before the operator's 2026-08-29 env cleanup (removed the three `OP_CHALLENGER_*` overrides, incl. `game-window=12h`) takes effect at the next cycle.
- **Finding 3 — the effective knobs are not the merged defaults.** The wake log reports `http-poll=30s min-update=300s max-concurrency=2 game-window=12h`: `.env.sepolia` (mtime 2026-08-27 06:21, predating #168 and its runbook) contains `OP_CHALLENGER_MAX_CONCURRENCY=2`, `OP_CHALLENGER_HTTP_POLL_INTERVAL=30s`, `OP_CHALLENGER_GAME_WINDOW=12h` — binary-native env vars the challenger honoured directly even before #168. Two consequences: the measured reduction was achieved mostly by `min-update 0s→300s` (the one knob not overridden), and **`game-window=12h` contradicts the #168 runbook** ("do not shrink; bond-claim buffer", binary default 672h). Risk today is low (all sampled games have `credit(proposer)=0`, D-0105), but the override should be removed or consciously re-affirmed.
- **Decision:** *(settled 2026-08-29)* Operator deleted the three `OP_CHALLENGER_*` lines from `.env.sepolia` (verified: zero matching lines remain); the script defaults (300s/300s/1, window at binary 672h) govern from the next restart — one source of truth, runbook-consistent. Queue unchanged otherwise: funding-gate cross-provider corroboration next, then resolve-games, then check-launchd/cloudflared coverage.
- **Consequence:** Next free decision id is **D-0109**.

### D-0109 — **op-reth Task 1 merged: pin confirmed at the P:0 floor; FORTEL2_EL selector and op-reth pid name settled; Task 2 dispatch plan**
- **Context:** op-reth migration PRD Task 1 (`feat/op-reth-task1-pin`, #176, merged 2026-08-29 as `1f0ba38`). Deliverables: pin recorded in `.env.example` / `.env.sepolia.example` / README, new `scripts/check-el-pins.sh`, +7 stub tests in `scripts/test-helpers.sh`.
- **Finding 1 — pin confirmed, no bump.** op-node **v1.19.2** (`da197e45`) + tag **op-reth/v2.3.3**, which reports `Reth Version: 2.3.0-dev` commit `9384bc53d8c0c77e59cac83fdaaf3b372c6d2216` (tag string ≠ reported version — the check asserts reported string + commit, refuses bare `2.3` and `2.3.3` greps). Worker research: Upgrade 19 pairs op-node v1.19.1+ with op-reth v2.3.3; Jovian minimums are far older (op-node v1.16.2 / op-reth v1.9.2); rollup has `jovian_time: 0`, no `karst_time`, so no getPayloadV5 requirement. Later tags exist (op-node v1.19.5, op-reth v2.4.2); adopting any bump requires a Mini sidecar hash-match re-run before Task 3 (PRD Task 1 rule).
- **Finding 2 — review evidence (planner, scratch clone of `0e38b81`, deleted after).** `test-helpers.sh` 373 PASS / 0 FAIL (main baseline 366; +7 = exactly the new stub tests); `check-el-pins.sh` exit 0 against the real Mini arm64 binaries; six adversarial probes all exit 1 (tag-string `2.3.3` stub, right-version/wrong-commit, `v1.19.20` prefix, op-node wrong commit, `FORTEL2_EL=bogus`, geth binary under `FORTEL2_EL=reth`). Scope exact (5 permitted files, `tasks/` and `lib.sh` untouched, test-helpers additive 106+/0−). Codex `file -L` fix verified by diff read; not independently verified: the release-notes reading (nothing rides on it — the pin is the already-proven floor) and the cold-start rebuild block (cross-checked against `tasks/spike-op-reth.md`, not rebuilt).
- **Decision (settled 2026-08-29, operator-approved):** (1) EL selector env var is **`FORTEL2_EL`** = `geth` | `reth`, default `geth` until Task 5 — Task 2 implements it, `check-el-pins.sh` already consumes it. (2) reth pid/process/log name is explicit **`op-reth`** (log `data/logs/op-reth.log`), not a neutral `l2-el` — the migration window runs both clients concurrently, and §10 of the PRD is the closed rename set. (3) Dispatch plan: Task 2 (strongest tier, solo) → Task 3 (strongest, Codex review per the metered-credits policy) → Task 4; Task 5 operator-owned, not scheduled.
- **Consequence:** Task 2 is unblocked. Next free decision id is **D-0110**.

### D-0110 — **op-reth Task 2 approved: opt-in reth config behind FORTEL2_EL; live start paths refuse reth; Q6 rewind = wipe + re-derive**
- **Context:** op-reth PRD Task 2 (`feat/op-reth-task2-config`, #178, approved 2026-08-29). Adds selector-gated init/start/stop/status/reset for op-reth without changing the geth default: additive `scripts/lib.sh` helpers (start_bg/stop_bg bodies untouched), `scripts/start-op-reth-verifier.sh` / `stop-op-reth-verifier.sh`, 852-only init path in `03-init-l2.sh`, +26 helper tests.

- **Decision 1 — live start scripts refuse `FORTEL2_EL=reth` rather than honoring it** (worker-proposed, planner-accepted): `04-start-sequencer*.sh` exit 1 and point at the sidecar script, so no wake/start-all path can put reth on :9545 before Task 5. Task 5 will convert the refusal into the real cutover path.
- **Decision 2 — PRD §11 Q6 standing answer:** mid-chain rewind on a keeper = wipe the reth datadir and re-derive from 852 genesis; `debug_setHead` never. No non-destructive path found; Task 3 may revisit only with evidence.
- **Review evidence (planner, scratch clone, deleted):** 399/0 helper tests (main 373); pins exit 0; nine adversarial refusal probes all correct (traversal, dot-path, symlink, out-of-allowlist datadir, two live ports, missing profile, `.env.sepolia` refuse, live-path reth refuse) with the default geth path inert; `--preflight` clean with zero processes; both profile flags confirmed present in the pinned binary's `--help` (sequencer_faultproof is arg-valid; sufficiency stays Task 4). Five bot findings (2 cursor, 3 Codex P2) fixed on the reviewed tip, verified by probe. Not re-run: the worker's Mini sidecar derive (genesis match, head 21) — corroborated by clean datadir/live-stack state; Task 3 re-exercises it end-to-end.
- **Consequence:** Task 3 (Sepolia verifier to safe head, strongest tier, Codex review per the metered policy) is unblocked. Next free decision id is **D-0111**.
### D-0111 — **First cycle on the full fix stack: clean unattended wake, gate inert as designed, credits steady at ~30% of the incident-week rate; residual per-cycle 429 burst noted, not queued**
- **Context:** 2026-08-29 → 30 overnight cycle, the first with #175 (funding-gate second opinion) merged and the operator's env cleanup live. Live verdict for #175 per the reservation made in D-0108 (as “D-0109”, an id the parallel op-reth stream consumed in the interim — hence this entry is D-0111).
- **Finding 1 — wake clean, defaults live.** All six services up unattended; challenger survived start attempt 1/3; effective knobs now `http-poll=300s min-update=300s max-concurrency=1 game-window=binary-default-672h` — the runbook state, no overrides. Wake err log untouched (last write 2026-08-27); zero alert emails in 24 h.
- **Finding 2 — the hardened gate passed silently.** Healthy primary reads → zero secondary calls, no disagreement WARN: exactly the designed inert path. The disagreement branch remains unproven against a real stale provider and will stay so until one occurs; its behavior is pinned by the #175 shim tests (366 PASS, go-red verified at review).
- **Finding 3 — credit steady state, and why the env cleanup moved nothing further.** Aug 30 04:00–08:00 PT: eth_call **66.7K credits/hr**, total **~149K/hr** — flat vs Aug 29's 64.9K / 143K. Explanation: `min-update-interval=300s` (live since the #168 wake) is the binding throttle on scan cycles; the later poll 30s→300s and concurrency 2→1 changes reshape the burst but not the per-cycle work. Net position vs the incident week: **~490K → ~149K credits/hr (−70%)**, ≈3M credits/day steady.
- **Finding 4 — residual, observed and deliberately not queued:** each 300 s scan cycle still bursts past QuickNode's 50 req/s once (56 non-fatal, self-retried "Failed to progress games" 429s on Aug 30 by 09:30; op-node logged 13 transient engine backoffs of the same class; safe head trails unsafe ~5 min, normal). Functional and cost-stable. If it ever escalates, the known levers are a client batch-size cap or the l1-batch-proxy smoothing argued (not implemented) in #168 — either needs its own brief.
- **Decision:** #175 verdict: **holds in production; no follow-up required.** The 2026-08-26/27 incident arc (D-0102–D-0109) is closed. Queue: resolve-games type filter (D-0105 Finding 4) next, then check-launchd coverage for the cloudflared daemon (D-0107).
- **Consequence:** Next free decision id is **D-0112**.

### D-0112 — **resolve-games live verdict: the respected-type filter uncovered 113 pending legs, the cap held, one inherited assertion caused a night of false alerts, and the fixed agent now drains the backlog cleanly**
- **Context:** Live verdicts for #182 (respected-type filter + tx cap) and #183 (zero-bond credit confirmation), per the D-0112 reservation.
- **Finding 1 — the filter and cap worked exactly as reviewed.** First live run (2026-08-30 21:01): `respected_game_type=8`, 113 ready tx legs uncovered in the backlog the hardcoded type-1 filter had hidden, selection capped at 5 legs including a mid-game split — the designed money guard.
- **Finding 2 — one inherited assertion turned success into a paging failure.** `confirm_leg_advanced` treated credit==0 after resolveClaim as unconditionally fatal (bonded type-1 R-13 assumption). These type-8 games carry zero-bond claims (on-chain proof: game 73 claimDataLen=1, bond=0, resolved to status 2 by the agent's own legs), so every overnight run sent ~2 legs, exited 1, and paged the operator — progress at 2 legs/hour instead of 5, alerts every cooldown cycle. #183 conditioned the fatal on plan-time `expected_credit_wei != 0` (missing/unparseable stays fatal; bonded protection proven go-red-able at review).
- **Finding 3 — fixed agent verified live.** First post-#183 run (2026-08-31 09:01): exit 0, `txs_sent=5`, three `OK resolveClaim confirmed with credit 0 (expected 0; zero-bond)` confirmations, err log untouched. `remaining_ready=105` → backlog drains in ~21 hours at cap rate; the resolve-games-nonzero alerts end with the exit-1s. Total gas spent on the false-alert night: ~0.0009 ETH, all on wanted transactions.
- **Decision:** #182 + #183 verdict: **hold in production; no follow-up.** The once-observed JSON-decode RPC failure (21:01 first run) did not recur; reopen only if it does.
- **Consequence:** Next free decision id is **D-0114**.

### D-0113 — **cloudflared-watch live verdict: condition present, silent on a healthy daemon, and the not-running hole is closed**
- **Context:** Live verdict for #181, per the D-0113 reservation.
- **Finding (corrected per Codex review on #185 — the first draft cited a 20:30 run as "since merge" when #181 merged at 21:14 PT):** verified 2026-08-31 morning against the live system: the checkout the hourly agents actually execute contains the `cloudflared-failing` condition, the latest alert-watch runs log `active conditions: 0` / `no alert` with the real daemon healthy, and `check-launchd.sh` reports `PASS com.cloudflare.cloudflared system domain state=running`. The exact first post-merge run is not reconstructible, because of the next finding. The review round had already proved both directions in shims against the real daemon paths: any `not running` state fires (including clean exit 0, which KeepAlive would never restart — the silent-outage hole the first draft had), unparseable output fails toward alerting, a plist-less host stays quiet.
- **Finding — surfaced by the correction, worth its own task:** the launchd agents (alerts, resolve-games, health, and the 23:45/03:00 sleep/wake) execute scripts from the mutable shared dev checkout at `~/ForteL2`, which parallel workers check out at will — at verification time it sat on a worker branch (`feat/op-reth-task3-verifier`) that happened to include current main. A worker parking the tree on a stale or broken branch silently changes production agent behavior. Queued: pin the agents to a deployment copy or add a branch guard to the agent entrypoints.
- **Decision:** #181 verdict: **holds in production; no follow-up.** The incident week's entire queue (D-0105/D-0107/D-0108 items) is now closed: rpckind, challenger resilience + scan cost, funding-gate second opinion, resolve-games filter + cap + zero-bond, cloudflared watch.
- **Consequence:** Next free decision id is **D-0114**.
